/// Runs the start-up code of the app before its first frame: first what
/// must come before anything else, then the platform services, then the
/// services of the app, then what needs them.
Future<void> bootstrap() async {
  debugPrint('early');
  debugPrint('platform');
  await initAnalytics();
  await initAuth();
  await initCrashReporting();
  installCrashReporting();
  await initEvents();
  await initPreferences();
  await initNotifications();
  await initPurchases();
  await initPush();
  debugPrint('di');
  await registerDependencies();
  debugPrint(createBadge().labels.join());
  setUpFixtureParent();
  debugPrint('late');
}
=== lib/core/analytics/analytics_service.dart (role:analytics) ===
import 'package:fixture_app/core/fixture_analytics/fixture_analytics.dart'
    as impl0;
import 'package:flutter/foundation.dart';

/// Records what users do in the app.
abstract interface class AnalyticsService {
  /// Logs the event [name] with [parameters].
  Future<void> logEvent(String name, {Map<String, Object>? parameters});

  /// Logs that the user signed in, with the sign-in [method].
  Future<void> logSignIn({String? method, Map<String, Object>? parameters});

  /// Logs that the user signed up, with the sign-up [method].
  Future<void> logSignUp({
    required String method,
    Map<String, Object>? parameters,
  });

  /// Turns the collection of analytics data on or off.
  Future<void> setAnalyticsCollectionEnabled(bool enabled);

  /// Sets the id of the signed-in user, or clears it with `null`.
  Future<void> setUserId(String? userId);

  /// Sets the user property [name] to [value], or clears it with `null`.
  Future<void> setUserProperty({required String name, required String? value});
}

/// Returns the analytics service of the app, which forwards every call to
/// the analytics services of all modules.
///
/// It calls each of them on its own, and its future completes once all of
/// them are done. An analytics service that throws, or whose future fails,
/// keeps no other from the call, and its failure does not reach the code
/// that called: that code may not await the call, and then nothing would
/// catch the failure. In debug mode the failure is printed, so that an
/// analytics service that does not work, such as one that is not set up,
/// shows in the console. Each analytics service gets a copy of its own of
/// the map of parameters, so that one that changes the map changes nothing
/// that the caller or another analytics service has. An analytics service
/// whose factory throws, or that fails to start, is left out, and in debug
/// mode its error is printed.
AnalyticsService createAnalyticsService() => _analyticsService;

final AnalyticsService _analyticsService = _AnalyticsServices(
  _analyticsServices,
);

final List<AnalyticsService> _analyticsServices = [];

/// Creates the implementations of AnalyticsService that start
/// asynchronously; `bootstrap()` awaits it.
Future<void> initAnalytics() async {
  final started = await Future.wait([
    _startAlone('initFixtureAnalytics', impl0.initFixtureAnalytics),
  ]);
  _analyticsServices.addAll(started.nonNulls);
}

/// Calls [start], the function [name], on its own: returns what it starts,
/// or `null` if it throws or its future fails, so that the app starts and
/// works without it, and in debug mode prints the error.
Future<AnalyticsService?> _startAlone(
  String name,
  Future<AnalyticsService> Function() start,
) async {
  try {
    return await start();
  } on Object catch (error) {
    if (kDebugMode) {
      debugPrint('$name() failed, so the app works without it: $error');
    }
    return null;
  }
}

final class _AnalyticsServices implements AnalyticsService {
  const _AnalyticsServices(this._services);

  final List<AnalyticsService> _services;

  @override
  Future<void> logEvent(String name, {Map<String, Object>? parameters}) =>
      _forAll(
        (service) => service.logEvent(name, parameters: _copyOf(parameters)),
      );

  @override
  Future<void> logSignIn({String? method, Map<String, Object>? parameters}) =>
      _forAll(
        (service) =>
            service.logSignIn(method: method, parameters: _copyOf(parameters)),
      );

  @override
  Future<void> logSignUp({
    required String method,
    Map<String, Object>? parameters,
  }) => _forAll(
    (service) =>
        service.logSignUp(method: method, parameters: _copyOf(parameters)),
  );

  @override
  Future<void> setAnalyticsCollectionEnabled(bool enabled) =>
      _forAll((service) => service.setAnalyticsCollectionEnabled(enabled));

  @override
  Future<void> setUserId(String? userId) =>
      _forAll((service) => service.setUserId(userId));

  @override
  Future<void> setUserProperty({
    required String name,
    required String? value,
  }) => _forAll((service) => service.setUserProperty(name: name, value: value));

  /// Calls [call] with each analytics service on its own, and completes
  /// once all of them are done, whatever each does; see
  /// [createAnalyticsService].
  Future<void> _forAll(
    Future<void> Function(AnalyticsService service) call,
  ) async {
    await Future.wait([
      for (final service in _services) _callAlone(service, call),
    ]);
  }

  /// A copy of [parameters] for one analytics service; see
  /// [createAnalyticsService].
  static Map<String, Object>? _copyOf(Map<String, Object>? parameters) =>
      parameters == null ? null : Map.of(parameters);

  /// Calls [call] with [service], and keeps what it throws, or the error of
  /// its future, from the caller: in debug mode it prints it.
  static Future<void> _callAlone(
    AnalyticsService service,
    Future<void> Function(AnalyticsService service) call,
  ) async {
    try {
      await call(service);
    } on Object catch (error) {
      if (kDebugMode) {
        debugPrint(
          'The analytics service ${service.runtimeType} failed: $error',
        );
      }
    }
  }
}
=== lib/core/app/status_bar_icons.dart (role:app_entry) ===
import 'package:flutter/material.dart';
import 'package:flutter/services.dart';

/// Asks the system for the icons of the status bar that suit the theme,
/// around [child]: dark icons on a light theme, light ones on a dark theme.
///
/// The root of the app puts it around the content of every route, below
/// the `MaterialApp` whose theme it reads. It leaves the navigation bar of
/// the system as the platform has it. A screen with an `AppBar` gets the
/// icons that suit its bar, since the bar tells them for the top of the
/// screen itself.
class StatusBarIcons extends StatelessWidget {
  /// Creates the widget around [child].
  const StatusBarIcons({required this.child, super.key});

  /// What the icons suit: the content of a route.
  final Widget child;

  @override
  Widget build(BuildContext context) {
    final brightness = Theme.of(context).brightness;
    return AnnotatedRegion<SystemUiOverlayStyle>(
      // iOS takes the brightness of the bar, and Android that of its icons.
      value: SystemUiOverlayStyle(
        statusBarBrightness: brightness,
        statusBarIconBrightness: brightness == Brightness.dark
            ? Brightness.light
            : Brightness.dark,
      ),
      child: child,
    );
  }
}
=== lib/core/auth/app_session.dart (role:auth) ===
import 'dart:async';

import 'package:fixture_app/core/fixture_auth/fixture_auth.dart' as impl0;
import 'package:flutter/foundation.dart';
import 'package:flutter/widgets.dart';

import 'auth_service.dart';
import 'guest_data.dart';
import 'sign_in_methods.dart';

export 'auth_service.dart'
    show AuthFailure, AuthFailureReason, IdTokenCredential, SignInCredential;

/// Who may use the app without an account.
enum AuthMode {
  /// Nobody: the user signs in first.
  required,

  /// Everyone. The app has no user until someone signs in, and asks for an
  /// account only where a screen needs one.
  guest,

  /// Everyone, as an anonymous user that the app signs in itself when it
  /// starts, so that code has the id of a user from the first frame. An
  /// account is asked for only where a screen needs one, and signing up
  /// gives it to that same user.
  anonymous,
}

/// The mode of this app, which was chosen when the app was generated.
///
/// The mode gates nothing on its own. It decides what [appSession] does when
/// it starts and what [AppSessionController.allowsApp] says, which a guard
/// of the routes reads.
///
/// To change the mode, change its value and keep the declaration as it is,
/// on a line that it starts: a tool of the app that cannot import this
/// file, which imports Flutter, reads the mode from this line.
const AuthMode authMode = AuthMode.required;

/// Who uses the app: nobody that the app knows, an anonymous user, or the
/// user of an account.
@immutable
sealed class AppSession {
  const AppSession();

  /// The id of the user, or `null` when nobody is signed in.
  String? get uid;
}

/// Nobody is signed in.
final class SignedOutSession extends AppSession {
  /// Creates the session of an app without a user.
  const SignedOutSession();

  @override
  String? get uid => null;

  @override
  bool operator ==(Object other) => other is SignedOutSession;

  @override
  int get hashCode => (SignedOutSession).hashCode;
}

/// An anonymous user is signed in: one without an account, whom the app
/// signed in itself.
final class AnonymousSession extends AppSession {
  /// Creates the session of the anonymous user [uid].
  const AnonymousSession(this.uid);

  @override
  final String uid;

  @override
  bool operator ==(Object other) =>
      other is AnonymousSession && other.uid == uid;

  @override
  int get hashCode => uid.hashCode;
}

/// The user of an account is signed in.
final class AccountSession extends AppSession {
  /// Creates the session of the account [uid].
  const AccountSession(this.uid, {this.email, this.issuers = const {}});

  @override
  final String uid;

  /// The email address of the account, or `null` if it has none, as an
  /// account of a way to sign in may not.
  final String? email;

  /// The identity providers that the account signs in with, each by the
  /// `issuer` of its `SignInMethod` in `appSignInMethods` of
  /// `sign_in_methods.dart`. It is empty for an account that signs in with
  /// a password alone.
  final Set<String> issuers;

  @override
  bool operator ==(Object other) =>
      other is AccountSession &&
      other.uid == uid &&
      other.email == email &&
      other.issuers.length == issuers.length &&
      other.issuers.containsAll(issuers);

  @override
  int get hashCode => Object.hash(uid, email, Object.hashAllUnordered(issuers));
}

/// The session of the app: who uses it, known before the first frame, and
/// the way to sign in, up and out.
///
/// Its `value` is the [AppSession], and it tells its listeners when that
/// changes. A widget follows it with a `ValueListenableBuilder`.
final appSession = AppSessionController(
  mode: authMode,
  takeGuestData: takeGuestData,
);

/// Keeps the session of the app up to date with the service of the provider
/// of sign-in, and signs in, up and out through that service. The app has
/// one, [appSession].
///
/// Every call here fails only with an [AuthFailure], whose reason the app
/// shows in a text of its own. An error of another kind becomes a failure
/// with the reason [AuthFailureReason.unknown] and the error as its
/// [AuthFailure.developerHint].
///
/// The calls run one after another, in the order they were made, and none
/// runs next to an anonymous sign-in that is on its way. So the user of the
/// app is the result of the call that was made last. In the mode
/// [AuthMode.anonymous], a call that leaves nobody signed in is followed by
/// an anonymous sign-in.
final class AppSessionController extends ChangeNotifier
    implements ValueListenable<AppSession> {
  /// Creates a controller for an app in [mode]. The app creates
  /// [appSession] and no other. A test creates one to see another mode, or
  /// a shorter [anonymousWait], and disposes of it.
  AppSessionController({
    required this.mode,
    required this.takeGuestData,
    this.anonymousWait = const Duration(seconds: 3),
  });

  /// Who may use the app without an account.
  final AuthMode mode;

  /// Takes what an anonymous user has before that user signs in to an
  /// account: `takeGuestData` of `guest_data.dart`.
  final Future<GiveGuestData?> Function(String guestUid) takeGuestData;

  /// How long the app waits for its anonymous user when it starts, and
  /// after a sign-out or the deletion of an account, in the mode
  /// [AuthMode.anonymous].
  final Duration anonymousWait;

  AuthService? _service;
  StreamSubscription<AuthUser?>? _userChanges;
  AppSession _session = const SignedOutSession();
  bool _disposed = false;

  /// Whether the app has started for the first time.
  bool _started = false;

  /// Completes at the first start. Only the first call made before it waits
  /// for it, and each later call waits for the call before it. That call
  /// creates it, so the start completes it in the zone of the call: created
  /// with the controller, it would complete in a microtask of the zone that
  /// read [appSession] first, which may run none any more, as the fake time
  /// of a widget test that is over before a later test starts the app in
  /// real time.
  Completer<void>? _untilStarted;

  /// The call that was made last, while a call is on its way: the next one
  /// waits for it. It is `null` when none is, and a call then awaits
  /// nothing. A future that completed in another zone would hold the call
  /// back in a widget test, where the app starts in real time and the test
  /// goes on in fake time.
  Future<void>? _lastCall;

  /// The anonymous sign-ins that are on their way, each by its service, so
  /// that the app starts no second one next to it.
  final Map<AuthService, Future<void>> _anonymousSignIns = Map.identity();

  /// What the reads of the token wait for that were made before the first
  /// start. Each read has one of its own, which completes in the zone of that
  /// read, so a read in a zone that runs nothing any more holds nothing up.
  final List<Completer<void>> _tokensUntilStarted = [];

  /// The refreshes of the token that are on their way, each by the id of its
  /// user, so that the next user does not get the token of the user before.
  final Map<String?, Future<String?>> _tokenRefreshes = {};

  /// Tells when the user comes back to the app, in the mode
  /// [AuthMode.anonymous].
  _ResumeObserver? _observer;

  /// Who uses the app.
  @override
  AppSession get value => _session;

  /// Whether the user may see the app: always in the modes [AuthMode.guest]
  /// and [AuthMode.anonymous], and with an account in the mode
  /// [AuthMode.required].
  ///
  /// It is computed from the session each time it is read, and its
  /// listeners are those of the session. So when the session changes, a
  /// listener finds this value and [hasAccount] both up to date, whichever
  /// of the two it listens to. A listener is also called for a change of
  /// the session that leaves this value as it was.
  late final ValueListenable<bool> allowsApp = _SessionFlag(
    this,
    (session) => mode != AuthMode.required || session is AccountSession,
  );

  /// Whether the user of an account is signed in, which an anonymous user
  /// is not. It follows the session as [allowsApp] does.
  late final ValueListenable<bool> hasAccount = _SessionFlag(
    this,
    (session) => session is AccountSession,
  );

  /// Starts the session with [service]: takes the user who is signed in on
  /// this device, and follows the changes that the service tells of.
  ///
  /// `initAuth()` calls it in `bootstrap()`, so the session is known before
  /// the first frame. In the mode [AuthMode.anonymous], an app without a
  /// user signs in anonymously here and waits for that for at most
  /// [anonymousWait]. When the sign-in fails or takes longer, the app
  /// starts without a user. A sign-in that takes longer still becomes the
  /// session when it comes, and the app tries again each time the user
  /// comes back to it.
  ///
  /// A call made before the first start waits for it: [signIn], [signUp],
  /// [signInWith], [sendPasswordReset], [signOut] and [deleteAccount]. Such
  /// calls run in
  /// their order once the start has the user, and the future of this start
  /// completes after them, but waits for no call that is made while it
  /// runs. The mocks of the tests of an app use this: a call of [signUp]
  /// made before `bootstrap()`, which nothing awaits, gives the app an
  /// account before its first frame. A test makes such a call outside the
  /// body of a test, as in a `setUpAll`: made in the body of a widget
  /// test, it waits in the fake time of that body, and a start that runs in
  /// real time never completes.
  ///
  /// It may be called again, with the service that the session has from
  /// then on: `initAuth()` does so in a test of the next launch of the app,
  /// and a test gives it a service of its own. Such a start takes its turn
  /// after the calls that are on their way.
  Future<void> start(AuthService service) async {
    // The calls that were made before this start. The first start lets
    // them run once it has the user, and a later one waits for them.
    final earlier = _lastCall;
    final first = !_started;
    if (!first) await earlier;
    unawaited(_userChanges?.cancel());
    _service = service;
    _userChanges = service.userChanges.listen(
      (_) => _sync(),
      onError: (Object error, StackTrace stackTrace) => _report(
        error,
        stackTrace,
        'while following the user of the provider of sign-in',
      ),
    );
    _sync();
    if (mode == AuthMode.anonymous) {
      if (_observer == null) {
        final observer = _observer = _ResumeObserver(_retry);
        WidgetsBinding.instance.addObserver(observer);
      }
      await _signInAnonymously();
    }
    if (!_started) {
      _started = true;
      _untilStarted?.complete();
      for (final read in _tokensUntilStarted) {
        read.complete();
      }
      _tokensUntilStarted.clear();
    }
    if (first) await earlier;
  }

  /// Signs in to the account of [email] with [password].
  ///
  /// An anonymous user becomes the user of that account, and what the
  /// anonymous user had moves to the account as `takeGuestData` says.
  Future<void> signIn({required String email, required String password}) =>
      _call(
        (service) =>
            _toAccount(() => service.signIn(email: email, password: password)),
      );

  /// Creates an account for [email] with [password] and signs in to it.
  ///
  /// An anonymous user gets the account and keeps the id, so everything
  /// that the user had stays.
  Future<void> signUp({required String email, required String password}) =>
      _call(
        (service) => _session is AnonymousSession
            ? service.linkPassword(email: email, password: password)
            : service.signUp(email: email, password: password),
      );

  /// Signs in with [credential], which a way to sign in of
  /// `appSignInMethods` got from an identity provider.
  ///
  /// An anonymous user gets the identity of the credential and keeps the id,
  /// so everything that the user had stays. When that identity belongs to
  /// another user already, or its email address has an account that signs
  /// in in another way, the anonymous user signs in to that account
  /// instead, or the call fails with
  /// [AuthFailureReason.accountExistsWithOtherMethod], as the backend of the
  /// provider decides. What the anonymous user had moves to the account as
  /// `takeGuestData` says, as with [signIn].
  Future<void> signInWith(SignInCredential credential) => _call((
    service,
  ) async {
    if (_session is AnonymousSession) {
      try {
        await service.linkWith(credential);
        return;
      } on AuthFailure catch (failure) {
        if (failure.reason != AuthFailureReason.credentialInUse &&
            failure.reason != AuthFailureReason.accountExistsWithOtherMethod) {
          rethrow;
        }
      }
    }
    await _toAccount(() => service.signInWith(credential));
  });

  /// Sends the message with which the owner of the account of [email] sets
  /// a new password. It completes whether [email] has an account or not.
  Future<void> sendPasswordReset(String email) =>
      _call((service) => service.sendPasswordReset(email));

  /// Signs the user out. In the mode [AuthMode.anonymous], the app then
  /// signs in anonymously again, as when it starts.
  Future<void> signOut() =>
      _call((service) => service.signOut(), waitsForAnonymousUser: true);

  /// Deletes the user who is signed in. The app is then without a user, as
  /// after [signOut], and in the mode [AuthMode.anonymous] it signs in
  /// anonymously again.
  ///
  /// For an account that signs in with a way to sign in that
  /// `revokesOnDeletion` and is available on this platform, it first asks
  /// that way for a credential again, such as with the sheet of its
  /// identity provider, so that the provider of sign-in can revoke the
  /// tokens of the app. When the user closes the sheet, the call completes
  /// and deletes nothing, which is no failure.
  ///
  /// It may fail with [AuthFailureReason.recentSignInRequired]: the user
  /// then signs out, signs in again and repeats it.
  Future<void> deleteAccount() => _call((service) async {
    final revoking = _revokingMethod();
    if (revoking == null) {
      await service.deleteAccount();
      return;
    }
    final fresh = await revoking.credential();
    if (fresh != null) await service.deleteAccount(fresh: fresh);
  }, waitsForAnonymousUser: true);

  /// The token of the user for a server of the app, or `null` when nobody is
  /// signed in.
  ///
  /// It waits for the first start and for an anonymous sign-in on its way,
  /// together at most [anonymousWait], and for no other call: the sheet of a
  /// way to sign in may be open for minutes, and a request does not wait for
  /// it. So a read made before `initAuth()` is not over, as in `bootstrap()`,
  /// answers `null` once that wait is over, if there is no service by then.
  ///
  /// It is not one of the calls that run one after another, and tells the
  /// listeners of the session nothing, also when the provider tells of the
  /// same user again, unless the provider found that the session of the user
  /// has ended on the server: the session then has nobody when the call fails,
  /// with [AuthFailureReason.recentSignInRequired].
  Future<String?> userToken() => _token((service) => service.userToken());

  /// A token to use in place of [rejected], which a server refused, or `null`
  /// when nobody is signed in.
  ///
  /// Reads at the same time share one refresh. A read that starts after that
  /// refresh, with the same [rejected], takes its token and needs no other. A
  /// read that was on its way during the refresh may still answer [rejected],
  /// which costs one more refresh. It waits and fails as [userToken] does.
  Future<String?> userTokenAfter(String rejected) async {
    final current = await userToken();
    if (current != rejected) return current;
    final uid = _session.uid;
    return _tokenRefreshes[uid] ??=
        _token((service) => service.userToken(forceRefresh: true)).whenComplete(
          () {
            // Not as an arrow: the refresh would then wait for itself.
            _tokenRefreshes.remove(uid);
          },
        );
  }

  /// Stops following the service and the lifecycle of the app. A call that
  /// is on its way still ends as it would have, but tells no listener, and
  /// no anonymous sign-in follows it.
  @override
  void dispose() {
    _disposed = true;
    unawaited(_userChanges?.cancel());
    if (_observer case final observer?) {
      WidgetsBinding.instance.removeObserver(observer);
    }
    super.dispose();
  }

  /// The way to sign in of the account of the session that asks for a
  /// credential again before the account is deleted and is available on
  /// this platform, or `null` if it has none: the first of
  /// `appSignInMethods` that does.
  SignInMethod? _revokingMethod() => switch (_session) {
    AccountSession(:final issuers) =>
      appSignInMethods
          .where(
            (method) =>
                method.revokesOnDeletion &&
                method.isAvailable &&
                issuers.contains(method.issuer),
          )
          .firstOrNull,
    _ => null,
  };

  /// Reads the token of the user with [read], once the wait of [userToken]
  /// is over, and `null` when the app has no service by then.
  ///
  /// When it fails, the session takes the user that the service has left. A
  /// call that takes its turn follows, so that in the mode
  /// [AuthMode.anonymous] the app signs in anonymously again, but not next to
  /// a sign-in that is on its way.
  Future<String?> _token(
    Future<String?> Function(AuthService service) read,
  ) async {
    if (!_started || _anonymousSignIns.containsKey(_service)) {
      await _untilTokenCanBeRead().timeout(anonymousWait, onTimeout: () {});
    }
    final service = _service;
    if (service == null) return null;
    try {
      return await read(service);
    } on Object catch (error, stackTrace) {
      _sync();
      _retry();
      if (error is AuthFailure) rethrow;
      Error.throwWithStackTrace(
        AuthFailure(AuthFailureReason.unknown, developerHint: '$error'),
        stackTrace,
      );
    }
  }

  Future<void> _untilTokenCanBeRead() async {
    if (!_started) {
      final started = Completer<void>();
      _tokensUntilStarted.add(started);
      await started.future;
    }
    await _anonymousSignIns[_service];
  }

  /// The user came back to the app. In the mode [AuthMode.anonymous], an
  /// app without a user then signs in anonymously again: as what follows
  /// every call, so this takes a turn among the calls with nothing else to
  /// do, and starts no anonymous sign-in next to a sign-in.
  void _retry() => unawaited(_call((service) async {}));

  /// Runs [call] with the service once the calls made before it are over,
  /// and not before the app has started for the first time.
  ///
  /// In the mode [AuthMode.anonymous], a call that leaves nobody signed in
  /// is followed by an anonymous sign-in. A call that
  /// [waitsForAnonymousUser], as a sign-out, completes once that sign-in is
  /// over or took too long, as the start does.
  Future<void> _call(
    Future<void> Function(AuthService service) call, {
    bool waitsForAnonymousUser = false,
  }) async {
    final earlier =
        _lastCall ??
        (_started ? null : (_untilStarted ??= Completer<void>()).future);
    final done = Completer<void>();
    final own = _lastCall = done.future;
    if (earlier != null) await earlier;
    try {
      await _guarded(call);
      final signIn = _signInAnonymously();
      if (waitsForAnonymousUser) await signIn;
    } finally {
      if (identical(_lastCall, own)) _lastCall = null;
      done.complete();
    }
  }

  /// Runs [call] with the service, once an anonymous sign-in that is on its
  /// way is over: of a call and a sign-in that run at the same time, the
  /// one that ended last would decide who the user is.
  ///
  /// Whatever [call] throws that is no [AuthFailure] becomes one, and the
  /// session has the user of the service when it is over, also when it
  /// failed. A call that failed does not wait for the anonymous sign-in
  /// that follows it, so that its failure is not late.
  Future<void> _guarded(Future<void> Function(AuthService service) call) async {
    final service = _service!;
    try {
      await _anonymousSignIns[service];
      await call(service);
    } on Object catch (error, stackTrace) {
      _sync();
      unawaited(_signInAnonymously());
      if (error is AuthFailure) rethrow;
      Error.throwWithStackTrace(
        AuthFailure(AuthFailureReason.unknown, developerHint: '$error'),
        stackTrace,
      );
    }
    _sync();
  }

  /// Signs in to an account with [signIn], in whichever way, and moves what
  /// an anonymous user had to that account, when it has another id.
  ///
  /// It takes the data before the sign-in, while the anonymous user is
  /// still signed in, and gives it once the user of another id has an
  /// account. If taking fails, the call fails and nothing has changed. If
  /// giving fails, the user is signed in already, so the call completes and
  /// the error is reported as an error of the app.
  Future<void> _toAccount(Future<void> Function() signIn) async {
    final guest = _session;
    final give = guest is AnonymousSession
        ? await takeGuestData(guest.uid)
        : null;
    await signIn();
    _sync();
    final account = _session;
    if (give == null ||
        account is! AccountSession ||
        account.uid == guest.uid) {
      return;
    }
    try {
      await give(account.uid);
    } on Object catch (error, stackTrace) {
      _report(
        error,
        stackTrace,
        'while giving the data of a guest to the account',
      );
    }
  }

  /// Reports [error], which no call can fail with, as an error of the app.
  void _report(Object error, StackTrace stackTrace, String during) =>
      FlutterError.reportError(
        FlutterErrorDetails(
          exception: error,
          stack: stackTrace,
          library: 'app session',
          context: ErrorDescription(during),
        ),
      );

  /// In the mode [AuthMode.anonymous], signs in anonymously when nobody is
  /// signed in, and waits for that for at most [anonymousWait].
  ///
  /// It never fails. A sign-in that takes longer goes on and becomes the
  /// session when it comes. One that fails leaves the app without a user
  /// until the next try, and in debug mode its error is printed.
  Future<void> _signInAnonymously() async {
    final service = _service!;
    if (_disposed ||
        mode != AuthMode.anonymous ||
        service.currentUser != null) {
      return;
    }
    final signIn = _anonymousSignIns[service] ??= _tryAnonymously(service)
        .whenComplete(() {
          _anonymousSignIns.remove(service);
          _sync();
        });
    await signIn.timeout(anonymousWait, onTimeout: () {});
  }

  Future<void> _tryAnonymously(AuthService service) async {
    try {
      await service.signInAnonymously();
    } on Object catch (error) {
      if (kDebugMode) {
        debugPrint(
          'The anonymous sign-in failed, so the app has no user: $error',
        );
      }
    }
  }

  /// Takes the user of the service as the session, and tells the listeners
  /// when that changes it, unless the session was disposed of.
  void _sync() {
    if (_disposed) return;
    final session = switch (_service!.currentUser) {
      null => const SignedOutSession(),
      AuthUser(isAnonymous: true, :final uid) => AnonymousSession(uid),
      AuthUser(:final uid, :final email, :final issuers) => AccountSession(
        uid,
        email: email,
        issuers: Set.unmodifiable(issuers),
      ),
    };
    if (session == _session) return;
    _session = session;
    notifyListeners();
  }
}

/// Calls [_onResume] each time the app is resumed, whatever state it was in
/// before.
final class _ResumeObserver with WidgetsBindingObserver {
  _ResumeObserver(this._onResume);

  final VoidCallback _onResume;

  @override
  void didChangeAppLifecycleState(AppLifecycleState state) {
    if (state == AppLifecycleState.resumed) _onResume();
  }
}

/// A flag of the session of a controller: its value is computed from the
/// session each time it is read, and its listeners are those of the
/// session.
final class _SessionFlag implements ValueListenable<bool> {
  const _SessionFlag(this._controller, this._of);

  final AppSessionController _controller;
  final bool Function(AppSession session) _of;

  @override
  bool get value => _of(_controller.value);

  @override
  void addListener(VoidCallback listener) => _controller.addListener(listener);

  @override
  void removeListener(VoidCallback listener) =>
      _controller.removeListener(listener);
}

/// Returns the service of the provider of sign-in, which [initAuth] created:
/// `bootstrap()` awaits it before the first frame, and there is none to
/// return before that.
///
/// The code of the app does not call it: it signs in through [appSession].
AuthService createAuthService() => _authService;

late AuthService _authService;

/// Creates the service of the provider of sign-in and starts the session of
/// the app with it; `bootstrap()` awaits it before the first frame, and
/// nothing else in the app calls it.
///
/// It may run again, as a test does to see what the next launch of the app
/// finds: it creates the service anew, with the user who is signed in, and
/// starts the session with it.
Future<void> initAuth() async {
  _authService = await impl0.openFixtureAuth();
  await appSession.start(_authService);
}
=== lib/core/auth/auth_service.dart (role:auth) ===
/// A user as the provider of sign-in knows them.
final class AuthUser {
  /// Creates the user [uid].
  const AuthUser({
    required this.uid,
    required this.isAnonymous,
    this.email,
    this.issuers = const {},
  });

  /// The id of the user. It stays the same for as long as the user exists,
  /// also when an anonymous user gets an account.
  final String uid;

  /// Whether the user is anonymous: a user that the app signed in itself,
  /// who has no account and nothing to sign in with again.
  final bool isAnonymous;

  /// The email address of the user, or `null` for a user without one, such
  /// as an anonymous user.
  final String? email;

  /// The identity providers whose identities the user signs in with, each
  /// by the issuer of its tokens, such as `https://accounts.example.com`:
  /// the [IdTokenCredential.issuer] of a credential that the user signed in
  /// or linked with. It is empty for a user who signs in with a password
  /// alone, and for an anonymous user.
  final Set<String> issuers;
}

/// What a way to sign in got from an identity provider, such as the token
/// of an account of the user at another service, with which the provider
/// of sign-in signs the user in: [AuthService.signInWith] and
/// [AuthService.linkWith] take it.
sealed class SignInCredential {
  const SignInCredential();
}

/// An OpenID Connect ID token that an identity provider issued, with what
/// came with it.
final class IdTokenCredential extends SignInCredential {
  /// Creates the credential of [idToken], which [issuer] issued.
  const IdTokenCredential({
    required this.issuer,
    required this.idToken,
    this.rawNonce,
    this.authorizationCode,
    this.givenName,
    this.familyName,
  });

  /// The identity provider that issued the token: the `iss` of the token,
  /// an https URL such as `https://accounts.example.com`.
  final String issuer;

  /// The ID token, a JSON Web Token.
  final String idToken;

  /// The nonce whose hash the request for the token had, which the
  /// identity provider wrote into the token, or `null` if the request had
  /// none. The provider of sign-in passes it on where its backend checks
  /// the token against it.
  final String? rawNonce;

  /// The authorization code that came with the token, or `null`. With it, a
  /// provider of sign-in revokes the tokens that the identity provider gave
  /// the app when the account is deleted.
  final String? authorizationCode;

  /// The given name of the user, or `null`, for an identity provider that
  /// tells it apart from the token, and maybe only at the first sign-in.
  final String? givenName;

  /// The family name of the user, as [givenName].
  final String? familyName;

  /// Names the issuer, and no token, so that a token never reaches a log.
  @override
  String toString() => 'IdTokenCredential($issuer)';
}

/// Why a call of sign-in failed.
enum AuthFailureReason {
  /// The email address and the password match no account. A wrong password
  /// and an address without an account are this one reason, so that nobody
  /// learns from the app which addresses have an account.
  ///
  /// It is also the reason of a [SignInCredential] that the provider
  /// refuses, such as one that expired or that was issued for another app,
  /// and of one of another user than the one whose account is deleted.
  invalidCredentials,

  /// The email address has an account already.
  emailInUse,

  /// The identity of a [SignInCredential] belongs to another user already,
  /// so [AuthService.linkWith] gives it to nobody else. The session then
  /// signs in to that user.
  credentialInUse,

  /// The email address of the identity of a [SignInCredential] has an
  /// account that signs in in another way, such as with a password. The
  /// user signs in that way.
  accountExistsWithOtherMethod,

  /// The provider takes no password as weak as this one.
  weakPassword,

  /// The text is no email address.
  invalidEmail,

  /// The account is disabled.
  userDisabled,

  /// Too many attempts: the provider takes no more for a while.
  tooManyAttempts,

  /// The device did not reach the provider.
  network,

  /// The call needs a user who signed in a short while ago, as the deletion
  /// of an account does: the user signs in again and repeats it.
  ///
  /// It is also the reason of a call for a user whose session has ended on
  /// the server. The service signs that user out on the device before it
  /// fails.
  recentSignInRequired,

  /// Sign-in is not set up at the provider, such as a way to sign in that
  /// is not enabled there. This is a mistake in the setup of the app, which
  /// [AuthFailure.developerHint] explains.
  notConfigured,

  /// Anything else. [AuthFailure.developerHint] has the error.
  unknown,
}

/// The failure of a call of sign-in: the only thing that such a call throws.
final class AuthFailure implements Exception {
  /// Creates the failure for [reason].
  const AuthFailure(this.reason, {this.developerHint});

  /// Why the call failed. The app shows a text of its own for each reason.
  final AuthFailureReason reason;

  /// What went wrong in the words of the provider, for the developer of the
  /// app, or `null`. It is no text for the user: show it in debug mode
  /// only.
  final String? developerHint;

  @override
  String toString() => developerHint == null
      ? 'AuthFailure: ${reason.name}'
      : 'AuthFailure: ${reason.name} ($developerHint)';
}

/// The accounts of the users of the app and the user of this device, as the
/// provider of sign-in keeps them.
///
/// The provider implements it. The code of the app does not call it: it
/// signs in through `appSession` of `app_session.dart`, which calls this
/// service and does what is the same with every provider.
///
/// Every implementation keeps to this, in every mode of the app, whatever
/// the provider generates for the mode:
/// - The service has [currentUser] from the moment it is created: the user
///   who was signed in on this device when the app last ran, without a
///   request to the server. The function that creates the service may be
///   called again, and the service that it returns then has the user who is
///   signed in now.
/// - When the future of a call completes, [currentUser] is the result of
///   the call. [userChanges] tells of every change of [currentUser], also
///   of one that no call here made, and when it tells of a user,
///   [currentUser] is that user.
/// - A call fails only with an [AuthFailure], and every call ends: when the
///   server does not answer, its future completes with
///   [AuthFailureReason.network].
/// - A call that fails leaves the user who is signed in as that user was:
///   an anonymous user stays signed in when [linkPassword] or [linkWith]
///   fails, and so does whoever is signed in when [signIn], [signUp] or
///   [signInWith] fails. The one exception is a call for a user whose
///   session has ended on the server.
/// - [linkPassword], [linkWith] and [deleteAccount] are calls for the user
///   who is signed in. Made when nobody is signed in, or when the session of
///   that user has ended on the server, such a call fails with
///   [AuthFailureReason.recentSignInRequired], and in the second case it
///   signs the user out on the device first. [userToken] reads, so it answers
///   `null` when nobody is signed in, and does the same as those calls when
///   the session of the user has ended on the server.
/// - A [SignInCredential] whose identity provider the provider does not
///   take fails with [AuthFailureReason.notConfigured], with the issuer in
///   the [AuthFailure.developerHint].
abstract interface class AuthService {
  /// The user who is signed in on this device, or `null`.
  AuthUser? get currentUser;

  /// Tells of each change of [currentUser], once [currentUser] has changed:
  /// a broadcast stream. When it tells of a user, [currentUser] is that
  /// user. It may also tell of a user who has not changed.
  ///
  /// An error that it sends changes nothing: the session reports it as an
  /// error of the app and goes on listening.
  Stream<AuthUser?> get userChanges;

  /// Signs in to the account of [email] with [password].
  ///
  /// A wrong password and an address without an account both fail with
  /// [AuthFailureReason.invalidCredentials].
  ///
  /// It is also called while a user is signed in, an anonymous one or the
  /// user of another account: the user of this account then replaces that
  /// user on the device. When it fails, that user stays signed in.
  Future<void> signIn({required String email, required String password});

  /// Creates an account for [email] with [password] and signs in to it.
  ///
  /// For an address that has an account already, it fails with
  /// [AuthFailureReason.emailInUse] and changes nothing: that account keeps
  /// its password, and whoever is signed in stays signed in.
  ///
  /// It is also called while the user of another account is signed in, and
  /// the user of the new account then replaces that user on the device.
  /// For an anonymous user, the session calls [linkPassword] instead.
  Future<void> signUp({required String email, required String password});

  /// Gives the anonymous user who is signed in an account for [email] with
  /// [password]. The user keeps the [AuthUser.uid] and is no longer
  /// anonymous.
  ///
  /// For an address that has an account already, it fails with
  /// [AuthFailureReason.emailInUse] and changes nothing: the anonymous user
  /// stays signed in, with the same id.
  Future<void> linkPassword({required String email, required String password});

  /// Signs in with [credential], which a way to sign in got from an
  /// identity provider: to the user of its identity, whom it creates when
  /// the identity is new.
  ///
  /// It is also called while a user is signed in, an anonymous one or the
  /// user of another account: the user of the identity then replaces that
  /// user on the device. When it fails, that user stays signed in.
  ///
  /// It takes a credential that [linkWith] refused just before, with
  /// [AuthFailureReason.credentialInUse] or
  /// [AuthFailureReason.accountExistsWithOtherMethod]: the session passes
  /// the identical object. An identity provider may take a credential only
  /// once, so the service signs in with what its backend answered the
  /// refused link for that object, or with the credential itself when the
  /// backend gave nothing. For an identity whose email address has an
  /// account that signs in in another way, it fails with
  /// [AuthFailureReason.accountExistsWithOtherMethod] or signs in to that
  /// account, as the backend of the provider decides.
  Future<void> signInWith(SignInCredential credential);

  /// Gives the user who is signed in the identity of [credential]: the user
  /// keeps the [AuthUser.uid], is no longer anonymous, and signs in with
  /// that identity from then on. The session calls it for an anonymous
  /// user.
  ///
  /// When the identity belongs to another user, it fails with
  /// [AuthFailureReason.credentialInUse], and when its email address has an
  /// account of another user, with
  /// [AuthFailureReason.accountExistsWithOtherMethod]. Either way it changes
  /// nothing: the user stays signed in, with the same id.
  ///
  /// When it fails with either reason, it keeps what its backend answered
  /// for this credential object, such as a credential to sign in with,
  /// without holding the token longer than needed. The session passes the
  /// identical object to [signInWith], which uses that answer, or the
  /// credential itself when the backend gave nothing.
  Future<void> linkWith(SignInCredential credential);

  /// Creates an anonymous user, with an id of its own, and signs in as that
  /// user.
  Future<void> signInAnonymously();

  /// Sends the message with which the owner of the account of [email] sets
  /// a new password.
  ///
  /// It completes whether [email] has an account or not, so that nobody
  /// learns from the app which addresses have one.
  Future<void> sendPasswordReset(String email);

  /// Signs the user out on this device. It completes when nobody is signed
  /// in too.
  Future<void> signOut();

  /// Deletes the user who is signed in, an anonymous one too, and signs
  /// out.
  ///
  /// It succeeds for a user who has just signed in, in whichever way, and
  /// for an anonymous user who was just created. For a user who signed in
  /// longer ago, such as one whom the service found on the device when it
  /// was created, it may fail with
  /// [AuthFailureReason.recentSignInRequired]. It then succeeds once the
  /// user has signed in again, in whichever way the account allows.
  ///
  /// With [fresh], a credential that a way to sign in of the user got just
  /// before, the service first signs the user in again with it, so the
  /// deletion needs no recent sign-in, and revokes the tokens that the
  /// identity provider gave the app where its backend can, such as with
  /// [IdTokenCredential.authorizationCode]. A credential of another user
  /// fails with [AuthFailureReason.invalidCredentials], and a revocation
  /// that fails, such as one that the backend is not set up for, with
  /// [AuthFailureReason.notConfigured]. Either way nothing is deleted.
  Future<void> deleteAccount({SignInCredential? fresh});

  /// The token with which a server of the app tells who the user is, or
  /// `null` when nobody is signed in. An anonymous user has one too.
  ///
  /// It comes from the device when it can, and the service refreshes one that
  /// is about to expire on its own, so most reads make no request. With
  /// [forceRefresh] it asks the server of the provider for a new token, which
  /// differs from the one before and which the reads after it give. A token
  /// that the server issued in the same second may come back the same.
  ///
  /// A read changes no user, and leaves whoever is signed in, but in the case
  /// below. [userChanges] may tell of the same user again after it, as after
  /// any change of the token, which the session ignores.
  ///
  /// It fails with [AuthFailureReason.network] when it needs the server and
  /// does not reach it, and with [AuthFailureReason.tooManyAttempts] when the
  /// server refuses for now. It fails with
  /// [AuthFailureReason.recentSignInRequired] for a user whose session has
  /// ended on the server, or may fail with [AuthFailureReason.userDisabled]
  /// for one whom the server disabled: the service signs that user out on the
  /// device first, while that user is still signed in. Anything else is
  /// [AuthFailureReason.unknown], with what the provider answered as its
  /// [AuthFailure.developerHint]. No hint of a failure has the token.
  Future<String?> userToken({bool forceRefresh = false});
}
=== lib/core/auth/guest_data.dart (role:auth) ===
/// Gives the account [accountUid] what a guest had: the function that
/// [takeGuestData] returns.
typedef GiveGuestData = Future<void> Function(String accountUid);

/// Takes what the guest [guestUid] has in the app, and returns the function
/// that gives it to an account, or `null` when there is nothing to move.
///
/// A guest is an anonymous user. When a guest signs in to an account that
/// exists already, the user of the app changes from the guest to that
/// account, and what was kept under the id of the guest, such as a cart on
/// a server, would be left behind. So the session of the app calls this
/// function before the sign-in, while the guest is still signed in and may
/// read that data, and calls the function that it returns once the user of
/// the account is signed in:
///
/// ```dart
/// Future<GiveGuestData?> takeGuestData(String guestUid) async {
///   final cart = await carts.read(guestUid);
///   if (cart.isEmpty) return null;
///   return (accountUid) => carts.add(accountUid, cart);
/// }
/// ```
///
/// Only read here: the sign-in may still fail, and then the guest stays. An
/// error thrown here fails the sign-in before it starts. An error of the
/// function that this one returns is reported as an error of the app, and
/// the user stays signed in.
///
/// Call no method of `appSession` here, nor in the function that this one
/// returns. The sign-in is one of its calls, which run one after another,
/// so such a call would wait for the sign-in, and the sign-in for it.
///
/// It is not called when a guest signs up, since the guest then keeps the
/// id and everything under it, nor when a guest signs in with a way to sign
/// in whose identity no user has yet, which the guest then gets with the
/// id, nor in an app without anonymous users.
Future<GiveGuestData?> takeGuestData(String guestUid) async => null;

/// A way to sign in with an identity provider, such as the account of the
/// user at another service: the label and the icon of its button, the
/// platforms that it works on, and the function that asks the identity
/// provider for a credential.
///
/// A screen of sign-in shows a button for each method of
/// [appSignInMethods] that [isAvailable]. On a tap, the state of the screen
/// calls [credential] and gives what it returns to `appSession.signInWith`
/// of `app_session.dart`.
final class SignInMethod {
  /// Creates a way to sign in with the identity provider [issuer].
  const SignInMethod({
    required this.label,
    required this.icon,
    required this.platforms,
    required this.credential,
    required this.issuer,
    this.revokesOnDeletion = false,
  });

  /// Returns the label of the button in the language of the app, for the
  /// context of the widget that shows it. That widget calls it each time it
  /// builds, so the text follows the language of the app.
  final String Function(BuildContext context) label;

  /// The icon of the button, a constant widget: an `Icon` takes the colour
  /// of the label, and an `Image` keeps its own colours.
  final Widget icon;

  /// The platforms that the way to sign in works on. On any other,
  /// [isAvailable] is false.
  final Set<TargetPlatform> platforms;

  /// Asks the identity provider for a credential, such as with a sheet of
  /// its own, and returns it, or `null` when the user closed the sheet,
  /// which is no failure. It fails only with an `AuthFailure`.
  ///
  /// The state of a screen calls it, and no code within a call of
  /// `appSession`: `appSession.deleteAccount()` calls it itself, for a
  /// method that [revokesOnDeletion].
  final Future<SignInCredential?> Function() credential;

  /// The identity provider: the issuer of its tokens, an https URL such as
  /// `https://accounts.example.com`. `AccountSession.issuers` of
  /// `app_session.dart` lists those of the user of an account.
  final String issuer;

  /// Whether the deletion of an account that signs in this way asks the
  /// identity provider for a credential again first, so that the provider
  /// of sign-in can revoke the tokens that the identity provider gave the
  /// app. `appSession.deleteAccount()` does it.
  final bool revokesOnDeletion;

  /// Whether the way to sign in works on the platform that the app runs on.
  bool get isAvailable => platforms.contains(defaultTargetPlatform);
}

/// of their modules. An email address and a password are no such way:
/// `appSession.signIn` and `appSession.signUp` take them.

=== lib/core/crash_reporting/crash_reporter.dart (role:crash_reporting) ===
import 'dart:async';

import 'package:fixture_app/core/fixture_crash/fixture_crash.dart' as impl0;
import 'package:flutter/foundation.dart';

/// Reports the errors of the app.
abstract interface class CrashReporter {
  /// Reports [error] with its [stackTrace]; a [fatal] error ended the app.
  Future<void> recordError(
    Object error,
    StackTrace? stackTrace, {
    bool fatal = false,
    String? reason,
  });

  /// Reports an error that Flutter caught, described by [details].
  Future<void> recordFlutterError(
    FlutterErrorDetails details, {
    bool fatal = false,
  });

  /// Adds [message] to the log sent with the next report.
  Future<void> log(String message);

  /// Sets the id of the signed-in user for the next reports, or clears it
  /// with `null`.
  Future<void> setUserId(String? userId);
}

/// Returns the crash reporter of the app, which forwards every call to the
/// crash reporters of all modules.
///
/// It calls each of them on its own, and its future completes once all of
/// them are done. A crash reporter that throws, or whose future fails,
/// keeps no other from the call, and its failure reaches neither the code
/// that called nor the handlers of [installCrashReporting], which would
/// report it to the same crash reporter again, without end. In debug mode
/// the failure is printed, so that a crash reporter that does not work,
/// such as one that is not set up, shows in the console. A crash reporter
/// whose factory throws, or that fails to start, is left out, and in debug
/// mode its error is printed.
CrashReporter createCrashReporter() => _crashReporter;

/// Reports the errors of the main isolate that nothing handles, each once.
///
/// `bootstrap()` calls it once the crash reporting services are ready. Two
/// handlers cover the main isolate:
/// - `FlutterError.onError` gets the errors that Flutter catches, such as in
///   a build or a layout, and still presents them as Flutter does by
///   default;
/// - `PlatformDispatcher.instance.onError` gets every other uncaught error,
///   such as in a `Future`, a `Timer` or the handler of a port, with its
///   type. In debug mode it leaves the error unhandled, so the engine prints
///   it.
///
/// Each report reaches every crash reporter, and the failure of a crash
/// reporter comes back to neither handler; see [createCrashReporter].
///
/// `compute()` and `Isolate.run()` throw the error of their isolate to the
/// code that awaits them, so it reaches the platform dispatcher unless that
/// code catches it. The uncaught errors of an isolate that the app spawns
/// itself go only to the error listener of that isolate, such as the
/// `onError` port of `Isolate.spawn`, so the app reports them there to its
/// crash reporter.
void installCrashReporting() {
  final reporter = createCrashReporter();
  final presentError = FlutterError.onError;
  FlutterError.onError = (details) {
    presentError?.call(details);
    unawaited(reporter.recordFlutterError(details, fatal: true));
  };
  PlatformDispatcher.instance.onError = (error, stackTrace) {
    unawaited(reporter.recordError(error, stackTrace, fatal: true));
    return !kDebugMode;
  };
}

final CrashReporter _crashReporter = _CrashReporters(_crashReporters);

final List<CrashReporter> _crashReporters = [];

/// Creates the implementations of CrashReporter that start
/// asynchronously; `bootstrap()` awaits it.
Future<void> initCrashReporting() async {
  final started = await Future.wait([
    _startAlone('initFixtureCrashReporter', impl0.initFixtureCrashReporter),
  ]);
  _crashReporters.addAll(started.nonNulls);
}

/// Calls [start], the function [name], on its own: returns what it starts,
/// or `null` if it throws or its future fails, so that the app starts and
/// works without it, and in debug mode prints the error.
Future<CrashReporter?> _startAlone(
  String name,
  Future<CrashReporter> Function() start,
) async {
  try {
    return await start();
  } on Object catch (error) {
    if (kDebugMode) {
      debugPrint('$name() failed, so the app works without it: $error');
    }
    return null;
  }
}

final class _CrashReporters implements CrashReporter {
  const _CrashReporters(this._reporters);

  final List<CrashReporter> _reporters;

  @override
  Future<void> recordError(
    Object error,
    StackTrace? stackTrace, {
    bool fatal = false,
    String? reason,
  }) => _forAll(
    (reporter) =>
        reporter.recordError(error, stackTrace, fatal: fatal, reason: reason),
  );

  @override
  Future<void> recordFlutterError(
    FlutterErrorDetails details, {
    bool fatal = false,
  }) =>
      _forAll((reporter) => reporter.recordFlutterError(details, fatal: fatal));

  @override
  Future<void> log(String message) =>
      _forAll((reporter) => reporter.log(message));

  @override
  Future<void> setUserId(String? userId) =>
      _forAll((reporter) => reporter.setUserId(userId));

  /// Calls [call] with each crash reporter on its own, and completes once
  /// all of them are done, whatever each does; see [createCrashReporter].
  Future<void> _forAll(
    Future<void> Function(CrashReporter reporter) call,
  ) async {
    await Future.wait([
      for (final reporter in _reporters) _callAlone(reporter, call),
    ]);
  }

  /// Calls [call] with [reporter], and keeps what it throws, or the error of
  /// its future, from the caller: in debug mode it prints it.
  static Future<void> _callAlone(
    CrashReporter reporter,
    Future<void> Function(CrashReporter reporter) call,
  ) async {
    try {
      await call(reporter);
    } on Object catch (error) {
      if (kDebugMode) {
        debugPrint('The crash reporter ${reporter.runtimeType} failed: $error');
      }
    }
  }
}

    dispose: di0.closeFixtureCounter,

    () => di2.createCommunicationService(),
  );
  locator.lazy<di3.AppPreferences>(() => di3.createAppPreferences());
  locator.lazy<di4.AnalyticsService>(() => di4.createAnalyticsService());
}

/// Disposes of the services that the map created, with the functions that
/// dispose of them, in the reverse order of their registration, and
/// removes every service, so that [registerDependencies] can register them
/// again.
Future<void> resetDependencies() => (serviceLocator as _MapLocator).reset();

final class _MapLocator implements ServiceLocator {
  final Map<(Type, String?), Object Function()> _getters = {};

  /// What disposes of each service that has a function to dispose of it,
  /// in the order of the registrations.
  final List<FutureOr<void> Function()> _disposers = [];

  @override
  T resolve<T extends Object>({String? instanceName}) {
    final getter = _getters[(T, instanceName)];
    if (getter == null) throw StateError('$T is not registered.');
    return getter() as T;
  }

  void singleton<T extends Object>(
    T instance, {
    String? name,
    FutureOr<void> Function(T service)? dispose,
  }) {
    _getters[(T, name)] = () => instance;
    if (dispose != null) _disposers.add(() => dispose(instance));
  }

  void lazy<T extends Object>(
    T Function() create, {
    String? name,
    FutureOr<void> Function(T service)? dispose,
  }) {
    T? instance;
    _getters[(T, name)] = () => instance ??= create();
    if (dispose != null) {
      // A lazy singleton that nothing resolved is not created only to be
      // disposed of.
      _disposers.add(() {
        if (instance case final created?) return dispose(created);
      });
    }
  }

  void factoryOf<T extends Object>(T Function() create, {String? name}) =>
      _getters[(T, name)] = create;

  Future<void> reset() async {
    for (final dispose in _disposers.reversed) {
      await dispose();
    }
    _disposers.clear();
    _getters.clear();
  }
}

=== lib/core/env/app_env.dart (fake_env) ===
/// The values of `.env` (fixture): a command of the `flutter` tool that
/// builds, runs or tests the app reads `.env` when it starts, given
/// `--dart-define-from-file .env`, and the class keeps what it read in a
/// constant map, behind a static getter for each key, which returns the
/// value, or `null` while it is empty or missing.
abstract final class AppEnv {
  /// The value of `FAKE_ENV_USER_FIRST`.
  static String? get fakeEnvUserFirst => _valueOf('FAKE_ENV_USER_FIRST');

  /// The value of `FAKE_ENV_USER_SECOND`.
  static String? get fakeEnvUserSecond => _valueOf('FAKE_ENV_USER_SECOND');

  /// The value of `FAKE_ENV_USER_THIRD`.
  static String? get fakeEnvUserThird => _valueOf('FAKE_ENV_USER_THIRD');

  /// The value of `REST_BASE_URL`.
  static String? get restBaseUrl => _valueOf('REST_BASE_URL');

  /// What the command that built the app read from `.env`, by the keys of
  /// the app: an empty value as an empty text, and no entry for a key that
  /// `.env` lacks.
  static const Map<String, String> _values = {
    if (bool.hasEnvironment('FAKE_ENV_USER_FIRST'))
      'FAKE_ENV_USER_FIRST': String.fromEnvironment('FAKE_ENV_USER_FIRST'),
    if (bool.hasEnvironment('FAKE_ENV_USER_SECOND'))
      'FAKE_ENV_USER_SECOND': String.fromEnvironment('FAKE_ENV_USER_SECOND'),
    if (bool.hasEnvironment('FAKE_ENV_USER_THIRD'))
      'FAKE_ENV_USER_THIRD': String.fromEnvironment('FAKE_ENV_USER_THIRD'),
    if (bool.hasEnvironment('REST_BASE_URL'))
      'REST_BASE_URL': String.fromEnvironment('REST_BASE_URL'),
  };

  /// The value of [key], or `null` while `.env` leaves it empty or lacks
  /// it.
  static String? _valueOf(String key) {
    final value = _values[key];
    return value == null || value.isEmpty ? null : value;
  }
}
=== lib/core/events/communication_service.dart (role:events) ===
import 'package:fixture_app/core/fixture_events/fixture_events.dart' as impl0;

/// A message that one part of the app sends to the others, such as
/// `final class ItemAdded extends AppEvent`.
abstract class AppEvent {
  /// Allows the events to have constant constructors.
  const AppEvent();
}

/// Delivers [AppEvent]s between parts of the app that do not know each
/// other.
///
/// Whether a listener of a type that an event extends or implements, such
/// as a listener of `on<AppEvent>()`, gets the event is up to the provider
/// of the service.
abstract interface class CommunicationService {
  /// Sends [event] to everyone listening to its type when it is fired.
  void fire(AppEvent event);

  /// The events of type [T] fired after the stream is listened to. An event
  /// fired before, even after this call returned the stream, is not in it.
  Stream<T> on<T extends AppEvent>();
}

/// Returns the communication service of the app.
CommunicationService createCommunicationService() => _communicationService;

late final CommunicationService _communicationService;

/// Creates the CommunicationService of the app, which starts asynchronously;
/// `bootstrap()` awaits it.
Future<void> initEvents() async {
  _communicationService = await impl0.openFixtureEvents();
}

=== lib/core/fixture_auth/fixture_auth.dart (fake_auth) ===
import 'dart:async';
import 'dart:convert';

import '../auth/auth_service.dart';

/// An account of the fixture: its email address and its password, if it has
/// them, and the identity providers that it signs in with, each by the
/// issuer of its tokens.
typedef _Account = ({String? email, String? password, Set<String> issuers});

/// The accounts of the fixture by the ids of their users. They stand for
/// the server of a provider of sign-in, which every start of the app finds
/// as the last one left it. An anonymous user has none.
final Map<String, _Account> _accounts = {};

/// The ids of the users of the identities of the fixture, by the issuer and
/// the subject of each identity, as its tokens name it.
final Map<(String, String), String> _identities = {};

/// The identity of a test token: its issuer, its subject and its email
/// address.
typedef _Identity = ({String issuer, String subject, String? email});

/// The nonces of the tokens that the fixture took. An identity provider that
/// checks a nonce takes a token with it once, and so does the fixture.
final Set<String> _spentNonces = {};

/// How many tokens the server gave each user, by the id of the user, once
/// the user has read one: a start of the app keeps a token, and a refresh
/// gives the next one.
final Map<String, int> _tokens = {};

/// The user who is signed in on this device, or `null`. It stands for the
/// storage of a device, which a start of the app reads.
AuthUser? _onDevice;

/// How many users the fixture created, anonymous ones too.
int _users = 0;

/// The id of a new user.
String _newUid() => 'fixture-user-${++_users}';

/// Waits for the answer of the server or of the storage of the device,
/// which takes a moment, as with a provider that has them: a turn of the
/// event loop. So a test makes the calls of sign-in in real time, as it
/// runs the start-up of the app.
Future<void> _answer() => Future<void>.delayed(Duration.zero);

/// Opens the sign-in of the fixture, as a start of the app does: it reads
/// from the storage of the device who is signed in, which takes a moment,
/// and returns the service with that user.
///
/// A start keeps the accounts, and the user who was signed in on the
/// device, an anonymous one too. It loses that the user signed in a short
/// while ago: the service deletes the account of a user whom it found on
/// the device only once that user has signed in again. Nothing outlives the
/// process of the app, so each run of the tests of a file starts without
/// an account and with nobody signed in.
Future<AuthService> openFixtureAuth() async {
  final service = FixtureAuth._();
  await service._restore();
  return service;
}

/// Sign-in with accounts in memory. It takes any text for an email address
/// and any password, and sends no message to reset one. Each of its calls
/// takes a moment, as one that asks a server does.
///
/// It takes a credential of any identity provider whose token is a test
/// token: a JSON Web Token without a signature whose payload has `iss`, the
/// issuer of the credential, `sub`, the identity, maybe `email`, the email
/// address of the identity, and maybe `nonce`, which is then the rawNonce of
/// the credential. It keeps each identity by its issuer and its subject, and
/// refuses any other token with [AuthFailureReason.invalidCredentials], as
/// it refuses a token whose nonce it took already. An identity whose
/// address has an account of another user, such as one with a password,
/// signs in to nothing: [linkWith] and [signInWith] fail with
/// [AuthFailureReason.accountExistsWithOtherMethod]. A refused link takes
/// the token too, and keeps the identity for the credential object, which
/// [signInWith] of the identical object then signs in with. It has no
/// tokens of an identity provider to revoke when it deletes an account. The
/// token that it gives a server is no JSON Web Token: it names the user and
/// how many tokens that user got.
final class FixtureAuth implements AuthService {
  FixtureAuth._();

  /// The identity of each credential object that [linkWith] refused, until
  /// [signInWith] of the identical object takes it.
  final Expando<_Identity> _refused = Expando('refused links');

  final StreamController<AuthUser?> _changes = StreamController.broadcast();

  AuthUser? _user;

  /// Whether the user signed in through this service, and so since the app
  /// started: a short while ago.
  bool _signedInHere = false;

  /// Takes the user who is signed in on the device, once its storage
  /// answered.
  Future<void> _restore() async {
    await _answer();
    _user = _onDevice;
    // No listener hears this: nothing has the service before
    // [openFixtureAuth] returns it, which waits for this. It is here so
    // that the fixture with a known bug that returns the service first
    // differs in one line, and tells of the user that it finds late.
    _changes.add(_user);
  }

  /// Signs [user] in on the device, or nobody, and tells of the change once
  /// [currentUser] has changed.
  void _set(AuthUser? user) {
    _signedInHere = user != null;
    _user = user;
    _onDevice = user;
    _changes.add(user);
  }

  /// The user who is signed in, for a call that is about that user; it
  /// fails when nobody is.
  AuthUser _signedIn() {
    final user = _user;
    if (user == null) {
      throw const AuthFailure(AuthFailureReason.recentSignInRequired);
    }
    return user;
  }

  /// The id of the user whose account has [email], or `null`.
  String? _ownerOf(String email) => _accounts.entries
      .where((account) => account.value.email == email)
      .firstOrNull
      ?.key;

  /// Fails when [email] has an account already.
  void _checkFree(String email) {
    if (_ownerOf(email) != null) {
      throw const AuthFailure(AuthFailureReason.emailInUse);
    }
  }

  /// The user of the account of [uid], which has one.
  AuthUser _userOf(String uid) {
    final account = _accounts[uid]!;
    return AuthUser(
      uid: uid,
      isAnonymous: false,
      email: account.email,
      issuers: {...account.issuers},
    );
  }

  /// The identity of [credential], whose token the call takes: what the
  /// fixture kept for the identical object when [linkWith] refused it just
  /// before, or else the identity of its test token, whose nonce is then
  /// spent. Fails with [AuthFailureReason.invalidCredentials] for a token
  /// that is no test token of the issuer of the credential, one whose nonce
  /// is not the rawNonce of the credential, and one whose nonce the fixture
  /// took already.
  _Identity _take(SignInCredential credential) {
    if (_refused[credential] case final kept?) {
      _refused[credential] = null;
      return kept;
    }
    final (issuer, idToken, rawNonce) = switch (credential) {
      IdTokenCredential(:final issuer, :final idToken, :final rawNonce) => (
        issuer,
        idToken,
        rawNonce,
      ),
    };
    final parts = idToken.split('.');
    final payload = parts.length == 3 ? _payloadOf(parts[1]) : null;
    if (payload == null ||
        payload['iss'] != issuer ||
        payload['sub'] is! String ||
        payload['email'] is! String? ||
        payload['nonce'] is! String? ||
        payload['nonce'] != rawNonce) {
      throw AuthFailure(
        AuthFailureReason.invalidCredentials,
        developerHint:
            'The fixture sign-in takes a test token, a JSON Web '
            'Token whose payload has iss, the issuer of the credential, sub, '
            'and maybe nonce, the rawNonce of the credential, which '
            '$credential does not have.',
      );
    }
    if (payload['nonce'] case final String nonce
        when !_spentNonces.add(nonce)) {
      throw AuthFailure(
        AuthFailureReason.invalidCredentials,
        developerHint:
            'The fixture sign-in took the token of $credential '
            'already, and takes a token with a nonce once.',
      );
    }
    return (
      issuer: issuer,
      subject: payload['sub']! as String,
      email: payload['email'] as String?,
    );
  }

  /// The payload of a JSON Web Token as an object, from [part], the second
  /// part of the token, or `null` if it is none.
  static Map<String, Object?>? _payloadOf(String part) {
    try {
      final json = utf8.decode(base64Url.decode(base64Url.normalize(part)));
      return switch (jsonDecode(json)) {
        final Map<String, Object?> payload => payload,
        _ => null,
      };
    } on FormatException {
      return null;
    }
  }

  @override
  AuthUser? get currentUser => _user;

  @override
  Stream<AuthUser?> get userChanges => _changes.stream;

  @override
  Future<void> signIn({required String email, required String password}) async {
    await _answer();
    final uid = _ownerOf(email);
    if (uid == null || _accounts[uid]!.password != password) {
      throw const AuthFailure(AuthFailureReason.invalidCredentials);
    }
    _set(_userOf(uid));
  }

  @override
  Future<void> signUp({required String email, required String password}) async {
    await _answer();
    _checkFree(email);
    final uid = _newUid();
    _accounts[uid] = (email: email, password: password, issuers: {});
    _set(_userOf(uid));
  }

  @override
  Future<void> linkPassword({
    required String email,
    required String password,
  }) async {
    await _answer();
    final user = _signedIn();
    if (!user.isAnonymous) {
      throw const AuthFailure(
        AuthFailureReason.unknown,
        developerHint: 'The user who is signed in has an account already.',
      );
    }
    _checkFree(email);
    // The anonymous user keeps the id.
    final uid = user.uid;
    _accounts[uid] = (email: email, password: password, issuers: {});
    _set(_userOf(uid));
  }

  /// Signs in to the user of the identity of [credential], and creates that
  /// user when the identity is new, unless the address of the identity has
  /// an account of another user.
  @override
  Future<void> signInWith(SignInCredential credential) async {
    await _answer();
    final identity = _take(credential);
    final key = (identity.issuer, identity.subject);
    if (_identities[key] case final uid?) {
      _set(_userOf(uid));
      return;
    }
    if (identity.email case final email? when _ownerOf(email) != null) {
      throw const AuthFailure(AuthFailureReason.accountExistsWithOtherMethod);
    }
    final uid = _newUid();
    _accounts[uid] = (
      email: identity.email,
      password: null,
      issuers: {identity.issuer},
    );
    _identities[key] = uid;
    _set(_userOf(uid));
  }

  @override
  Future<void> linkWith(SignInCredential credential) async {
    await _answer();
    final user = _signedIn();
    final identity = _take(credential);
    final key = (identity.issuer, identity.subject);
    if (_identities[key] case final owner?) {
      if (owner == user.uid) {
        throw const AuthFailure(
          AuthFailureReason.unknown,
          developerHint: 'The user who is signed in has the identity already.',
        );
      }
      // The token is spent, so the sign-in that follows takes what the
      // fixture keeps for the credential object.
      _refused[credential] = identity;
      throw const AuthFailure(AuthFailureReason.credentialInUse);
    }
    if (identity.email case final email?
        when (_ownerOf(email) ?? user.uid) != user.uid) {
      _refused[credential] = identity;
      throw const AuthFailure(AuthFailureReason.accountExistsWithOtherMethod);
    }
    final account = _accounts.remove(user.uid);
    // The user who is signed in keeps the id with the identity.
    final uid = user.uid;
    _accounts[uid] = (
      email: account?.email ?? identity.email,
      password: account?.password,
      issuers: {...?account?.issuers, identity.issuer},
    );
    _identities[key] = uid;
    _set(_userOf(uid));
  }

  @override
  Future<void> signInAnonymously() async {
    await _answer();
    _set(AuthUser(uid: _newUid(), isAnonymous: true));
  }

  @override
  Future<void> sendPasswordReset(String email) => _answer();

  @override
  Future<void> signOut() async {
    await _answer();
    _set(null);
  }

  /// An anonymous user has nothing to sign in with again, so the service
  /// deletes one whenever it is asked to. It deletes the user of an account
  /// only if that user signed in through this service, since the app
  /// started, or with [fresh], a credential of an identity of the user,
  /// which signs the user in again. A credential of another identity fails
  /// with [AuthFailureReason.invalidCredentials] and deletes nothing.
  @override
  Future<void> deleteAccount({SignInCredential? fresh}) async {
    await _answer();
    final user = _signedIn();
    if (fresh != null) {
      final identity = _take(fresh);
      if (_identities[(identity.issuer, identity.subject)] != user.uid) {
        throw const AuthFailure(
          AuthFailureReason.invalidCredentials,
          developerHint:
              'The credential is not of the user who is signed '
              'in.',
        );
      }
      _signedInHere = true;
    }
    if (!user.isAnonymous && !_signedInHere) {
      throw const AuthFailure(AuthFailureReason.recentSignInRequired);
    }
    _accounts.remove(user.uid);
    _identities.removeWhere((identity, uid) => uid == user.uid);
    _set(null);
  }

  @override
  Future<String?> userToken({bool forceRefresh = false}) async {
    await _answer();
    final user = _user;
    if (user == null) return null;
    final uid = user.uid;
    final turn = _tokens[uid] ?? 1;
    _tokens[uid] = forceRefresh ? turn + 1 : turn;
    // As a provider that tells of each change of the token, of the same user.
    if (forceRefresh) _changes.add(user);
    return 'fixture-token-$uid-${_tokens[uid]}';
  }
}

/// Delivers events to the listeners of their type through one stream.
