=== lib/core/fixture_notes/fixture_notes.dart (fake_rest_user) ===
import 'package:flutter/foundation.dart';

import '../rest/app_rest.dart';

/// A note of the server of the fixture.
final class FixtureNote {
  /// Creates the note.
  const FixtureNote({required this.id, required this.text});

  /// The note in the JSON of the server.
  factory FixtureNote.fromJson(Map<String, dynamic> json) =>
      FixtureNote(id: json['id'] as int, text: json['text'] as String);

  /// The id that the server gave the note.
  final int id;

  /// The text of the note.
  final String text;
}

/// What a screen shows of the notes.
sealed class FixtureNotesView {
  const FixtureNotesView();
}

/// The notes are on their way.
final class FixtureNotesLoading extends FixtureNotesView {
  /// Creates the view.
  const FixtureNotesLoading();
}

/// The notes that the server has.
final class FixtureNotesShown extends FixtureNotesView {
  /// Creates the view.
  const FixtureNotesShown(this.notes);

  /// The notes.
  final List<FixtureNote> notes;
}

/// A call failed, for which a screen shows a text of its own.
final class FixtureNotesFailed extends FixtureNotesView {
  /// Creates the view.
  const FixtureNotesFailed(this.reason);

  /// Why the call failed.
  final RestFailureReason reason;
}

/// The state of a screen with the notes of a server, as the state of a screen
/// uses the REST client of the app: it knows the role and no provider of it,
/// and cancels its call when it is disposed of.
final class FixtureNotes extends ValueNotifier<FixtureNotesView> {
  /// Creates the state.
  FixtureNotes() : super(const FixtureNotesLoading());

  RestCancel? _load;

  /// Loads the notes.
  Future<void> load() async {
    _load?.cancel();
    final cancel = _load = RestCancel();
    value = const FixtureNotesLoading();
    await _ending(cancel, () async {
      final response = await appRest.get('notes', cancel: cancel);
      return FixtureNotesShown(response.decoded(_notesOf));
    });
  }

  /// Adds a note with [text], and shows it after the notes that are shown.
  Future<void> add(String text) async {
    final before = value;
    final cancel = _load = RestCancel();
    await _ending(cancel, () async {
      final response = await appRest.post(
        'notes',
        json: {'text': text},
        cancel: cancel,
      );
      return FixtureNotesShown([
        if (before is FixtureNotesShown) ...before.notes,
        response.decoded(FixtureNote.fromJson),
      ]);
    });
  }

  /// Shows what [call] ends with, unless [cancel] ended it.
  Future<void> _ending(
    RestCancel cancel,
    Future<FixtureNotesView> Function() call,
  ) async {
    FixtureNotesView view;
    try {
      view = await call();
    } on RestFailure catch (failure) {
      view = FixtureNotesFailed(failure.reason);
    }
    if (!cancel.isCancelled) value = view;
  }

  static List<FixtureNote> _notesOf(List<dynamic> json) => [
    for (final item in json) FixtureNote.fromJson(item as Map<String, dynamic>),
  ];

  @override
  void dispose() {
    _load?.cancel();
    super.dispose();
  }
}
=== lib/core/fixture_notifications/fixture_notifications.dart (fake_notifications) ===
import '../notifications/notifications_service.dart';

/// Whether the app may show notifications on this device. It stands for the
/// settings of the system, which every start of the app finds as the last
/// one left them: at first the app may not, as on a first launch.
bool _enabled = false;

/// The notifications that are scheduled on this device, by their ids. They
/// stand for the alarms of the system, which outlive a start of the app.
final Map<int, AppNotification> _scheduled = {};

/// Waits for the answer of the system, which takes a moment, as with a
/// provider that asks it: a turn of the event loop. So a test makes the
/// calls of the notifications in real time, as it runs the start-up of the
/// app.
Future<void> _answer() => Future<void>.delayed(Duration.zero);

/// Opens the notifications of the fixture, as a start of the app does: it
/// asks the system for what it keeps, which takes a moment, and returns the
/// service. It asks the user nothing.
///
/// A start keeps whether the app may show notifications and the
/// notifications that are scheduled. Nothing outlives the process of the
/// app, so each run of the tests of a file starts as on a first launch.
Future<NotificationsService> openFixtureNotifications() async {
  await _answer();
  return FixtureNotifications._();
}

/// Notifications in memory, on a device whose system has no question for
/// the permission to show them, as Android before 13: [request] opens the
/// settings of the system for the notifications of the app instead, as the
/// notifications role asks of a provider. The user of the tests turns the
/// notifications on there, as the mocks of a provider grant the permission
/// when the app asks, so one request turns them on: through the role, that
/// looks like a question that the user allows.
///
/// The device shows nothing and has no user who taps a notification, so
/// the service tells of no tap.
final class FixtureNotifications implements NotificationsService {
  FixtureNotifications._();

  @override
  NotificationTap? get launchTap => null;

  @override
  Stream<NotificationTap> get taps => const Stream.empty();

  @override
  Future<bool> areEnabled() async {
    await _answer();
    return _enabled;
  }

  /// Opens the settings of the system, since the system has no question to

  @override

    await _answer();

  /// The user of the tests turns the notifications of the app on in the

  @override

    await _answer();

  @override

    await _answer();
    _scheduled.remove(notification.id);
  }

  @override

    await _answer();
    _scheduled[notification.id] = notification;

  @override

    await _answer();
    _scheduled.remove(id);
  }

  @override
  Future<void> cancelAll() async {
    await _answer();
    _scheduled.clear();
  }

  @override

    await _answer();

import '../preferences/app_preferences.dart';

/// What the preferences of the fixture have saved: it stands for the disk
/// of a device, which outlives the preferences that a start of the app
/// opens. A list on it is a copy of the list that was saved.
final Map<String, Object> fixturePreferencesDisk = {};

/// Creates the preferences of the fixture, which read
/// [fixturePreferencesDisk] into memory, as a start of the app does.
AppPreferences createFixturePreferences() => FixturePreferences();

/// The preferences of the fixture: the settings of the app that are no
/// secret, in memory, over [fixturePreferencesDisk]. Nothing here is
/// encrypted: never store a token, a password, an API key or an encryption
/// key in them.
final class FixturePreferences implements AppPreferences {
  /// Reads [fixturePreferencesDisk] into memory.
  FixturePreferences() {
    for (final MapEntry(:key, :value) in fixturePreferencesDisk.entries) {
      _values[key] = _copyOf(value);
    }
  }

  final Map<String, Object> _values = {};

  @override
  String? getString(String key) => _read<String>(key);

  @override
  bool? getBool(String key) => _read<bool>(key);

  @override
  int? getInt(String key) => _read<int>(key);

  @override
  double? getDouble(String key) => _read<double>(key);

  @override
  List<String>? getStringList(String key) => switch (_values[key]) {
    final List<String> list => List.of(list),
    _ => null,
  };

  @override
  Future<void> setString(String key, String value) => _save(key, value);

  @override
  Future<void> setBool(String key, bool value) => _save(key, value);

  @override
  Future<void> setInt(String key, int value) => _save(key, value);

  @override
  Future<void> setDouble(String key, double value) => _save(key, value);

  @override
  Future<void> setStringList(String key, List<String> value) =>
      _save(key, List.of(value));

  @override
  Future<void> remove(String key) async {
    _values.remove(key);
    fixturePreferencesDisk.remove(key);
  }

  /// The value of [key] in memory if it is a [T], or `null`.
  T? _read<T>(String key) => switch (_values[key]) {
    final T value => value,
    _ => null,
  };

  /// Keeps [value] under [key] in memory, and saves it to the disk.
  Future<void> _save(String key, Object value) async {
    _values[key] = value;
    fixturePreferencesDisk[key] = _copyOf(value);
  }

  /// [value], or a copy of it if it is a list.
  static Object _copyOf(Object value) =>
      value is List<String> ? List.of(value) : value;
}
=== lib/core/fixture_purchases/fixture_purchases.dart (fake_purchases) ===
import 'dart:async';

import '../purchases/purchases_service.dart';

/// A purchase of a plan: when the user bought it, and whether its first
/// period was the free trial of the plan.
typedef _Purchase = ({DateTime at, bool inTrial});

/// The id of the plan that renews each month, with a free week for a user
/// who never bought it.
const _monthlyId = 'fixture.monthly';

/// The id of the plan that renews each year.
const _annualId = 'fixture.annual';

/// The id of the plan that the user pays for once, for good.
const _lifetimeId = 'fixture.lifetime';

/// The free week of the plan of [_monthlyId].
const _freeWeek = IntroOffer(
  price: r'$0.00',
  amount: 0,
  period: BillingPeriod(1, BillingUnit.week),
);

/// The plans of the offer of the fixture, in its order, without the free
/// week that only some users may take.
const _plans = [
  Plan(
    id: _monthlyId,
    title: 'Monthly (fixture)',
    price: r'$4.99',
    amount: 4.99,
    currencyCode: 'USD',
    period: BillingPeriod(1, BillingUnit.month),
  ),
  Plan(
    id: _annualId,
    title: 'Annual (fixture)',
    price: r'$39.99',
    amount: 39.99,
    currencyCode: 'USD',
    period: BillingPeriod(1, BillingUnit.year),
  ),
  Plan(
    id: _lifetimeId,
    title: 'Lifetime (fixture)',
    price: r'$99.99',
    amount: 99.99,
    currencyCode: 'USD',
  ),
];

/// The page of the store of the fixture where a user manages a plan that
/// renews.
final Uri _manageUrl = Uri.parse('https://store.fixture.example/manage');

/// What each user bought: the purchase of each plan by its id, by the key
/// of the user, which is the id of a user of the app or that of a user of
/// this device ([_deviceUser]). It stands for the backend of a provider,
/// which every start of the app finds as the last one left it.
final Map<String, Map<String, _Purchase>> _bought = {};

/// The purchases that were made on this device, by the id of their plans:
/// what the store account of the device bought, which a restore gives the
/// user.
final Map<String, _Purchase> _boughtOnDevice = {};

/// The ids of users of the app that an identification named, on any
/// device: the fixture has one.
final Set<String> _named = {};

/// The key of the user whom this device has, the user whose purchases
/// count: the one that was identified last, also in an earlier start of the
/// app, or `null` before the first identification.
String? _onDevice;

/// How many users of this device the fixture started.
int _deviceUsers = 0;

/// The start of the key of a user of this device, which no id of a user of
/// the app has.
const _devicePrefix = r'$fixture-device:';

/// The key of the [number]th user of this device.
String _deviceUser(int number) => '$_devicePrefix$number';

/// Waits for the answer of the store or of the backend, which takes a
/// moment, as with a provider that asks them: a turn of the event loop. So
/// a test makes the calls of purchases in real time, as it runs the
/// start-up of the app.
Future<void> _answer() => Future<void>.delayed(Duration.zero);

/// The time now, in UTC.
DateTime _now() => DateTime.now().toUtc();

/// Opens the purchases of the fixture, as a start of the app does: it asks
/// the store for what it keeps, which takes a moment, and returns the
/// service, without a user. It asks the user nothing.
///
/// A start keeps what each user bought and the user whom the device has.
/// Nothing outlives the process of the app, so each run of the tests of a
/// file starts as on a first install.
Future<PurchasesService> openFixturePurchases() async {
  await _answer();
  return FixturePurchases._();
}

/// Purchases in memory, with an offer of three plans: one that renews each
/// month, with a free week for a user who never bought it, one that renews
/// each year, and one that the user pays for once. A purchase completes at
/// once, with no sheet of a store, and nothing expires while a test runs.
///
/// It keeps the purchases of each user of the app as the purchases role
/// says: `identify(null)` after another user starts a new user of this
/// device with nothing bought; a user that no identification named before,
/// identified while the user of this device is identified, takes over the
/// purchases of the user of this device; and a restore gives the user who
/// is identified each purchase made on this device, which the other users
/// keep too. No server grants an access, so the service tells of a change
/// only when a purchase or a restore made one.
final class FixturePurchases implements PurchasesService {
  FixturePurchases._();

  final StreamController<AppAccess> _changes =
      StreamController<AppAccess>.broadcast();

  /// Whether [identify] was called, which the role asks before any other
  /// call.
  bool _identified = false;

  /// What [user] bought: the purchase of each plan by its id.
  Map<String, _Purchase> _of(String user) =>
      _bought.putIfAbsent(user, () => {});

  /// The key of the user whose purchases count. Throws a [StateError] before
  /// [identify], as the role asks every provider to be identified first.
  String _user() {
    if (!_identified) {
      throw StateError('The purchases of the fixture were not identified.');
    }
    return _onDevice!;
  }

  @override
  Future<void> identify(String? userId) async {
    await _answer();
    _identified = true;
    final onDevice = _onDevice;
    final ofDevice = onDevice != null && onDevice.startsWith(_devicePrefix);
    if (userId == null) {
      // The user of this device stays, also from an earlier start; after a
      // user of the app, a new one starts, with nothing bought.
      if (!ofDevice) _onDevice = _deviceUser(++_deviceUsers);
    } else if (userId != onDevice) {
      // A user that no identification named before takes over the
      // purchases of the user of this device, who no longer has them.
      if (ofDevice && !_named.contains(userId)) {
        _bought[userId] = _bought.remove(onDevice) ?? {};
      }
      _named.add(userId);
      _onDevice = userId;
    }
  }

  @override
  Future<AppAccess> access() async {
    await _answer();
    return _accessOf(_of(_user()));
  }

  @override
  Stream<AppAccess> get accessChanges => _changes.stream;

  @override
  Future<List<Plan>> plans() async {
    await _answer();
    final purchases = _of(_user());
    return [for (final plan in _plans) _offered(plan, purchases)];
  }

  @override
  Future<AppAccess?> purchase(Plan plan) async {
    await _answer();
    final purchases = _of(_user());
    final offered = _plans.where((other) => other.id == plan.id).firstOrNull;
    if (offered == null) {
      throw PurchasesFailure(
        PurchasesFailureReason.unavailable,
        developerHint: 'The fixture has no plan ${plan.id}.',
      );
    }
    if (_isActive(offered, purchases[offered.id])) {
      throw const PurchasesFailure(PurchasesFailureReason.alreadyOwned);
    }
    final purchase = (
      at: _now(),
      inTrial: _offered(offered, purchases).introOffer?.isFree ?? false,
    );
    purchases[offered.id] = purchase;
    _boughtOnDevice[offered.id] = purchase;
    return _told(purchases);
  }

  @override
  Future<AppAccess> restore() async {
    await _answer();
    final purchases = _of(_user());
    for (final MapEntry(key: id, value: purchase) in _boughtOnDevice.entries) {
      final kept = purchases[id];
      if (kept == null || purchase.at.isAfter(kept.at)) {
        purchases[id] = purchase;
      }
    }
    return _told(purchases);
  }

  /// The access of [purchases], which the service tells of.
  AppAccess _told(Map<String, _Purchase> purchases) {
    final access = _accessOf(purchases);
    _changes.add(access);
    return access;
  }
}

/// [plan] as the user with [purchases] gets it: the plan of [_monthlyId]
/// with its free week for a user who never bought it.
Plan _offered(Plan plan, Map<String, _Purchase> purchases) =>
    plan.id == _monthlyId && !purchases.containsKey(_monthlyId)
    ? Plan(
        id: plan.id,
        title: plan.title,
        price: plan.price,
        amount: plan.amount,
        currencyCode: plan.currencyCode,
        period: plan.period,
        introOffer: _freeWeek,
      )
    : plan;

/// Whether [purchase] of [plan] gives access now: that of a plan paid
/// once does for good.
bool _isActive(Plan plan, _Purchase? purchase) {
  if (purchase == null) return false;
  final ends = _endOf(plan, purchase);
  return ends == null || ends.isAfter(_now());
}

/// When the access of [purchase] of [plan] ends, or `null` for a plan that
/// the user pays for once, which never ends.
DateTime? _endOf(Plan plan, _Purchase purchase) {
  final at = purchase.at;
  if (purchase.inTrial) return at.add(const Duration(days: 7));
  return switch (plan.period) {
    null => null,
    BillingPeriod(:final count, unit: BillingUnit.day) => at.add(
      Duration(days: count),
    ),
    BillingPeriod(:final count, unit: BillingUnit.week) => at.add(
      Duration(days: 7 * count),
    ),
    BillingPeriod(:final count, unit: BillingUnit.month) => DateTime.utc(
      at.year,
      at.month + count,
      at.day,
      at.hour,
      at.minute,
      at.second,
      at.millisecond,
      at.microsecond,
    ),
    BillingPeriod(:final count, unit: BillingUnit.year) => DateTime.utc(
      at.year + count,
      at.month,
      at.day,
      at.hour,
      at.minute,
      at.second,
      at.millisecond,
      at.microsecond,
    ),
  };
}

/// The access that [purchases] give: premium access for good with the plan
/// paid once, and otherwise that of the plan whose access ends last, while
/// it has not ended.
AppAccess _accessOf(Map<String, _Purchase> purchases) {
  if (purchases.containsKey(_lifetimeId)) return const PremiumAccess();
  PremiumAccess? access;
  for (final plan in _plans) {
    final purchase = purchases[plan.id];
    if (purchase == null || !_isActive(plan, purchase)) continue;
    final ends = _endOf(plan, purchase)!;
    if (access == null || ends.isAfter(access.expiresAt!)) {
      access = PremiumAccess(
        expiresAt: ends,
        renews: true,
        inTrial: purchase.inTrial,
        period: plan.period,
        manageUrl: _manageUrl,
      );
    }
  }
  return access ?? const NoAccess();
}

import 'dart:async';

import '../push/push_service.dart';

/// The address of this device for push messages, or `null` while the
/// device has none. It stands for the registration of the device with the
/// service of push messages, which every start of the app finds as the last
/// one left it.
String? _address;

/// How many addresses the service of push messages gave this device, so
/// that each one differs from the others.
int _given = 0;

/// The registration of the device while it is on its way.
Future<void>? _registration;

/// Tells the app of the address that the device gets: it stands for the
/// platform, so it outlives a start of the app.
final StreamController<String> _addresses =
    StreamController<String>.broadcast();

/// Waits for the answer of the system, which takes a moment, as with a
/// provider that asks it: a turn of the event loop. So a test makes the
/// calls of the push messages in real time, as it runs the start-up of the
/// app.
Future<void> _answer() => Future<void>.delayed(Duration.zero);

/// Opens the push messages of the fixture, as a start of the app does: it
/// asks the system for what it keeps, which takes a moment, and returns the
/// service. It asks the user nothing.
///
/// The first start registers the device, which gets its address a while
/// later, as an iPhone gets the token of APNs: the service answers `null`
/// until then, and tells of the address on its address changes. A later
/// start finds the address that the device kept. Nothing outlives the

/// first launch.
Future<PushService> openFixturePush() async {
  await _answer();

  return FixturePush._();
}

/// while, and tells the app of the address that the device got.
Future<void> _register() async {
  await Future<void>.delayed(const Duration(milliseconds: 50));
  final address = _address = 'fixture-device-${++_given}';
  _registration = null;
  _addresses.add(address);
}

/// Push messages in memory, on a device that shows nothing itself, so the

    return _address;

  @override

  @override
  Stream<PushMessage> get messages => const Stream.empty();

  @override
  Stream<PushMessage> get taps => const Stream.empty();

  @override
  Future<PushMessage?> launchMessage() async {
    await _answer();
    return null;
  }
}
=== lib/core/fixture_rest/fixture_rest_transport.dart (fake_rest) ===
import 'dart:async';
import 'dart:io';
import 'dart:typed_data';

import '../rest/rest_transport.dart';

/// Creates the transport of the fixture.
RestTransport createFixtureRestTransport() => const FixtureRestTransport();

/// One exchange on `HttpClient` of `dart:io`, with nothing but what the
/// contract of [RestTransport] asks.
final class FixtureRestTransport implements RestTransport {
  /// Creates the transport.
  const FixtureRestTransport();

  @override
  Future<RestReply> send(RestExchange exchange) async {
    // A client for each exchange, so nothing stays between two calls.
    final client = HttpClient();
    HttpClientRequest? opened;
    unawaited(
      exchange.stop.then((_) {
        opened?.abort();
        // This also closes a connection that is being made, or that is in the
        // middle of the body of a reply, which a request can no longer abort.
        client.close(force: true);
      }),
    );
    try {
      final request = opened = await client.openUrl(
        exchange.method,
        exchange.uri,
      );
      request.followRedirects = false;
      exchange.headers.forEach(request.headers.set);
      if (exchange.body case final body?) {
        request.add(Uint8List.fromList(body));
      }
      final response = await request.close();
      final body = await response.fold(
        BytesBuilder(copy: false),
        (all, part) => all..add(part),
      );
      final headers = <String, String>{};
      response.headers.forEach(
        (name, values) => headers[name] = values.join(', '),
      );
      return RestReply(
        status: response.statusCode,
        headers: headers,
        bytes: body.takeBytes(),
      );
    } on IOException catch (error) {
      // SocketException, HttpException and TlsException. Their text may quote
      // the address with its query, so the hint has the type alone.
      throw RestConnectionFailed(
        'The connection failed with a ${error.runtimeType}.',
      );
    } finally {
      client.close();
    }
  }
}

=== lib/core/fixture_setting/fixture_setting.dart (fake_preferences_user) ===
import '../preferences/app_preferences.dart';

/// The setting of the fixture: a number that the app remembers between its
/// launches when it has preferences.
final fixtureSetting = FixtureSetting();

/// A number, 0 until something sets it.
final class FixtureSetting {
  int _value = 0;

  /// The preferences that remember the number, once the app opened them.
  AppPreferences? _preferences;

  /// The number.
  int get value => _value;

  /// Sets the number to [value]. In an app with preferences, it saves the
  /// number too, once the app opened them: before that, it changes only
  /// memory.
  Future<void> set(int value) async {
    _value = value;
    await _preferences?.setInt(fixtureSettingKey, value);
  }
}

/// The key of the setting of the fixture in the preferences.
const fixtureSettingKey = 'fake_preferences_user.setting';

/// What the restorers of the fixture read each time the app gave them the
/// preferences, in the order they ran: the name of each and the number
/// that was saved, or `null`.
final List<(String, int?)> fixtureRestored = [];

/// a test that a restorer that throws keeps no other from restoring sets

/// that cannot read what is saved.
bool fixtureRestorersThrow = false;

/// The first restorer of the fixture, which only notes in [fixtureRestored]

void noteFixtureSetting(AppPreferences preferences) {
  fixtureRestored.add(('note', preferences.getInt(fixtureSettingKey)));
  if (fixtureRestorersThrow) {
    throw StateError('The first restorer of the fixture throws.');
  }
}

/// The restorer of the setting of the fixture: takes the number that is
/// saved, or keeps the current one when nothing is saved, and keeps
/// [preferences] for the writes of the setting. It notes in
/// [fixtureRestored] what is saved, and then throws an exception if
/// [fixtureRestorersThrow].
void restoreFixtureSetting(AppPreferences preferences) {

  fixtureSetting
    .._value = saved ?? fixtureSetting._value
    .._preferences = preferences;
  fixtureRestored.add(('setting', saved));
  if (fixtureRestorersThrow) {
    throw const FormatException(
      'The restorer of the setting of the fixture throws.',

  }

import 'dart:convert';

import 'package:flutter/material.dart';

import '../auth/app_session.dart';

/// The issuer of the tokens of the identity provider of the fixture.
const String fixtureIssuer = 'https://accounts.fixture.example.com';

/// The subject of the identity of the user at the identity provider of the
/// fixture, which every credential of [fixtureCredential] is of.
const String fixtureSubject = 'fixture-identity';

/// The email address of that identity.
const String fixtureEmail = 'identity@sign-in.fixture.example.com';

/// How many credentials [fixtureCredential] returned, which gives each a
/// nonce and an authorization code of its own.
int _credentials = 0;

/// Asks the identity provider of the fixture for a credential, as the sheet
/// of an identity provider does, and returns it a turn of the event loop
/// later: a credential of the one identity of the fixture, each time with a
/// new nonce and a new authorization code, as from an identity provider
/// that revokes the tokens of an account that is deleted.
///
/// Its token is a test token, a JSON Web Token without a signature whose
/// payload has the issuer, the subject and the email address of the
/// identity, and the nonce, which is the rawNonce of the credential itself.
/// A provider of sign-in in the tests of an app takes such a token once.
Future<SignInCredential?> fixtureCredential() async {
  await Future<void>.delayed(Duration.zero);
  final number = ++_credentials;
  final nonce = 'fixture-nonce-$number';
  String part(Map<String, Object?> json) =>
      base64Url.encode(utf8.encode(jsonEncode(json))).replaceAll('=', '');
  final header = part({'alg': 'none', 'typ': 'JWT'});
  final payload = part({
    'iss': fixtureIssuer,
    'sub': fixtureSubject,
    'email': fixtureEmail,
    'nonce': nonce,
  });
  return IdTokenCredential(
    issuer: fixtureIssuer,
    idToken: '$header.$payload.',
    rawNonce: nonce,
    authorizationCode: 'fixture-code-$number',
  );
}

/// The steps of the onboarding of the fixture whose action ran, in the

/// The action of the step `ask`: notes its call in [fixtureStepCalls],

/// onboarding: with a route without a page on the navigator at the root of

=== lib/core/l10n/app_locale.dart (role:localization) ===
import 'package:flutter/widgets.dart';

import '../preferences/app_preferences.dart';

/// The languages of the app. The app shows its texts in the language that
/// the device prefers among them, and in the first of the list when the
/// device asks for none of them.
///
/// A language has one locale here. The app tells its languages apart by
/// their codes alone, so of two locales of one language, such as
/// `Locale('pt', 'BR')` and `Locale('pt', 'PT')`, `appLocale.choose()`
/// takes the first, whichever of them it is given.
const appLocales = <Locale>[Locale('en'), Locale('uk')];

/// The key of the preferences of the app under which the app saves the
/// language that the user chose, as the code of the language, such as `uk`.
/// Nothing is saved under it while the app follows the languages of the
/// device.
const _key = 'localization.locale';

/// The language that the user chose for the app, which the app remembers
/// between its launches.
///
/// The root of the app reads it through [AppLocaleScope], and rebuilds in
/// the new language when the choice changes.
final appLocale = AppLocaleController._();

/// Keeps the language that the user chose for the app, and saves each
/// choice in the preferences of the app.
final class AppLocaleController extends ChangeNotifier {
  AppLocaleController._();

  Locale? _locale;

  /// The preferences of the app, once it opened them.
  AppPreferences? _preferences;

  /// The language that the user chose, one of [appLocales], or `null` while
  /// the app follows the languages of the device.
  Locale? get value => _locale;

  /// Chooses the language of [locale] as the language of the app, or with
  /// `null` lets the app follow the languages of the device again.
  ///
  /// The choice is the one of [appLocales] with the language of [locale],
  /// so `Locale('uk', 'UA')` chooses `Locale('uk')`. A locale of a language
  /// that the app is not in is refused: the future completes with an
  /// [ArgumentError], and nothing changes.
  ///
  /// The language of the app changes at once, and the choice is then saved
  /// in the preferences once the app opened them: before that, it changes
  /// only memory. When saving fails, the future completes with the error of
  /// the preferences. The app stays in the new language while it runs, and
  /// its next launch starts with what was saved before; choosing the
  /// language again saves it again.
  Future<void> choose(Locale? locale) async {
    final chosen = locale == null ? null : _ofApp(locale.languageCode);
    if (locale != null && chosen == null) {
      throw ArgumentError.value(
        '$locale',
        'locale',
        'The app is not in the language of this locale',
      );
    }
    _set(chosen);
    final preferences = _preferences;
    if (preferences == null) return;
    if (chosen == null) {
      await preferences.remove(_key);
    } else {
      await preferences.setString(_key, chosen.languageCode);
    }
  }

  void _set(Locale? locale) {
    if (locale == _locale) return;
    _locale = locale;
    notifyListeners();
  }
}

/// The one of [appLocales] with the language of the code [code], or `null`.
Locale? _ofApp(String? code) {
  for (final locale in appLocales) {
    if (locale.languageCode == code) return locale;
  }
  return null;
}

/// Restores the language that the user chose from [preferences], which the
/// app opened, and keeps them for the choices to come: a restorer of the
/// preferences, which the app calls before its first frame.
///
/// When nothing is saved, or what is saved is not the code of one of
/// [appLocales], the choice stays as it is.
void restoreAppLocale(AppPreferences preferences) {
  appLocale._preferences = preferences;
  final saved = _ofApp(preferences.getString(_key));
  if (saved != null) appLocale._set(saved);
}

/// Gives the root of the app the language that the user chose, and rebuilds
/// the root when the choice changes.
class AppLocaleScope extends InheritedNotifier<AppLocaleController> {
  /// Creates the scope of [notifier] around [child].
  const AppLocaleScope({
    required AppLocaleController super.notifier,
    required super.child,
    super.key,
  });

  /// The language that the user chose, or `null` to follow the device; the
  /// widget of [context] rebuilds when the choice changes.
  static Locale? of(BuildContext context) => context
      .dependOnInheritedWidgetOfExactType<AppLocaleScope>()!
      .notifier!
      .value;
}

=== lib/core/notifications/app_notifications.dart (role:notifications) ===
import 'dart:async';

import 'package:fixture_app/core/fixture_notifications/fixture_notifications.dart'
    as impl0;
import 'package:flutter/foundation.dart';
import 'package:flutter/widgets.dart';

import '../preferences/app_preferences.dart';
import 'notifications_service.dart';

export 'notifications_service.dart' show AppNotification, NotificationTap;

/// Whether the app may show notifications.
enum NotificationPermission {
  /// The user allowed it.
  granted,

  /// The app asked, and the user did not allow it or turned the
  /// notifications of the app off later. The system may ask no more: iOS
  /// asks once, and Android after a second no. So the app opens its
  /// settings, where the user turns the notifications on:
  /// [NotificationsController.openSettings].
  denied,

  /// The app may not show notifications, and has not asked yet.
  notAsked,
}

/// The key under which a notification with an owner names its owner in its
/// [AppNotification.data], such as a push message that the app shows itself.
///
/// [NotificationsController.taps] and [NotificationsController.launchTap]
/// leave out the taps on such a notification, which
/// [NotificationsController.tapsOf] and
/// [NotificationsController.launchTapOf] give to the owner. A notification
/// with an owner has a negative id, so that it never takes the place of a
/// notification of the app.
const String notificationOwnerKey = 'notifications.owner';

/// The notifications of the app: the permission to show them, the
/// notifications that the app shows now or at a time to come, and the taps
/// of the user on them.
final appNotifications = NotificationsController._();

/// Shows the notifications of the app through the service of the provider
/// of notifications, keeps the permission to show them up to date, and
/// tells of the taps on them. The app has one, [appNotifications].
///
/// A call made before the app started the service waits for it, and fails
/// with the error of that start if it fails.
///
/// What the service throws reaches the code that called. An error of the
/// service when the user comes back to the app, or one that its taps send,
/// has no caller, so it reaches the handlers of the uncaught errors of the
/// app, and the controller goes on.
final class NotificationsController {
  NotificationsController._();

  /// The key under which the preferences remember that the app asked for
  /// the permission.
  static const _askedKey = 'notifications.asked';

  /// The smallest and the largest id of a notification: the platforms keep
  /// it in 32 bits.
  static const _smallestId = -0x80000000;
  static const _largestId = 0x7FFFFFFF;

  NotificationsService? _service;
  StreamSubscription<NotificationTap>? _serviceTaps;
  NotificationTap? _launchTap;

  /// Whether the app has started for the first time, also when that start
  /// failed.
  bool _started = false;

  /// What the calls made before the first start wait for, each created by
  /// its call. The first start completes them, with its error if it failed,
  /// each in the zone of its call: created with the controller, they would
  /// complete in a microtask of the zone that read [appNotifications]
  /// first, which may run none any more, as the fake time of a widget test
  /// that is over before a later test starts the app in real time.
  final List<Completer<void>> _untilStarted = [];

  /// The preferences that remember that the app asked, once the app opened
  /// them.
  AppPreferences? _preferences;

  /// Whether the app asked for the permission.
  bool _asked = false;

  /// Whether the service last said that the app may show notifications.
  bool _enabled = false;

  /// The request for the permission that is on its way, if any.
  Future<NotificationPermission>? _request;

  /// Tells when the user comes back to the app.
  _ResumeObserver? _observer;

  final StreamController<NotificationTap> _taps =
      StreamController<NotificationTap>.broadcast();

  final ValueNotifier<NotificationPermission> _permission = ValueNotifier(
    NotificationPermission.notAsked,
  );

  /// Whether the app may show notifications. It follows what the user
  /// answers, and what the user changed in the settings of the system once
  /// the user comes back to the app. A widget follows it with a
  /// `ValueListenableBuilder`.
  ValueListenable<NotificationPermission> get permission => _permission;

  /// The tap on a notification of the app that launched the app, or `null`
  /// if the app was launched in another way: known once `bootstrap()` is
  /// over. The tap is not among the [taps]. It is `null` for a tap on a
  /// notification of an owner, as [taps] leaves those out.
  NotificationTap? get launchTap => switch (_launchTap) {
    final tap? when _isOfApp(tap) => tap,
    _ => null,
  };

  /// Tells of each tap on a notification of the app while the app runs,
  /// with the data of the notification: a broadcast stream, the same for
  /// the life of the app. It leaves out the taps on the notifications of an
  /// owner, which [tapsOf] gives, and those with a negative id: the
  /// notification of an owner whose data the provider could not read.
  late final Stream<NotificationTap> taps = _taps.stream.where(_isOfApp);

  /// Tells of each tap on a notification whose owner is [owner], as it is
  /// under [notificationOwnerKey] in its data: a broadcast stream.
  Stream<NotificationTap> tapsOf(String owner) =>
      _taps.stream.where((tap) => _ownerOf(tap) == owner);

  /// The tap on a notification whose owner is [owner] that launched the
  /// app, or `null`.
  NotificationTap? launchTapOf(String owner) => switch (_launchTap) {
    final tap? when _ownerOf(tap) == owner => tap,
    _ => null,
  };

  /// Starts with [service]: takes the tap that launched the app, follows
  /// the taps, and asks whether the app may show notifications.
  ///
  /// `initNotifications()` calls it in `bootstrap()`, so [permission] and
  /// [launchTap] are known before the first frame. It may be called again,
  /// with the service that the app has from then on: `initNotifications()`
  /// does so in a test of the next launch of the app, and a test gives it a
  /// service of its own.
  ///
  /// If the service fails to tell whether the app may show notifications,
  /// the future fails with its error, and so do the calls that waited for
  /// the first start. A call after that goes to the service.
  Future<void> start(NotificationsService service) async {
    unawaited(_serviceTaps?.cancel());
    _service = service;
    _launchTap = service.launchTap;
    // An error of the taps has no caller: it reaches the handlers of the
    // uncaught errors of the app, and the taps go on.
    _serviceTaps = service.taps.listen(_taps.add);
    if (_observer == null) {
      final observer = _observer = _ResumeObserver(() => unawaited(_refresh()));
      WidgetsBinding.instance.addObserver(observer);
    }
    try {
      await _refresh();
    } on Object catch (error, stackTrace) {
      _firstStarted(error, stackTrace);
      rethrow;
    }
    _firstStarted();
  }

  /// Asks the user for the permission to show notifications, unless the app
  /// has it, and completes with the permission once the user answered.
  ///
  /// Ask where the user turns on something that notifies, so that the
  /// question explains itself. Where the platform has no question, the
  /// service opens the settings of the system instead. After
  /// [NotificationPermission.denied] the system may ask no more: iOS asks
  /// once, and Android after a second no. So open its settings then:
  /// [openSettings].
  ///
  /// A request while another is on its way completes with that one. If the
  /// preferences fail to save that the app asked, the future completes with
  /// their error: [permission] is up to date by then, but the next launch
  /// of the app takes it that the app has not asked.
  Future<NotificationPermission> request() =>
      _request ??= _ask().whenComplete(() => _request = null);

  /// Opens the settings of the system for the notifications of the app.
  Future<void> openSettings() async => (await _ready()).openSettings();

  /// Shows [notification] now.
  ///
  /// Its id is a 32-bit integer, 0 or more unless the notification has an
  /// owner under [notificationOwnerKey] in its data, which takes a negative
  /// id. Its future fails with an [ArgumentError] for another id.
  Future<void> show(AppNotification notification) async {
    _check(notification);
    await (await _ready()).show(notification);
  }

  /// Shows [notification] at about [at], not to the minute, also when the
  /// app is closed then. Its future fails with an [ArgumentError] when [at]
  /// is not in the future, and for an id that [show] does not take.
  Future<void> schedule(
    AppNotification notification, {
    required DateTime at,
  }) async {
    _check(notification);
    if (!at.isAfter(DateTime.now())) {
      throw ArgumentError.value(at, 'at', 'The time is not in the future');
    }
    await (await _ready()).schedule(notification, at);
  }

  /// Removes the notification [id], whether it is shown or scheduled.
  Future<void> cancel(int id) async => (await _ready()).cancel(id);

  /// Removes every notification of the app, shown or scheduled, those of an
  /// owner too.
  Future<void> cancelAll() async => (await _ready()).cancelAll();

  /// The notifications that are scheduled and not shown yet, those of an
  /// owner too.
  Future<List<AppNotification>> scheduled() async =>
      (await _ready()).scheduled();

  /// The service, once the app has started for the first time.
  Future<NotificationsService> _ready() async {
    if (!_started) {
      final untilStarted = Completer<void>();
      _untilStarted.add(untilStarted);
      await untilStarted.future;
    }
    return _service!;
  }

  /// Takes it that the app has started for the first time, unless it had,
  /// and lets the calls that waited for that go on, or fail with [error] of
  /// the start. Nothing but these calls reports that error, which the start
  /// throws itself.
  void _firstStarted([Object? error, StackTrace? stackTrace]) {
    if (_started) return;
    _started = true;
    for (final untilStarted in _untilStarted) {
      if (error == null) {
        untilStarted.complete();
      } else {
        untilStarted.completeError(error, stackTrace);
      }
    }
    _untilStarted.clear();
  }

  Future<NotificationPermission> _ask() async {
    final service = await _ready();
    if (await service.areEnabled()) {
      _enabled = true;
      _update();
      return _permission.value;
    }
    await service.request();
    _asked = true;
    _enabled = await service.areEnabled();
    _update();
    await _preferences?.setBool(_askedKey, true);
    return _permission.value;
  }

  /// Asks the service whether the app may show notifications.
  Future<void> _refresh() async {
    _enabled = await _service!.areEnabled();
    _update();
  }

  /// Takes that the app asked from [preferences], and keeps it as it is when
  /// they have nothing saved. It keeps [preferences] for the next request.
  void _restore(AppPreferences preferences) {
    _preferences = preferences;
    _asked = preferences.getBool(_askedKey) ?? _asked;
    _update();
  }

  void _update() => _permission.value = _enabled
      ? NotificationPermission.granted
      : _asked
      ? NotificationPermission.denied
      : NotificationPermission.notAsked;

  static String? _ownerOf(NotificationTap tap) =>
      tap.data[notificationOwnerKey];

  /// Whether [tap] is on a notification of the app: one without an owner,
  /// whose id is not negative.
  static bool _isOfApp(NotificationTap tap) =>
      tap.id >= 0 && _ownerOf(tap) == null;

  /// Throws an [ArgumentError] for an id of [notification] that the app
  /// does not take; see [show].
  static void _check(AppNotification notification) {
    final AppNotification(:id, :data) = notification;
    if (id < _smallestId || id > _largestId) {
      throw ArgumentError.value(id, 'id', 'The id is no 32-bit integer');
    }
    final owned = data.containsKey(notificationOwnerKey);
    if (id < 0 && !owned) {
      throw ArgumentError.value(
        id,
        'id',
        'A negative id is for a notification with an owner, which names it '
            'under notificationOwnerKey in its data',
      );
    }
    if (id >= 0 && owned) {
      throw ArgumentError.value(
        id,
        'id',
        'A notification with an owner has a negative id',
      );
    }
  }
}

/// Calls [_onResume] each time the app is resumed, whatever state it was in
/// before.
final class _ResumeObserver with WidgetsBindingObserver {
  _ResumeObserver(this._onResume);

  final VoidCallback _onResume;

  @override
  void didChangeAppLifecycleState(AppLifecycleState state) {
    if (state == AppLifecycleState.resumed) _onResume();
  }
}

/// show notifications, and keeps them for the next request: a restorer of
/// the preferences, which the app calls before its first frame.
void restoreNotifications(AppPreferences preferences) =>
    appNotifications._restore(preferences);

=== lib/core/notifications/notifications_service.dart (role:notifications) ===
/// The id of the channel of Android in which the app shows its
/// notifications. Android shows a notification only in a channel, and the
/// user turns each channel of an app on or off in the settings of the
/// system.
const String notificationChannelId = 'default';

/// The name of that channel, which the settings of the system show: the
/// name of the app.
const String notificationChannelName = 'Fixture App';

/// The name of the drawable of Android that a notification of the app shows
/// as its small icon:
/// `android/app/src/main/res/drawable/ic_notification.xml`.
const String notificationIcon = 'ic_notification';

/// A notification of the app.
final class AppNotification {
  /// Creates the notification [id] with [title], and with [body] and [data]
  /// if they are given.
  const AppNotification({
    required this.id,
    required this.title,
    this.body,
    this.data = const {},
  });

  /// The id of the notification. A notification with the id of one that is
  /// shown or scheduled takes its place, a scheduled one when it shows.
  final int id;

  /// The title of the notification.
  final String title;

  /// The text below the title, or `null` for a notification with a title
  /// alone.
  final String? body;

  /// What the app needs to know when the user taps the notification, such
  /// as the screen that the tap opens.
  final Map<String, String> data;
}

/// A tap of the user on a notification of the app.
final class NotificationTap {
  /// Creates the tap on the notification [id] with [data].
  const NotificationTap({required this.id, this.data = const {}});

  /// The id of the notification.
  final int id;

  /// The data of the notification.
  final Map<String, String> data;
}

/// The notifications of the platform, which the provider of notifications
/// implements: the permission to show them, the notifications that the app
/// shows now or at a time to come, and the taps of the user on them.
///
/// The code of the app does not use it: it uses `appNotifications` of
/// `app_notifications.dart`, which calls this service, remembers that the
/// app asked for the permission and tells the taps of the app from those of
/// an owner.
///
/// Every implementation keeps to this:
/// - The function that creates the service asks the user nothing. It may be
///   called again, and the service that it returns then has what the device
///   kept, such as the notifications that are scheduled.
/// - [show] and [schedule] complete whether the app may show notifications
///   or not. Without the permission the system shows nothing, and may drop
///   a notification that is scheduled.
/// - Every notification goes to the channel [notificationChannelId] with a
///   high importance and, on Android, [notificationIcon] as its small icon.
///   It shows also while the app is in the foreground.
/// - [schedule] shows the notification at about the time that it is given,
///   not to the minute, also when the app is closed then, and after a
///   restart of the device. A notification with the id of one that is shown
///   or scheduled takes its place: [show] cancels the scheduled
///   notification of its id, if there is one, so that it does not show
///   later, and a notification scheduled with the id of one that is shown
///   takes its place when it shows. [cancel] removes the notification of
///   its id, shown or scheduled.
/// - [taps] tells of each tap on a notification of the app while the app
///   runs, and [launchTap] is the tap that launched the app. Each has the
///   data that the notification had, or none if the service cannot read it.
/// - [request] shows the question of the system where the platform has one,
///   and completes once the user answered, also with a no, for which it does
///   not throw. Where the platform has no question to show and the app may
///   not show notifications, as on Android before 13, it opens the settings
///   of the system for the notifications of the app instead, as
///   [openSettings] does, and completes. So the user needs only one request
///   to turn the notifications on, whatever the platform.
abstract interface class NotificationsService {
  /// The tap on a notification that launched the app, or `null` if the app
  /// was launched in another way: known when the service is created.
  NotificationTap? get launchTap;

  /// Tells of each tap of the user on a notification of the app while the
  /// app runs: a broadcast stream.
  Stream<NotificationTap> get taps;

  /// Whether the app may show notifications.
  Future<bool> areEnabled();

  /// Asks the user for the permission to show notifications, or opens the
  /// settings of the system where the platform has no question to show.
  Future<void> request();

  /// Opens the settings of the system for the notifications of the app.
  Future<void> openSettings();

  /// Shows [notification] now, in place of the notification of its id that
  /// is shown, and cancels the scheduled notification of its id.
  Future<void> show(AppNotification notification);

  /// Shows [notification] at about [at].
  Future<void> schedule(AppNotification notification, DateTime at);

  /// Removes the notification [id], whether it is shown or scheduled.
  Future<void> cancel(int id);

  /// Removes every notification of the app, shown or scheduled.
  Future<void> cancelAll();

  /// The notifications that are scheduled and not shown yet.
  Future<List<AppNotification>> scheduled();
}

/// A step of the onboarding, such as a page that asks for a permission: an
/// icon, a title, a text, and a button whose action is [run].
///
/// The onboarding shows each step of [appOnboardingSteps] on a page of its
/// own, in the order of the list, unless [showIf] returns `false` when the
/// onboarding opens. Skipping the onboarding skips its steps too.
final class OnboardingStep {
  /// Creates a step with [icon], [title] and [text], whose button [action]
  /// calls [run], and with a second button [later] if it is set.
  const OnboardingStep({
    required this.icon,
    required this.title,
    required this.text,
    required this.action,
    required this.run,
    this.later,
    this.showIf,
  });

  /// The icon of the page of the step.

  /// Returns the title of the page in the language of the app, for the

  final String Function(BuildContext context) text;

  /// Returns the label of the button that calls [run], as [title] does.
  final String Function(BuildContext context) action;

  /// What the button [action] does, such as asking for a permission, for
  /// the context of the onboarding screen. The onboarding goes on to its
  /// next page once the future completes, also when it fails.

  final String Function(BuildContext context)? later;

  /// Whether the onboarding shows the step, which it asks once when it
  /// opens, or `null` for a step that it always shows.
  final bool Function()? showIf;
}

=== lib/core/preferences/app_preferences.dart (role:preferences) ===
import 'package:fixture_app/core/fixture_preferences/fixture_preferences.dart'
    as impl0;
import 'package:fixture_app/core/fixture_setting/fixture_setting.dart';
import 'package:fixture_app/core/l10n/app_locale.dart';
import 'package:fixture_app/core/notifications/app_notifications.dart';
import 'package:fixture_app/core/theme/theme_mode.dart';
import 'package:flutter/foundation.dart';

/// The settings of the app that are no secret, which it remembers between
/// its launches: the theme mode, the language, whether the user has seen
/// the onboarding.
///
/// Nothing here is encrypted, and a backup of the device carries it: never
/// store a token, a password, an API key or an encryption key in the
/// preferences.
///
/// A key is `<owner id>.<setting>`, such as `theme.mode`, where the owner
/// is the module or the role whose code keeps the setting. A key has one
/// value: a write replaces what the key had, a value of another type too.
///
/// A read is synchronous, from memory, and never throws: it returns `null`
/// when the key has no value of the type it asks for. Whether a number
/// saved as an `int` is read as a `double`, or the other way round, is up
/// to the provider of the preferences. Once the future of a write
/// completes, reads return what it saved, in this run of the app and after
/// its next launch.
abstract interface class AppPreferences {
  /// The text saved under [key], or `null` if it has none.
  String? getString(String key);

  /// The flag saved under [key], or `null` if it has none.
  bool? getBool(String key);

  /// The whole number saved under [key], or `null` if it has none.
  int? getInt(String key);

  /// The number saved under [key], or `null` if it has none.
  double? getDouble(String key);

  /// A copy of the list of texts saved under [key], or `null` if it has
  /// none: a change of the list changes nothing that is saved.
  List<String>? getStringList(String key);

  /// Saves the text [value] under [key].
  Future<void> setString(String key, String value);

  /// Saves the flag [value] under [key].
  Future<void> setBool(String key, bool value);

  /// Saves the whole number [value] under [key].
  Future<void> setInt(String key, int value);

  /// Saves the number [value] under [key].
  Future<void> setDouble(String key, double value);

  /// Saves a copy of the list of texts [value] under [key]: a later change
  /// of [value] changes nothing that is saved.
  Future<void> setStringList(String key, List<String> value);

  /// Removes what is saved under [key], which puts its setting back to its
  /// default: the reads of [key] return `null` again.
  Future<void> remove(String key);
}

/// Returns the preferences of the app, which [initPreferences] opened:
/// `bootstrap()` awaits it before the first frame, and there are none to
/// return before that.
AppPreferences createAppPreferences() => _appPreferences;

late AppPreferences _appPreferences;

/// Opens the preferences of the app and gives them to the restorers of its
/// parts; `bootstrap()` awaits it before the first frame, and nothing else
/// in the app calls it.
///
/// It may run again, as a test does to see what the next launch of the app
/// reads: it opens the preferences anew, with what is saved, and calls the
/// restorers again.
Future<void> initPreferences() async {
  _appPreferences = impl0.createFixturePreferences();
  _restore(_appPreferences);
}

/// The functions of the parts of the app that restore what they keep in the
/// preferences, such as the theme mode. [initPreferences] calls each with
/// the preferences once they are open, so that every part has its settings
/// before the first frame.
///
/// A restorer reads its settings, and keeps the preferences for the writes
/// of its part. It is synchronous and awaits nothing: nothing waits for an
/// asynchronous one, and what such a one throws is not caught. It may run
/// again, with preferences that were opened anew.
final List<void Function(AppPreferences preferences)> _restorers = [
  noteFixtureSetting,
  restoreFixtureSetting,
  restoreAppLocale,
  restoreNotifications,
  restoreAppThemeMode,
];

/// Calls each of [_restorers] with [preferences] on its own: what one
/// throws, an error or an exception, keeps no other from restoring and does
/// not stop the start-up of the app, which goes on with the defaults of
/// that part. In debug mode it is printed, so that a restorer that fails
/// shows in the console.
void _restore(AppPreferences preferences) {
  for (final restore in _restorers) {
    try {
      restore(preferences);
    } on Object catch (error) {
      if (kDebugMode) {
        debugPrint('A restorer of the preferences failed: $error');
      }
    }
  }
}
=== lib/core/purchases/app_purchases.dart (role:purchases) ===
import 'dart:async';

import 'package:fixture_app/core/auth/app_session.dart';
import 'package:fixture_app/core/fixture_purchases/fixture_purchases.dart'
    as impl0;
import 'package:flutter/foundation.dart';
import 'package:flutter/widgets.dart';

import 'purchases_service.dart';

export 'purchases_service.dart' hide PurchasesService;

/// The purchases of the app: whether the user has premium access, known
/// before the first frame, the plans that the app sells, and the way to buy
/// and to restore them.
///
/// Its `value` is the [AppAccess] of the user, and it tells its listeners
/// when that changes. A widget follows it with a `ValueListenableBuilder`.
final appPurchases = AppPurchasesController();

/// Keeps the access of the user up to date with the service of the provider
/// of purchases, and buys and restores through that service. The app has
/// one, [appPurchases].
///
/// Every call here fails only with a [PurchasesFailure], whose reason the
/// app shows in a text of its own. An error of another kind becomes a
/// failure with the reason [PurchasesFailureReason.unknown] and the error as
/// its [PurchasesFailure.developerHint].
///
/// The calls run one after another, in the order they were made, and so
/// does what the controller does itself when the user of the app changes or
/// comes back to the app. Before each, the controller identifies the user
/// with the service, unless the service has that user already. So a
/// purchase belongs to the user who made it: a change of the user waits for
/// it.
///
/// An error that no caller sees, of the start, of a change of the user, of
/// the return of the user to the app or of the changes that the service
/// tells of, changes nothing. A failure [PurchasesFailureReason.notConfigured]
/// prints its hint once in debug mode. [PurchasesFailureReason.network],
/// [PurchasesFailureReason.unavailable] and
/// [PurchasesFailureReason.storeProblem], which pass, say nothing. Any other
/// error reaches the handlers of the uncaught errors of the app. So the app
/// starts and runs without purchases when the provider is not set up or the
/// store does not answer.
final class AppPurchasesController extends ChangeNotifier
    implements ValueListenable<AppAccess> {
  /// Creates a controller. The app creates [appPurchases] and no other. A
  /// test creates one to start it with a service of its own, or with a
  /// shorter [startWait], and disposes of it.
  AppPurchasesController({this.startWait = const Duration(seconds: 3)});

  /// How long [start] waits at most for the access of the user and for the
  /// calls made before it.
  final Duration startWait;

  PurchasesService? _service;
  StreamSubscription<AppAccess>? _accessChanges;
  String? Function()? _userId;
  Listenable? _userChanges;
  AppAccess _access = const NoAccess();
  bool _disposed = false;

  /// The service and the user that the service was last identified with,
  /// or `null` while no identification with the service succeeded.
  _Identity? _identified;

  /// Whether [start] was called.
  bool _starting = false;

  /// Whether the controller has started for the first time.
  bool _started = false;

  /// Completes at the first start. Only the first call made before it waits
  /// for it, and each later call waits for the call before it. That call
  /// creates it, so the start completes it in the zone of the call: created
  /// with the controller, it would complete in a microtask of the zone that
  /// read [appPurchases] first, which may run none any more, as the fake
  /// time of a widget test that is over before a later test starts the app
  /// in real time.
  Completer<void>? _untilStarted;

  /// The call that was made last, while a call is on its way: the next one
  /// waits for it. It is `null` when none is, and a call then awaits
  /// nothing. A future that completed in another zone would hold the call
  /// back in a widget test, where the app starts in real time and the test
  /// goes on in fake time.
  Future<void>? _lastCall;

  /// Whether a look at the user and at the access waits for its turn, so
  /// that the changes and returns that come meanwhile need no other.
  bool _lookWaits = false;

  /// Tells when the user comes back to the app.
  _ResumeObserver? _observer;

  /// The hints of the failures [PurchasesFailureReason.notConfigured] that
  /// the controller printed.
  final Set<String?> _printed = {};

  /// The access of the user.
  @override
  AppAccess get value => _access;

  /// Whether the user has premium access, as a guard of the routes reads it.
  ///
  /// It is computed from [value] each time it is read, and its listeners
  /// are those of the controller. A listener is also called for a change of
  /// [value] that leaves this value as it was, such as a plan that renews.
  late final ValueListenable<bool> hasPremium = _PremiumFlag(this);

  /// Starts with [service]: identifies the user with it, takes the access of
  /// the user, and follows the changes of the access that the service tells
  /// of.
  ///
  /// [userId] returns the id of the user of the app, or `null` for the user
  /// of this device, and [userChanges] tells when it may have changed, such
  /// as the session of an app with sign-in. Without them, the purchases are
  /// those of the user of this device. When the user changes, [value] is
  /// [NoAccess] until the service has the new user, and the controller takes
  /// no change of the access that the service tells of meanwhile.
  ///
  /// `initPurchases()` calls it in `bootstrap()`, so [value] is known before
  /// the first frame. It waits for the access at most [startWait]: an access
  /// that comes later still becomes [value], and the app starts without
  /// waiting for it.
  ///
  /// A call made before the first start waits for it: [plans], [purchase]
  /// and [restore]. Such calls run in their order once the start has the
  /// access, and the future of this start completes after them, and after
  /// the calls that their completions make right away: it completes once no
  /// call is on its way in the microtask after the last one ended. The mocks
  /// of the tests of an app use this: `plans().then(...)` that buys a plan,
  /// made before `bootstrap()`, gives the app premium access before its first
  /// frame. A test makes such a call outside the body of a test, as in a
  /// `setUpAll`: made in the body of a widget test, it waits in the fake time
  /// of that body, and a start that runs in real time completes after
  /// [startWait] without it.
  ///
  /// It may be called again, with the service that the controller has from
  /// then on: `initPurchases()` does so in a test of the next launch of the
  /// app, and a test gives it a service of its own. Such a start takes its
  /// turn after the calls that are on their way, and [value] is then that of
  /// the new service.
  Future<void> start(
    PurchasesService service, {
    String? Function()? userId,
    Listenable? userChanges,
  }) =>
      _start(service, userId, userChanges).timeout(startWait, onTimeout: () {});

  /// The plans of the current offer of the app, at least one, in the order
  /// of the provider. Show them only to a user without premium access.
  Future<List<Plan>> plans() => _call((service, _) => service.plans());

  /// Buys [plan] through the sheet of the store, and completes with `true`
  /// once the user has bought it, or with `false` when the user cancelled,
  /// which is no failure. The access that the purchase gave becomes [value],
  /// unless the user of the app changed meanwhile.
  ///
  /// A payment that waits for an approval fails with
  /// [PurchasesFailureReason.pending]: the access comes once the store took
  /// the payment. A purchase whose answer gives no premium access, as with a
  /// plan that the backend of the provider does not tie to the access, fails
  /// with [PurchasesFailureReason.notConfigured]: the user paid, and a
  /// restore gives the access once the provider is set up.
  Future<bool> purchase(Plan plan) => _call((service, identity) async {
    final access = await service.purchase(plan);
    if (access == null) return false;
    if (access is! PremiumAccess) {
      throw const PurchasesFailure(
        PurchasesFailureReason.notConfigured,
        developerHint:
            'The plan was bought, but it gives no premium '
            'access: check the setup of the provider.',
      );
    }
    _take(identity, access);
    return true;
  });

  /// Gives the user what the store account of the user bought, such as on a
  /// new device, and completes with the access of the user then. That access
  /// becomes [value], unless the user of the app changed meanwhile.
  Future<AppAccess> restore() => _call((service, identity) async {
    final access = await service.restore();
    _take(identity, access);
    return access;
  });

  /// Stops following the service, the user and the lifecycle of the app. A
  /// call that is on its way still ends as it would have, but tells no
  /// listener. A start or a look at the access that waits for its turn does
  /// nothing then.
  @override
  void dispose() {
    _disposed = true;
    unawaited(_accessChanges?.cancel());
    _userChanges?.removeListener(_onUserChange);
    if (_observer case final observer?) {
      WidgetsBinding.instance.removeObserver(observer);
    }
    super.dispose();
  }

  Future<void> _start(
    PurchasesService service,
    String? Function()? userId,
    Listenable? userChanges,
  ) async {
    if (_starting) {
      await _turn(() => _use(service, userId, userChanges));
    } else {
      _starting = true;
      await _use(service, userId, userChanges);
      _started = true;
      _untilStarted?.complete();
    }
    await _settled();
  }

  /// Takes [service] as the service of the controller, with the user of
  /// [userId], and looks at the user and at the access, unless the
  /// controller was disposed of while the start waited for its turn.
  Future<void> _use(
    PurchasesService service,
    String? Function()? userId,
    Listenable? userChanges,
  ) async {
    if (_disposed) return;
    unawaited(_accessChanges?.cancel());
    _userChanges?.removeListener(_onUserChange);
    _service = service;
    _userId = userId;
    _userChanges = userChanges?..addListener(_onUserChange);
    _identified = null;
    _set(const NoAccess());
    _accessChanges = service.accessChanges.listen(
      (access) => _onAccessChange(service, access),
      onError: _report,
    );
    if (_observer == null) {
      final observer = _observer = _ResumeObserver(_lookLater);
      WidgetsBinding.instance.addObserver(observer);
    }
    await _quietly(_look);
  }

  /// Completes once no call is on its way, and none was made in the
  /// microtask after the last one ended.
  Future<void> _settled() async {
    while (true) {
      final last = _lastCall;
      if (last != null) {
        await last;
        continue;
      }
      await Future<void>.microtask(() {});
      if (_lastCall == null) return;
    }
  }

  /// Runs [call] with the service and the user of the app, once the calls
  /// made before it are over, and turns whatever it throws that is no
  /// [PurchasesFailure] into one.
  Future<T> _call<T>(
    Future<T> Function(PurchasesService service, _Identity identity) call,
  ) => _turn(() async {
    try {
      final service = _service!;
      final before = _identified;
      final identity = await _identify(service);
      // A call that identified the user again, as after an
      // identification that failed, is followed by a look at the access
      // of the user.
      if (identity != before) _lookLater();
      return await call(service, identity);
    } on PurchasesFailure {
      rethrow;
    } on Object catch (error, stackTrace) {
      Error.throwWithStackTrace(
        PurchasesFailure(
          PurchasesFailureReason.unknown,
          developerHint: '$error',
        ),
        stackTrace,
      );
    }
  });

  /// Runs [job] once the calls made before it are over, and not before the
  /// controller has started for the first time.
  Future<T> _turn<T>(Future<T> Function() job) async {
    final earlier =
        _lastCall ??
        (_started ? null : (_untilStarted ??= Completer<void>()).future);
    final done = Completer<void>();
    final own = _lastCall = done.future;
    if (earlier != null) await earlier;
    try {
      return await job();
    } finally {
      if (identical(_lastCall, own)) _lastCall = null;
      done.complete();
    }
  }

  /// Identifies the user of the app with [service], unless [service] has
  /// that user already, and returns the identity.
  Future<_Identity> _identify(PurchasesService service) async {
    final user = _userId?.call();
    final identified = _identified;
    if (identified != null &&
        identical(identified.service, service) &&
        identified.user == user) {
      return identified;
    }
    // Until the identification succeeds, the service has no user that the
    // controller knows of, and the user has no access.
    _identified = null;
    _set(const NoAccess());
    await service.identify(user);
    final identity = (service: service, user: user);
    if (identical(service, _service)) _identified = identity;
    return identity;
  }

  /// Identifies the user, and takes the access of that user, unless the
  /// controller was disposed of while the look waited for its turn.
  Future<void> _look() async {
    if (_disposed) return;
    final service = _service!;
    final identity = await _identify(service);
    _take(identity, await service.access());
  }

  /// Looks at the user and at the access in its turn, unless a look waits
  /// for its turn already.
  void _lookLater() {
    if (_lookWaits || _disposed) return;
    _lookWaits = true;
    unawaited(
      _turn(() {
        _lookWaits = false;
        return _quietly(_look);
      }),
    );
  }

  /// The user of the app may have changed. If it has, [value] is [NoAccess]
  /// until the service has the new user.
  void _onUserChange() {
    final identified = _identified;
    if (identified != null && identified.user == _userId?.call()) return;
    _set(const NoAccess());
    _lookLater();
  }

  /// Takes [access], which [service] told of, while [service] has the user
  /// of the app.
  void _onAccessChange(PurchasesService service, AppAccess access) {
    final identified = _identified;
    if (identified != null && identical(identified.service, service)) {
      _take(identified, access);
    }
  }

  /// Takes [access], the answer of the service of [identity], as [value],
  /// while the controller has that service and the service has the user of
  /// [identity], who is still the user of the app. The answer for a user
  /// who is no longer the user of the app changes nothing.
  void _take(_Identity identity, AppAccess access) {
    final identified = _identified;
    if (identified != null &&
        identical(identified.service, identity.service) &&
        identical(identity.service, _service) &&
        identified.user == identity.user &&
        identity.user == _userId?.call()) {
      _set(access);
    }
  }

  /// Takes [access] as [value], and tells the listeners when that changes
  /// it, unless the controller was disposed of.
  void _set(AppAccess access) {
    if (_disposed || access == _access) return;
    _access = access;
    notifyListeners();
  }

  /// Runs [job], whose errors no caller sees, and reports what it throws.
  Future<void> _quietly(Future<void> Function() job) async {
    try {
      await job();
    } on Object catch (error, stackTrace) {
      _report(error, stackTrace);
    }
  }

  /// Reports [error], which no caller sees.
  void _report(Object error, StackTrace stackTrace) {
    switch (error) {
      case PurchasesFailure(
        reason: PurchasesFailureReason.notConfigured,
        :final developerHint,
      ):
        if (kDebugMode && _printed.add(developerHint)) {
          debugPrint(
            'Purchases are not set up, so the app runs without them: '
            '$developerHint',
          );
        }
      case PurchasesFailure(
        reason: PurchasesFailureReason.network ||
            PurchasesFailureReason.unavailable ||
            PurchasesFailureReason.storeProblem,
      ):
        break;
      default:
        Zone.current.handleUncaughtError(error, stackTrace);
    }
  }
}

/// A service and the user that it was identified with.
typedef _Identity = ({PurchasesService service, String? user});

/// Calls [_onResume] each time the app is resumed, whatever state it was in
/// before.
final class _ResumeObserver with WidgetsBindingObserver {
  _ResumeObserver(this._onResume);

  final VoidCallback _onResume;

  @override
  void didChangeAppLifecycleState(AppLifecycleState state) {
    if (state == AppLifecycleState.resumed) _onResume();
  }
}

/// Whether the user of a controller has premium access: its value is
/// computed from the access each time it is read, and its listeners are
/// those of the controller.
final class _PremiumFlag implements ValueListenable<bool> {
  const _PremiumFlag(this._controller);

  final AppPurchasesController _controller;

  @override
  bool get value => _controller.value is PremiumAccess;

  @override
  void addListener(VoidCallback listener) => _controller.addListener(listener);

  @override
  void removeListener(VoidCallback listener) =>
      _controller.removeListener(listener);
}

    userId: () => appSession.value.uid,
