=== lib/core/purchases/purchases_service.dart (role:purchases) ===
/// What the user may use of the app: [NoAccess], or [PremiumAccess] while a
/// plan that the user bought is active.
sealed class AppAccess {
  const AppAccess();
}

/// The user has no plan that is active, or the app does not know of one.
final class NoAccess extends AppAccess {
  /// Creates the access of a user without premium access.
  const NoAccess();

  @override
  bool operator ==(Object other) => other is NoAccess;

  @override
  int get hashCode => (NoAccess).hashCode;

  @override
  String toString() => 'NoAccess()';
}

/// The user has premium access: a plan that the user bought is active.
/// Every plan of the app gives the same access.
final class PremiumAccess extends AppAccess {
  /// Creates the premium access of a plan, with what the provider knows of
  /// it.
  const PremiumAccess({
    this.expiresAt,
    this.renews = false,
    this.inTrial = false,
    this.period,
    this.manageUrl,
  });

  /// When the access ends unless the plan renews, or `null` for an access
  /// that never ends, as that of a plan paid once.
  final DateTime? expiresAt;

  /// Whether the store renews the plan when [expiresAt] comes: `false` once
  /// the user cancelled, and for a plan paid once.
  final bool renews;

  /// Whether the access is the free trial of its plan: the store charges the
  /// price of the plan once the trial ends, unless the user cancels.
  final bool inTrial;

  /// How often the plan is billed, or `null` for a plan paid once, or when
  /// the provider does not know.
  final BillingPeriod? period;

  /// The page of the store where the user manages or cancels the plan, or
  /// `null` when the store has none for it, as for a plan paid once.
  final Uri? manageUrl;

  @override
  bool operator ==(Object other) =>
      other is PremiumAccess &&
      other.expiresAt == expiresAt &&
      other.renews == renews &&
      other.inTrial == inTrial &&
      other.period == period &&
      other.manageUrl == manageUrl;

  @override
  int get hashCode =>
      Object.hash(expiresAt, renews, inTrial, period, manageUrl);

  @override
  String toString() =>
      'PremiumAccess(expiresAt: $expiresAt, renews: $renews, '
      'inTrial: $inTrial, period: $period, manageUrl: $manageUrl)';
}

/// The unit of a [BillingPeriod].
enum BillingUnit {
  /// A day.
  day,

  /// A week.
  week,

  /// A month.
  month,

  /// A year.
  year,
}

/// How often a plan is billed, or how long a period of an [IntroOffer]
/// lasts: [count] times the [unit], such as 3 months.
final class BillingPeriod {
  /// Creates the period of [count] times [unit].
  const BillingPeriod(this.count, this.unit);

  /// How many units the period lasts, 1 or more.
  final int count;

  /// The unit of the period.
  final BillingUnit unit;

  @override
  bool operator ==(Object other) =>
      other is BillingPeriod && other.count == count && other.unit == unit;

  @override
  int get hashCode => Object.hash(count, unit);

  @override
  String toString() => 'BillingPeriod($count ${unit.name})';
}

/// What a plan costs for a first while, for a user who may take it: a free
/// trial, or a lower price.
final class IntroOffer {
  /// Creates the offer of [cycles] periods of [period] at [price].
  const IntroOffer({
    required this.price,
    required this.amount,
    required this.period,
    this.cycles = 1,
  });

  /// The price of a [period] of the offer as the store shows it, such as
  /// `$0.99`.
  final String price;

  /// The price of a [period] of the offer as a number in the currency of
  /// its plan: 0 for a free trial.
  final num amount;

  /// How long a period of the offer lasts.
  final BillingPeriod period;

  /// How many periods the offer lasts, 1 or more.
  final int cycles;

  /// Whether the offer is a free trial.
  bool get isFree => amount == 0;
}

/// A plan of the current offer of the app, which the user can buy. Every
/// plan gives premium access.
final class Plan {
  /// Creates the plan [id].
  const Plan({
    required this.id,
    required this.title,
    required this.price,
    required this.amount,
    required this.currencyCode,
    this.period,
    this.introOffer,
  });

  /// The id that the provider gives the plan. The app does not read it.
  final String id;

  /// The name of the product of the plan in the store.
  final String title;

  /// The full price of the plan as the store shows it, in the currency of
  /// the user, such as `$4.99`: what a [period] costs, or what the plan
  /// costs once.
  final String price;

  /// The full price as a number in [currencyCode].
  final num amount;

  /// The ISO 4217 code of the currency of the price, such as `USD`.
  final String currencyCode;

  /// How often the plan renews, or `null` for a plan paid once, which gives
  /// premium access for good.
  final BillingPeriod? period;

  /// What the plan costs for a first while, if this user may take that, or
  /// `null`.
  final IntroOffer? introOffer;
}

/// Why a call of purchases failed.
enum PurchasesFailureReason {
  /// The device did not reach the store or the provider.
  network,

  /// The payment waits for an approval, as one that a parent approves, or
  /// for a payment method that takes a while. The access comes once the
  /// store took the payment, and the service tells of it then.
  pending,

  /// The store account of the user owns the plan already: a restore gives
  /// the user its access.
  alreadyOwned,

  /// The user may not pay on this device, as with parental controls or
  /// payments that are turned off.
  notAllowed,

  /// The store, or the plan, is not available on this device or for this
  /// store account, as on a device without the store.
  unavailable,

  /// The store failed on its side. Trying again later may help.
  storeProblem,

  /// Purchases are not set up, such as a key of the provider that is
  /// missing, or an offer without plans. This is a mistake in the setup of
  /// the app, which [PurchasesFailure.developerHint] explains.
  notConfigured,

  /// Anything else. [PurchasesFailure.developerHint] has the error.
  unknown,
}

/// The failure of a call of purchases: the only thing that such a call
/// throws. A user who cancels a purchase is no failure.
final class PurchasesFailure implements Exception {
  /// Creates the failure for [reason].
  const PurchasesFailure(this.reason, {this.developerHint});

  /// Why the call failed. The app shows a text of its own for each reason.
  final PurchasesFailureReason reason;

  /// What went wrong in the words of the provider, for the developer of the
  /// app, or `null`. It is no text for the user: show it in debug mode
  /// only.
  final String? developerHint;

  @override
  String toString() => developerHint == null
      ? 'PurchasesFailure: ${reason.name}'
      : 'PurchasesFailure: ${reason.name} ($developerHint)';
}

/// The purchases of the users of the app, as the provider of purchases and
/// the store keep them.
///
/// The provider implements it. The code of the app does not call it: it
/// uses `appPurchases` of `app_purchases.dart`, which calls this service and
/// does what is the same with every provider.
///
/// Every implementation keeps to this:
/// - The function that creates the service creates it without a user, asks
///   the user nothing, and never throws. It may be called again, and the
///   service that it returns then has what the store and the provider kept.
/// - [identify] comes before any other call, so a provider can set itself up
///   with the user. Each call is for the user that [identify] last gave.
/// - `identify(null)` makes the user of this device the user whose
///   purchases count. When it follows another user, also one of an earlier
///   launch of the app, it starts a new user of this device, with nothing
///   bought: what a user of the device had before comes back only through
///   [restore].
/// - When the user of this device is identified as a user that no
///   [identify] named before, on any device, that user takes over the
///   purchases of the user of this device, who no longer has them. So a
///   purchase before a sign-up stays with the new account.
/// - Any other [identify] keeps the purchases of each user apart.
/// - [restore] gives the user what the store account bought, and may move
///   purchases from another user to this one, as the backend of the
///   provider decides. So no test relies on a restore that moves them, or on
///   one that leaves them.
/// - [access] completes from what the device keeps, without the network
///   when it keeps anything.
/// - A call fails only with a [PurchasesFailure]. Without what it needs,
///   such as a key, the service fails every call with
///   [PurchasesFailureReason.notConfigured] and a
///   [PurchasesFailure.developerHint] that names what is missing, and it
///   never stops the app.
abstract interface class PurchasesService {
  /// Makes [userId] the user whose purchases count: the id of a user of the
  /// app, or `null` for the user of this device, who has no account of the
  /// app.
  ///
  /// `null` that follows another user starts a new user of this device,
  /// with nothing bought. The user of this device identified as a user that
  /// no call named before, on any device, takes over the purchases of the
  /// user of this device. Identifying the user who is identified already
  /// does nothing and does not fail, `null` too.
  Future<void> identify(String? userId);

  /// The access of the user, from what the device keeps, without the
  /// network when it keeps anything.
  Future<AppAccess> access();

  /// Tells of each change of the access of the user that the provider
  /// learns of, such as a renewal, an expiry, or a grant made elsewhere: a
  /// broadcast stream. It may also tell of an access that has not changed.
  Stream<AppAccess> get accessChanges;

  /// The plans of the current offer of the app, at least one, in the order
  /// of the provider. With nothing to offer, it fails with
  /// [PurchasesFailureReason.notConfigured] and a hint.
  Future<List<Plan>> plans();

  /// Buys [plan] for the user, through the sheet of the store, and
  /// completes with the access of the user once the store took the
  /// payment: a [PremiumAccess], since every plan gives premium access.
  ///
  /// It completes with `null` when the user cancelled, which is no failure.
  /// A payment that waits for an approval fails with
  /// [PurchasesFailureReason.pending], and [accessChanges] tells of the
  /// access once the store took it. A plan that the store account owns
  /// already fails with [PurchasesFailureReason.alreadyOwned].
  Future<AppAccess?> purchase(Plan plan);

  /// Gives the user what the store account of the user bought, such as on a
  /// new device, and completes with the access of the user then. It may
  /// move purchases from another user to this one, as the backend of the
  /// provider decides.
  Future<AppAccess> restore();
}

import 'dart:async';
import 'dart:convert';

import 'package:fixture_app/core/fixture_push/fixture_push.dart' as impl0;
import 'package:flutter/foundation.dart';
import 'package:flutter/widgets.dart';

import '../notifications/app_notifications.dart';
import 'push_service.dart';

export 'push_service.dart' show PushMessage;

/// The push messages of the app: the address of the device, the messages
/// that arrive while the app is in the foreground, and the taps of the user
/// on them.
final appPush = AppPushController._();

/// Receives the push messages of the app through the service of the
/// provider of push messages. The app has one, [appPush].
///
/// It shows a message with a title or a body that arrives while the app is
/// in the foreground as a notification of the app, through
/// [appNotifications], where the platform does not show it itself. Such a
/// notification has [appPush] as its owner, so a tap on it reaches [taps]
/// and not [NotificationsController.taps].
///
/// An error of the service on its streams, of a notification that the
/// controller shows, of an address that it asks for, or of the message that
/// launched the app, has no caller, so it reaches the handlers of the
/// uncaught errors of the app, and the controller goes on.
final class AppPushController {
  AppPushController._();

  /// The owner of the notifications that show the messages, under
  /// [notificationOwnerKey] in their data.
  static const _owner = 'push';

  /// The key under which such a notification keeps its message in its data.
  static const _messageKey = 'push.message';

  PushService? _service;
  StreamSubscription<PushMessage>? _serviceMessages;
  StreamSubscription<PushMessage>? _serviceTaps;
  StreamSubscription<String>? _serviceAddresses;
  StreamSubscription<NotificationTap>? _notificationTaps;
  PushMessage? _launchMessage;

  /// The service whose address the controller is asking for, if any.
  PushService? _asking;

  /// Tells when the user comes back to the app.
  _ResumeObserver? _observer;

  final ValueNotifier<String?> _address = ValueNotifier(null);

  final StreamController<PushMessage> _messages =
      StreamController<PushMessage>.broadcast();

  final StreamController<PushMessage> _taps =
      StreamController<PushMessage>.broadcast();

  /// The address of the device, to which the backend of the app sends the
  /// messages for it, or `null` while the platform gives none. Send each new
  /// one to the backend: it may come late, such as on iOS, and it may
  /// change. A widget follows it with a `ValueListenableBuilder`.
  ///
  /// The controller asks for it when it starts, without waiting for it, and
  /// again each time the user comes back to the app while it has none.
  ValueListenable<String?> get address => _address;

  /// The message whose tap launched the app, or `null` if the app was
  /// launched in another way: known once `bootstrap()` is over. The message
  /// is not among the [taps].
  PushMessage? get launchMessage => _launchMessage;

  /// Tells of each message that arrives while the app is in the foreground:
  /// a broadcast stream, the same for the life of the app.
  late final Stream<PushMessage> messages = _messages.stream;

  /// Tells of each tap of the user on a message while the app runs, whether
  /// the platform or the app showed it: a broadcast stream, the same for the
  /// life of the app.
  late final Stream<PushMessage> taps = _taps.stream;

  /// Starts with [service]: follows its messages, its taps and its address,
  /// asks for the address without waiting for it, and takes the message
  /// whose tap launched the app.
  ///
  /// `initPush()` calls it in `bootstrap()`, after the notifications of the
  /// app started, so [launchMessage] is known before the first frame. It may
  /// be called again, with the service that the app has from then on:
  /// `initPush()` does so in a test of the next launch of the app, and a
  /// test gives it a service of its own. The address is then that of the
  /// new service, and `null` until it gives one.
  ///
  /// If the service fails to give the message that launched the app, the
  /// app still starts: the error reaches the handlers of the uncaught errors
  /// of the app, and [launchMessage] is the message of a notification that
  /// the app showed, if its tap launched the app, or `null`.
  Future<void> start(PushService service) async {
    unawaited(_serviceMessages?.cancel());
    unawaited(_serviceTaps?.cancel());
    unawaited(_serviceAddresses?.cancel());
    _service = service;
    _launchMessage = null;
    _address.value = null;
    // An error of these streams has no caller: it reaches the handlers of
    // the uncaught errors of the app, and the streams go on.
    _serviceMessages = service.messages.listen(_onMessage);
    _serviceTaps = service.taps.listen(_taps.add);
    _serviceAddresses = service.addressChanges.listen(_setAddress);
    _notificationTaps ??= appNotifications
        .tapsOf(_owner)
        .listen((tap) => _taps.add(_messageIn(tap.data)));
    if (_observer == null) {
      final observer = _observer = _ResumeObserver(_askForAddress);
      WidgetsBinding.instance.addObserver(observer);
    }
    _askForAddress();
    PushMessage? launched;
    try {
      launched = await service.launchMessage();
    } on Object catch (error, stackTrace) {
      // A provider that fails here would otherwise keep the app from
      // starting.
      Zone.current.handleUncaughtError(error, stackTrace);
    }
    final tap = appNotifications.launchTapOf(_owner);
    _launchMessage = launched ?? (tap == null ? null : _messageIn(tap.data));
  }

  /// Asks the service for the address while the controller has none, unless
  /// it is asking already.
  void _askForAddress() {
    final service = _service!;
    if (_address.value != null || identical(_asking, service)) return;
    _asking = service;
    unawaited(_answer(service));
  }

  Future<void> _answer(PushService service) async {
    try {
      final address = await service.address();
      // An answer of a service that the controller no longer has, or one
      // after the service told of an address, changes nothing.
      if (address != null &&
          identical(service, _service) &&
          _address.value == null) {
        _setAddress(address);
      }
    } finally {
      if (identical(_asking, service)) _asking = null;
    }
  }

  void _setAddress(String address) {
    if (address == _address.value) return;
    _address.value = address;
    if (kDebugMode) {
      debugPrint('The address of this device for push messages: $address');
    }
  }

  /// Tells of [message], and shows it as a notification of the app if the
  /// platform does not show it and it has a title or a body. An empty title
  /// or body counts as none.
  void _onMessage(PushMessage message) {
    _messages.add(message);
    final title = _textOf(message.title);
    final body = _textOf(message.body);
    final text = title ?? body;
    if (text == null || _service!.platformShowsInForeground) return;
    final json = jsonEncode({
      'id': message.id,
      'title': message.title,
      'body': message.body,
      'data': message.data,
    });
    unawaited(
      appNotifications.show(
        AppNotification(
          // An equal message takes the same id, so its notification takes
          // the place of the one that showed it before rather than adding
          // another, and a negative one, so it never takes the place of a
          // notification of the app.
          id: -1 - (json.hashCode & 0x7FFFFFFF),
          // A message with a body alone shows it as its title.
          title: text,
          body: title == null ? null : body,
          data: {
            ...message.data,
            notificationOwnerKey: _owner,
            _messageKey: json,
          },
        ),
      ),
    );
  }

  /// [text], or `null` if it is empty.
  static String? _textOf(String? text) =>
      text == null || text.isEmpty ? null : text;

  /// The message that [data], the data of a notification that showed a
  /// message, keeps under [_messageKey], or a message with the rest of the
  /// data if the notification lost it.
  static PushMessage _messageIn(Map<String, String> data) =>
      switch (_decoded(data[_messageKey])) {
        {
          'id': final String? id,
          'title': final String? title,
          'body': final String? body,
          'data': final Map<String, Object?> values,
        }
            when values.values.every((value) => value is String) =>
          PushMessage(
            id: id,
            title: title,
            body: body,
            data: Map<String, String>.from(values),
          ),
        _ => PushMessage(
          data: {
            for (final MapEntry(:key, :value) in data.entries)
              if (key != notificationOwnerKey && key != _messageKey) key: value,
          },
        ),
      };

  /// What the JSON [text] holds, or `null` for no text or one that is no
  /// JSON.
  static Object? _decoded(String? text) {
    if (text == null) return null;
    try {
      return jsonDecode(text);
    } on FormatException {
      return null;
    }
  }
}

/// Calls [_onResume] each time the app is resumed, whatever state it was in
/// before.
final class _ResumeObserver with WidgetsBindingObserver {
  _ResumeObserver(this._onResume);

  final VoidCallback _onResume;

  @override
  void didChangeAppLifecycleState(AppLifecycleState state) {
    if (state == AppLifecycleState.resumed) _onResume();
  }
}

/// none to return before that.
///
/// The code of the app does not call it: it uses [appPush].
PushService createPushService() => _pushService;

/// It may run again, as a test does to see what the next launch of the app

Future<void> initPush() async {
  _pushService = await impl0.openFixturePush();
  await appPush.start(_pushService);
}
=== lib/core/push/push_service.dart (role:push) ===
/// A push message: a message that a server sends to this device through the
/// provider of push messages.
final class PushMessage {
  /// Creates the message with its [id], [title], [body] and [data], each if
  /// it has one.
  const PushMessage({this.id, this.title, this.body, this.data = const {}});

  /// The id that the provider gives the message, or `null` if it gives none.
  final String? id;

  /// The title of the message, or `null` for a message without one.
  final String? title;

  /// The text of the message, or `null` for a message without one.
  final String? body;

  /// What the server sent for the app to read, such as the screen that a
  /// tap opens. A value that the platform gives as something other than a
  /// string is its JSON.
  final Map<String, String> data;
}

/// The push messages of the platform, which the provider of push messages
/// implements: the address of the device, the messages that arrive while
/// the app is in the foreground, and the taps of the user on the messages
/// that the platform shows.
///
/// The code of the app does not use it: it uses `appPush` of
/// `app_push.dart`, which calls this service, shows a message in the
/// foreground where the platform does not, and tells of the taps on the
/// messages that it showed.
///
/// Every implementation keeps to this:
/// - The function that creates the service asks the user nothing. It may be
///   called again, and the service that it returns then has the address
///   that the device kept.
/// - The service never asks for the permission to show notifications, which
///   the notifications of the app ask for, and never keeps the notifications
///   of the app from showing while the app is in the foreground.
/// - [address] completes with the address of the device, or with `null`
///   while the platform gives none, and does not throw. It needs no
///   permission. The address stays the same from one launch of the app to
///   the next, unless the platform gives the device another, which
///   [addressChanges] tells of.
/// - [addressChanges] tells of each new address, and of the first address
///   that the device gets after [address] completed with `null`, such as
///   one that comes late on iOS.
/// - [messages] tells of each message that arrives while the app is in the
///   foreground, once.
/// - A value of the data of a message that the platform gives as something
///   other than a string becomes its JSON, as `jsonEncode` writes it.
/// - A message with a title or a body that arrives while the app is in the
///   background, or closed, is shown by the platform, on Android in the
///   channel of the notifications of the app. [taps] tells of each tap on
///   such a message while the app runs, and [launchMessage] is the message
///   whose tap launched the app, which is not among the [taps].
/// - In the foreground, the platform or the provider shows such a message
///   where [platformShowsInForeground] is true, and [taps] then tells of a
///   tap on it too. Where it is false, the service shows nothing of the
///   message, and the app shows it as a notification of its own.
/// - [launchMessage] completes without the network, and does not throw.
abstract interface class PushService {
  /// Whether the platform or the provider shows a message with a title or a
  /// body that arrives while the app is in the foreground. The app shows it
  /// otherwise.
  bool get platformShowsInForeground;

  /// The address of the device, to which a server sends the messages for
  /// this app, or `null` while the platform gives none.
  Future<String?> address();

  /// Tells of each new address of the device, and of the first one after
  /// [address] gave none: a broadcast stream.
  Stream<String> get addressChanges;

  /// Tells of each message that arrives while the app is in the foreground:
  /// a broadcast stream.
  Stream<PushMessage> get messages;

  /// Tells of each tap of the user on a message that the platform showed,
  /// while the app runs: a broadcast stream. The tap that launched the app
  /// is not among them.
  Stream<PushMessage> get taps;

  /// The message whose tap launched the app, or `null` if the app was
  /// launched in another way.
  Future<PushMessage?> launchMessage();
}

import 'dart:async';
import 'dart:convert';
import 'dart:math';

import 'package:fixture_app/core/fixture_rest/fixture_rest_transport.dart'
    as impl0;
import 'package:fixture_app/core/rest/session_tokens.dart';
import 'package:flutter/foundation.dart';

import '../env/app_env.dart';
import 'rest_transport.dart';

/// The REST client of the app. The code of the app makes its requests here,
/// and gets each answer or a [RestFailure].
final AppRestClient appRest = AppRestClient(
  transport: createRestTransport(),
  baseAddress: AppEnv.restBaseUrl,
  tokens: const SessionRestTokens(),
);

/// Why a call failed.
enum RestFailureReason {
  /// A relative path, and no address to join it to:
  /// `REST_BASE_URL` in `.env` is empty, or is no address of a server.
  notConfigured,

  /// The request did not reach a server, or the connection broke.
  network,

  /// No complete answer within the timeout of the attempt, or no token of the
  /// user within [AppRestClient.tokenWait]. It is never repeated: the user
  /// has waited long enough.
  timeout,

  /// The caller cancelled the call with a [RestCancel].
  cancelled,

  /// A 401, after one refresh of the token where the client added one. It
  /// signs nobody out, since a server may refuse for other reasons: the app
  /// decides what it means.
  unauthorized,

  /// A 403.
  forbidden,

  /// A 404.
  notFound,

  /// A 409.
  conflict,

  /// Any other 4xx, or a relative path that climbs out of the address of the
  /// API, which no server got.
  rejected,

  /// A 429.
  tooManyRequests,

  /// A 5xx.
  server,

  /// A body that [RestResponse.decoded] does not take.
  invalidResponse,

  /// Anything else, such as a redirect of a `POST`.
  unknown,
}

/// The only thing that a call of [AppRestClient] throws.
final class RestFailure implements Exception {
  /// Creates the failure.
  const RestFailure(
    this.reason, {
    this.status,
    this.response,
    this.retryAfter,
    this.developerHint,
  });

  /// Why the call failed, which the app shows in a text of its own.
  final RestFailureReason reason;

  /// The status of the answer, if a server answered.
  final int? status;

  /// The answer of a server that refused, whose body may say why:
  /// `failure.response?.decoded(...)`.
  final RestResponse? response;

  /// The wait that the server named, which the client did not take: the
  /// method does not repeat, the wait is too long, or the repeats are over.
  final Duration? retryAfter;

  /// For the developer, in debug mode only. It never has a token.
  final String? developerHint;

  @override
  String toString() => [
    'RestFailure(${reason.name}',
    if (status != null) ', $status',
    if (developerHint != null) ': $developerHint',
    ')',
  ].join();
}

/// What a server answered: the answer of a call with a status of success or
/// with 304, and the refusal in [RestFailure.response].
final class RestResponse {
  /// Creates the response; a test of the code of the app may too.
  const RestResponse({
    required this.status,
    required this.headers,
    required this.bytes,
  });

  /// The status of the answer.
  final int status;

  /// The headers, each name in lower case. [bytes] are always decoded, so a
  /// reply with a `content-encoding` has neither that header here nor its
  /// `content-length`, which would count the coded body.
  final Map<String, String> headers;

  /// The body, decoded of its `content-encoding`.
  final List<int> bytes;

  /// The body as UTF-8.
  String get text => utf8.decode(bytes, allowMalformed: true);

  /// [parse] applied to the JSON of the body, such as
  /// `response.decoded(Note.fromJson)`.
  ///
  /// A body that is no JSON, a body of another type than [parse] takes, and
  /// any error of [parse] fail with [RestFailureReason.invalidResponse], also
  /// an error of the future of an `async` [parse].
  ///
  /// The JSON of a list is a `List<dynamic>` and that of an object a
  /// `Map<String, dynamic>`, whatever they hold. So a function of a list takes
  /// a `List<dynamic>` and casts its items inside:
  /// `(List<dynamic> json) => [for (final item in json) Note.fromJson(item as Map<String, dynamic>)]`.
  /// One that takes a `List<Map<String, dynamic>>` fails with every body.
  T decoded<T, J>(T Function(J json) parse) {
    try {
      final json = jsonDecode(text);
      if (json is! J) {
        throw RestFailure(
          RestFailureReason.invalidResponse,
          status: status,
          response: this,
          developerHint:
              'The JSON of the body is no $J. A list of JSON is a '
              'List<dynamic> and an object a Map<String, dynamic>, so a typed '
              'list needs a cast: take one of those, and cast inside.',
        );
      }
      final parsed = parse(json);
      if (parsed is Future<Object?>) {
        return parsed.catchError(_notFitting) as T;
      }
      return parsed;
    } on RestFailure {
      rethrow;
    } on Object catch (error, stackTrace) {
      _notFitting(error, stackTrace);
    }
  }

  /// Fails with [RestFailureReason.invalidResponse] for [error]. The hint has
  /// its type and not its text, which may quote the body.
  Never _notFitting(Object error, StackTrace stackTrace) =>
      Error.throwWithStackTrace(
        RestFailure(
          RestFailureReason.invalidResponse,
          status: status,
          response: this,
          developerHint: 'The body does not fit: ${error.runtimeType}.',
        ),
        stackTrace,
      );

  @override
  String toString() => 'RestResponse($status)';
}

/// Ends a call, such as one of a screen that the user left: the call fails
/// with [RestFailureReason.cancelled] at once and its connection closes.
final class RestCancel {
  final List<void Function()> _listeners = [];
  bool _isCancelled = false;

  /// Whether [cancel] was called.
  bool get isCancelled => _isCancelled;

  /// Ends the calls that were given this object.
  void cancel() {
    if (_isCancelled) return;
    _isCancelled = true;
    for (final listener in [..._listeners]) {
      listener();
    }
  }
}

/// Who the requests of the app are made for. In an app without sign-in, the
/// client has none.
abstract interface class RestTokens {
  /// The id of the user right now, or `null` when nobody is signed in. A
  /// request of one user never repeats as another.
  String? get owner;

  /// The token of the user, or `null` for no header.
  ///
  /// It fails with a [RestFailure].
  Future<String?> current();

  /// A token to use in place of [rejected], which a server refused, or `null`
  /// when nobody is signed in. Calls at the same time share one refresh.
  ///
  /// It fails with a [RestFailure].
  Future<String?> after(String rejected);
}

/// The headers that go only to the origin they were given for.
const List<String> _originHeaders = [
  'authorization',
  'cookie',
  'proxy-authorization',
];

/// How many times a call repeats after a connection that failed, or after
/// one of [_repeatedStatuses].
const int _maxRepeats = 2;

/// The waits before the repeats, for a server that names none.
const List<Duration> _repeatWaits = [
  Duration(milliseconds: 500),
  Duration(seconds: 1),
];

/// How much a wait of [_repeatWaits] grows at most, so that the devices of
/// many users do not repeat at the same moment.
const int _maxJitterMilliseconds = 250;

/// The longest wait that a server can make a call take before it repeats.
const Duration _maxWait = Duration(seconds: 30);

/// The longest wait of a `Retry-After` that the client tells of, in seconds:
/// a year. A longer one counts as this, so that no number overflows.
const int _maxRetryAfterSeconds = 365 * 24 * 60 * 60;

/// The form of a wait in seconds in `Retry-After`.
final RegExp _retryAfterSeconds = RegExp(r'^\d+$');

/// The statuses after which `GET` and `HEAD` repeat.
const Set<int> _repeatedStatuses = {429, 502, 503, 504};

/// The statuses of a redirect that `GET` and `HEAD` follow.
const Set<int> _redirects = {301, 302, 303, 307, 308};

/// How many redirects a call follows at most.
const int _maxRedirects = 5;

const List<String> _months = [
  'Jan',
  'Feb',
  'Mar',
  'Apr',
  'May',
  'Jun',
  'Jul',
  'Aug',
  'Sep',
  'Oct',
  'Nov',
  'Dec',
];

/// The form of an HTTP date that a server sends today, such as
/// `Sun, 06 Nov 1994 08:49:37 GMT`.
final RegExp _httpDate = RegExp(
  r'^[A-Za-z]{3}, (\d{2}) ([A-Za-z]{3}) (\d{4}) (\d{2}):(\d{2}):(\d{2}) GMT$',
);

/// A path that is an address of its own: only `http://` and `https://` make
/// one, so `//other.example/x` stays a path on the server of the app.
final RegExp _absolute = RegExp(r'^https?://', caseSensitive: false);

/// What ends the path in a relative path.
final RegExp _queryOrFragment = RegExp('[?#]');

/// A dot of a path, encoded.
final RegExp _encodedDot = RegExp('%2e', caseSensitive: false);

/// What a server may take for a slash of a path.
final RegExp _slash = RegExp(r'/|\\|%2f|%5c', caseSensitive: false);

final Random _random = Random();

/// The REST client of the app, [appRest], over the transport of the provider
/// of the REST role.
///
/// What it guarantees is here, so it is the same with every provider:
/// - A path is relative to [baseAddress], with or without a leading slash, and
///   stays under it: one that climbs out with `..` fails with
///   [RestFailureReason.rejected] before anything is sent. An address with
///   `http://` or `https://` goes where it says.
/// - A call fails only with a [RestFailure].
/// - The token of the user goes only to the origin of [baseAddress], and a
///   redirect to another origin takes it away, with the cookies of the caller.
/// - A 401 of a request with that token gets a new token once and repeats
///   once, whatever the method, for the same user only.
/// - `GET` and `HEAD` repeat twice at most after a connection that failed and
///   after 429, 502, 503 and 504, keeping to `Retry-After`. Other methods
///   never repeat, since the server may have done what they asked.
/// - The timeout is per attempt, so a `GET` with its repeats may take longer,
///   and a [RestCancel] ends a call at once.
/// - A debug build prints one line for each attempt, with no token and no
///   query.
final class AppRestClient {
  /// Creates a client. The app has one, [appRest], and a test creates one over
  /// a transport of its own.
  AppRestClient({
    required this._transport,
    required this._baseAddress,
    this._tokens,
    this.timeout = const Duration(seconds: 30),
    this.tokenWait = const Duration(seconds: 10),
  });

  /// How long one attempt of a call takes at most, unless the call names
  /// another time.
  final Duration timeout;

  /// How long the client waits for the token of the user at most, each time
  /// that it needs one, and within the timeout of the attempt. A forced refresh
  /// on a device without a network may take a minute before it fails, and a
  /// request does not wait for that.
  final Duration tokenWait;

  RestTransport _transport;
  String? _baseAddress;
  RestTokens? _tokens;

  /// For a test: replaces what is given, and with [noBaseAddress] or [noTokens]
  /// takes away the address or the tokens. The code of the app never calls it.
  void use({
    RestTransport? transport,
    String? baseAddress,
    RestTokens? tokens,
    bool noBaseAddress = false,
    bool noTokens = false,
  }) {
    _transport = transport ?? _transport;
    _baseAddress = noBaseAddress ? null : baseAddress ?? _baseAddress;
    _tokens = noTokens ? null : tokens ?? _tokens;
  }

  /// Sends a `GET`, which the client repeats as [AppRestClient] tells.
  Future<RestResponse> get(
    String path, {
    Map<String, String>? query,
    Map<String, String>? headers,
    Duration? timeout,
    RestCancel? cancel,
  }) => _request(
    'GET',
    path,
    query: query,
    headers: headers,
    timeout: timeout,
    cancel: cancel,
  );

  /// Sends a `HEAD`, which the client repeats as [AppRestClient] tells.
  Future<RestResponse> head(
    String path, {
    Map<String, String>? query,
    Map<String, String>? headers,
    Duration? timeout,
    RestCancel? cancel,
  }) => _request(
    'HEAD',
    path,
    query: query,
    headers: headers,
    timeout: timeout,
    cancel: cancel,
  );

  /// Sends a `POST` with [json] as its body, or with [bytes].
  Future<RestResponse> post(
    String path, {
    Object? json,
    List<int>? bytes,
    Map<String, String>? query,
    Map<String, String>? headers,
    Duration? timeout,
    RestCancel? cancel,
  }) => _request(
    'POST',
    path,
    json: json,
    bytes: bytes,
    query: query,
    headers: headers,
    timeout: timeout,
    cancel: cancel,
  );

  /// Sends a `PUT` with [json] as its body, or with [bytes].
  Future<RestResponse> put(
    String path, {
    Object? json,
    List<int>? bytes,
    Map<String, String>? query,
    Map<String, String>? headers,
    Duration? timeout,
    RestCancel? cancel,
  }) => _request(
    'PUT',
    path,
    json: json,
    bytes: bytes,
    query: query,
    headers: headers,
    timeout: timeout,
    cancel: cancel,
  );

  /// Sends a `PATCH` with [json] as its body, or with [bytes].
  Future<RestResponse> patch(
    String path, {
    Object? json,
    List<int>? bytes,
    Map<String, String>? query,
    Map<String, String>? headers,
    Duration? timeout,
    RestCancel? cancel,
  }) => _request(
    'PATCH',
    path,
    json: json,
    bytes: bytes,
    query: query,
    headers: headers,
    timeout: timeout,
    cancel: cancel,
  );

  /// Sends a `DELETE`, with [json] as its body or with [bytes] if it has one.
  Future<RestResponse> delete(
    String path, {
    Object? json,
    List<int>? bytes,
    Map<String, String>? query,
    Map<String, String>? headers,
    Duration? timeout,
    RestCancel? cancel,
  }) => _request(
    'DELETE',
    path,
    json: json,
    bytes: bytes,
    query: query,
    headers: headers,
    timeout: timeout,
    cancel: cancel,
  );

  /// Makes the call, and turns any error of another kind into a failure with
  /// the reason [RestFailureReason.unknown]. Its hint has the type of the
  /// error and not its text, which may quote a header.
  Future<RestResponse> _request(
    String method,
    String path, {
    Object? json,
    List<int>? bytes,
    Map<String, String>? query,
    Map<String, String>? headers,
    Duration? timeout,
    RestCancel? cancel,
  }) async {
    try {
      if (json != null && bytes != null) {
        throw const RestFailure(
          RestFailureReason.unknown,
          developerHint: 'A call takes json or bytes, not both.',
        );
      }
      final base = _baseOf(_baseAddress);
      final body = json != null ? utf8.encode(jsonEncode(json)) : bytes;
      return await _Call(
        client: this,
        method: method,
        base: base,
        target: _addressOf(path, query, base),
        headers: {
          for (final MapEntry(:key, :value) in (headers ?? const {}).entries)
            key.toLowerCase(): value,
          if (json != null && _hasNoContentType(headers))
            'content-type': 'application/json',
          // Without it a stack may send the body in chunks, which many upload
          // addresses refuse.
          if (body != null) 'content-length': '${body.length}',
        },
        body: body,
        timeout: timeout ?? this.timeout,
        cancel: cancel,
      ).run();
    } on RestFailure {
      rethrow;
    } on Object catch (error, stackTrace) {
      Error.throwWithStackTrace(
        RestFailure(
          RestFailureReason.unknown,
          developerHint: 'The call ended with a ${error.runtimeType}.',
        ),
        stackTrace,
      );
    }
  }

  bool _hasNoContentType(Map<String, String>? headers) =>
      headers == null ||
      !headers.keys.any((name) => name.toLowerCase() == 'content-type');

  /// The address of [path] with [query]; see [AppRestClient].
  Uri _addressOf(String path, Map<String, String>? query, Uri? base) {
    final Uri address;
    if (_absolute.hasMatch(path)) {
      address = Uri.parse(path);
    } else {
      if (base == null) {
        throw RestFailure(
          RestFailureReason.notConfigured,
          developerHint: _baseProblem(_baseAddress),
        );
      }
      final root = base.toString().replaceFirst(RegExp(r'/+$'), '');
      final relative = path.replaceFirst(RegExp('^/+'), '');
      if (_climbsOut(relative)) {
        throw RestFailure(
          RestFailureReason.rejected,
          developerHint:
              'The path "${relative.split(_queryOrFragment).first}" '
              'climbs out of the address of the API, so nothing was sent. Keep '
              'what a user types out of a path, or encode it with '
              'Uri.encodeComponent.',
        );
      }
      address = Uri.parse(relative.isEmpty ? root : '$root/$relative');
    }
    final added = [
      for (final MapEntry(:key, :value) in (query ?? const {}).entries)
        '${Uri.encodeComponent(key)}=${Uri.encodeComponent(value)}',
    ];
    if (added.isEmpty) return address.removeFragment();
    return address.removeFragment().replace(
      query: [if (address.query.isNotEmpty) address.query, ...added].join('&'),
    );
  }

  /// Whether the [relative] path, joined to the address of the API, climbs out
  /// of the path of that address: `..` that goes above where it started,
  /// written as it is or encoded, with a slash that may be encoded too, since a
  /// server may decode it. So the token of the user goes to no other service
  /// behind one host.
  bool _climbsOut(String relative) {
    final segments = relative
        .split(_queryOrFragment)
        .first
        .replaceAll(_encodedDot, '.')
        .split(_slash);
    var depth = 0;
    for (final segment in segments) {
      if (segment == '..') {
        if (--depth < 0) return true;
      } else if (segment.isNotEmpty && segment != '.') {
        depth++;
      }
    }
    return false;
  }

  /// The address in [value] that paths are relative to, or `null` when it is
  /// none: it has no value, is no address of an `http` or `https` server, or
  /// has a query, a fragment or a user.
  Uri? _baseOf(String? value) {
    final uri = value == null ? null : Uri.tryParse(value);
    if (uri == null ||
        !(uri.isScheme('http') || uri.isScheme('https')) ||
        uri.host.isEmpty ||
        uri.hasQuery ||
        uri.hasFragment ||
        uri.userInfo.isNotEmpty) {
      return null;
    }
    return uri;
  }

  /// What the hint of a relative path says for [value], which [_baseOf] did
  /// not take. It does not quote the value, which may have a user.
  String _baseProblem(String? value) => value == null || value.isEmpty
      ? 'REST_BASE_URL in .env is empty, and the path is relative. Put the '
            'address of your REST API there, such as '
            'https://api.example.com/v1, and start flutter run again. The '
            'section "Values in .env" of the README tells how.'
      : 'REST_BASE_URL in .env is no address of a server: it needs http:// or '
            'https://, a host, and no query, fragment or user.';
}

/// One call of [AppRestClient], over its attempts.
final class _Call {
  _Call({
    required this.client,
    required this.method,
    required this.base,
    required this.target,
    required this.headers,
    required this.body,
    required this.timeout,
    required this.cancel,
  }) : _callerHasAuthorization = headers.containsKey('authorization');

  final AppRestClient client;
  final String method;

  /// The address that paths are relative to, whose origin gets the token.
  final Uri? base;

  /// The address of the next attempt, which a redirect changes.
  Uri target;

  /// The headers that the caller gave, which a redirect to another origin
  /// takes away from.
  final Map<String, String> headers;

  final List<int>? body;
  final Duration timeout;
  final RestCancel? cancel;

  /// A header `authorization` of the caller stays, and the token stays out.
  final bool _callerHasAuthorization;

  /// The user whom the call is made for, once a read of the token told.
  String? _owner;

  /// The token that the last attempt sent, if any.
  String? _sent;

  /// The token of a refresh, for the attempt after it.
  String? _refreshed;

  /// Whether the call has made its one refresh.
  bool _didRefresh = false;

  bool get _isSafe => method == 'GET' || method == 'HEAD';

  /// The attempts of the call; see [AppRestClient].
  Future<RestResponse> run() async {
    var repeats = 0;
    var hops = 0;
    for (;;) {
      final RestReply reply;
      try {
        reply = await _attempt();
      } on RestConnectionFailed catch (failure) {
        if (!_isSafe || repeats == _maxRepeats) {
          throw RestFailure(
            RestFailureReason.network,
            developerHint: failure.hint,
          );
        }
        await _wait(_repeatWait(repeats++));
        continue;
      }

      final status = reply.status;
      final response = RestResponse(
        status: status,
        headers: _ofDecodedBody(reply.headers),
        bytes: reply.bytes,
      );
      if (status == 401 && _sent != null && !_didRefresh) {
        await _refresh(_sent!, response);
        continue;
      }
      if (_redirects.contains(status)) {
        _follow(response, hops++);
        continue;
      }
      if (status == 304 || (status >= 200 && status < 300)) return response;

      final retryAfter = _retryAfterOf(reply.headers);
      if (_isSafe &&
          _repeatedStatuses.contains(status) &&
          repeats < _maxRepeats &&
          (retryAfter == null || retryAfter <= _maxWait)) {
        await _wait(retryAfter ?? _repeatWait(repeats));
        repeats++;
        continue;
      }
      throw RestFailure(
        _reasonOf(status),
        status: status,
        response: response,
        retryAfter: retryAfter,
        developerHint: _reasonOf(status) == RestFailureReason.unknown
            ? 'The server answered with the status $status.'
            : null,
      );
    }
  }

  /// One attempt: the token of the user where it may go, and one exchange,
  /// within the timeout. A debug build prints one line for it.
  Future<RestReply> _attempt() async {
    final limit = _Limit(timeout, cancel);
    final watch = Stopwatch()..start();
    var outcome = RestFailureReason.unknown.name;
    try {
      final sent = await _tokenFor(limit);
      _sent = sent;
      final exchange = RestExchange(
        method: method,
        uri: target,
        headers: {
          ...headers,
          if (sent != null) 'authorization': 'Bearer $sent',
        },
        body: body,
        stop: limit.stop,
      );
      final reply = await limit.race(() => client._transport.send(exchange));
      outcome = '${reply.status}';
      return reply;
    } on RestConnectionFailed {
      outcome = RestFailureReason.network.name;
      rethrow;
    } on RestFailure catch (failure) {
      outcome = failure.reason.name;
      rethrow;
    } finally {
      limit.close();
      if (kDebugMode) {
        // The origin and the path alone: the query may have a key.
        debugPrint(
          'REST $method ${target.origin}${target.path} $outcome '
          '(${watch.elapsedMilliseconds} ms)',
        );
      }
    }
  }

  /// [headers] without what no longer holds of a body that the provider decoded;
  /// see [RestResponse.headers].
  static Map<String, String> _ofDecodedBody(Map<String, String> headers) {
    if (!headers.containsKey('content-encoding')) return headers;
    return {...headers}
      ..remove('content-encoding')
      ..remove('content-length');
  }

  /// The token for the next attempt, or `null` for none: an attempt to
  /// another origin gets none, and neither does a call with a header
  /// `authorization` of its own.
  Future<String?> _tokenFor(_Limit limit) async {
    final tokens = client._tokens;
    final origin = base?.origin;
    if (tokens == null ||
        _callerHasAuthorization ||
        origin == null ||
        target.origin != origin) {
      return null;
    }
    if (_refreshed case final refreshed?) {
      _refreshed = null;
      return refreshed;
    }
    final (owner, token) = await limit.race(
      () => _tokenOfOwner(tokens),
      within: client.tokenWait,
    );
    final before = _owner;
    if (before != null && owner != before) throw _otherUser();
    _owner = owner;
    return token;
  }

  /// The user of [tokens] and that user's token. When the user changes during
  /// the read, as when the app starts, the token may be of the user before, so
  /// it reads once more, and fails when the user changes again.
  Future<(String?, String?)> _tokenOfOwner(RestTokens tokens) async {
    final owner = tokens.owner;
    final token = await tokens.current();
    if (tokens.owner == owner) return (owner, token);
    final next = tokens.owner;
    final again = await tokens.current();
    if (tokens.owner != next) throw _otherUser();
    return (next, again);
  }

  /// Gets the token that replaces [rejected], once in a call, for the same
  /// user. The server that refused it did nothing yet, so every method
  /// repeats after it.
  Future<void> _refresh(String rejected, RestResponse response) async {
    _didRefresh = true;
    final tokens = client._tokens!;
    final limit = _Limit(timeout, cancel);
    try {
      final token = await limit.race(
        () => tokens.after(rejected),
        within: client.tokenWait,
      );
      if (token == null || tokens.owner != _owner) {
        throw _otherUser(response: response);
      }
      _refreshed = token;
    } finally {
      limit.close();
    }
  }

  RestFailure _otherUser({RestResponse? response}) => RestFailure(
    RestFailureReason.unauthorized,
    status: response?.status,
    response: response,
    developerHint:
        'The user of the session changed during the call, so '
        'it did not repeat for the new user.',
  );

  /// Goes on to the address that the redirect in [response] names: for `GET`
  /// and `HEAD` only, since the server may have done what another method
  /// asked. A hop to another origin takes the headers of the caller that are
  /// only for the origin they were given for.
  void _follow(RestResponse response, int hops) {
    RestFailure refused(String hint) => RestFailure(
      RestFailureReason.unknown,
      status: response.status,
      response: response,
      developerHint: hint,
    );
    if (!_isSafe) {
      throw refused(
        'The server answered a $method with a redirect, which only GET and '
        'HEAD follow. Call the address that it names yourself, if you mean to.',
      );
    }
    if (hops == _maxRedirects) {
      throw refused('More than $_maxRedirects redirects.');
    }
    final location = response.headers['location'];
    if (location == null) throw refused('A redirect without a location.');
    final next = target.resolve(location).removeFragment();
    if (!(next.isScheme('http') || next.isScheme('https'))) {
      throw refused('A redirect to an address that is no http or https one.');
    }
    if (next.origin != target.origin) _originHeaders.forEach(headers.remove);
    target = next;
  }

  /// Waits for [duration], which a cancel ends at once.
  Future<void> _wait(Duration duration) async {
    final limit = _Limit(null, cancel);
    final waited = Completer<void>();
    final timer = Timer(duration, waited.complete);
    try {
      await limit.race(() => waited.future);
    } finally {
      timer.cancel();
      limit.close();
    }
  }

  static Duration _repeatWait(int repeats) =>
      _repeatWaits[repeats] +
      Duration(milliseconds: _random.nextInt(_maxJitterMilliseconds));

  /// The wait that [headers] name, in seconds or as an HTTP date, or `null`
  /// without one that the client can read.
  static Duration? _retryAfterOf(Map<String, String> headers) {
    final value = headers['retry-after']?.trim();
    if (value == null) return null;
    if (_retryAfterSeconds.hasMatch(value)) {
      final seconds = int.tryParse(value) ?? _maxRetryAfterSeconds;
      return Duration(seconds: min(seconds, _maxRetryAfterSeconds));
    }
    final match = _httpDate.firstMatch(value);
    final month = match == null ? -1 : _months.indexOf(match[2]!);
    if (match == null || month < 0) return null;
    final wait = DateTime.utc(
      int.parse(match[3]!),
      month + 1,
      int.parse(match[1]!),
      int.parse(match[4]!),
      int.parse(match[5]!),
      int.parse(match[6]!),
    ).difference(DateTime.now());
    return wait.isNegative ? Duration.zero : wait;
  }

  static RestFailureReason _reasonOf(int status) => switch (status) {
    401 => RestFailureReason.unauthorized,
    403 => RestFailureReason.forbidden,
    404 => RestFailureReason.notFound,
    409 => RestFailureReason.conflict,
    429 => RestFailureReason.tooManyRequests,
    >= 400 && < 500 => RestFailureReason.rejected,
    >= 500 && < 600 => RestFailureReason.server,
    _ => RestFailureReason.unknown,
  };
}

/// What ends an attempt or a wait: its timeout, if it has one, and the cancel
/// of the caller. The call ends then, also when a provider does not stop.
final class _Limit {
  _Limit(Duration? timeout, this._cancel) {
    final cancel = _cancel;
    if (cancel != null) {
      if (cancel.isCancelled) {
        _cancelled();
      } else {
        cancel._listeners.add(_cancelled);
      }
    }
    if (timeout != null) {
      _timer = Timer(
        timeout,
        () => _end(
          RestFailure(
            RestFailureReason.timeout,
            developerHint:
                'No complete answer within ${timeout.inMilliseconds} ms.',
          ),
        ),
      );
    }
  }

  final RestCancel? _cancel;
  final Completer<void> _stop = Completer();
  Timer? _timer;
  RestFailure? _ended;
  void Function(RestFailure failure)? _racing;

  /// Completes when the attempt has to stop.
  Future<void> get stop => _stop.future;

  void _cancelled() => _end(const RestFailure(RestFailureReason.cancelled));

  void _end(RestFailure failure) {
    if (_ended != null) return;
    _ended = failure;
    _stop.complete();
    _racing?.call(failure);
  }

  /// What [work] gives, unless the limit ends first or [within] is over.
  /// Whatever [work] does after that goes nowhere.
  Future<T> race<T>(Future<T> Function() work, {Duration? within}) async {
    if (_ended case final failure?) throw failure;
    final result = Completer<T>();
    void end(RestFailure failure) {
      if (!result.isCompleted) result.completeError(failure);
    }

    _racing = end;
    final wait = within == null
        ? null
        : Timer(
            within,
            () => end(
              RestFailure(
                RestFailureReason.timeout,
                developerHint:
                    'The token of the user did not come within '
                    '${within.inMilliseconds} ms.',
              ),
            ),
          );
    unawaited(
      Future<T>.sync(work).then(
        (value) {
          if (!result.isCompleted) result.complete(value);
        },
        onError: (Object error, StackTrace stackTrace) {
          if (!result.isCompleted) result.completeError(error, stackTrace);
        },
      ),
    );
    try {
      return await result.future;
    } finally {
      wait?.cancel();
      _racing = null;
    }
  }

  /// Ends the timer of the limit, once what it limits is over.
  void close() {
    _timer?.cancel();
    _cancel?._listeners.remove(_cancelled);
  }
}

/// The transport of the provider of the REST role. Only the client of the app
/// and a test of the provider call this.
RestTransport createRestTransport() => _restTransport;

final RestTransport _restTransport = impl0.createFixtureRestTransport();
=== lib/core/rest/rest_transport.dart (role:rest) ===
/// One exchange with a server, as the provider of the REST role sends it.
final class RestExchange {
  /// Creates the exchange; only the client of the app does.
  const RestExchange({
    required this.method,
    required this.uri,
    required this.headers,
    required this.stop,
    this.body,
  });

  /// The method, in upper case.
  final String method;

  /// The whole address, with its query.
  final Uri uri;

  /// The headers, each name in lower case, with `content-length` for a body.
  /// They may hold a token.
  final Map<String, String> headers;

  /// The body, or `null` for none.
  final List<int>? body;

  /// Completes when the exchange has to stop: a timeout, or a cancel.
  final Future<void> stop;

  /// Names no header and no query, so that a token never reaches a log.
  @override
  String toString() => 'RestExchange($method ${uri.origin}${uri.path})';
}

/// What a server answered.
final class RestReply {
  /// Creates the reply; a provider does, from what the server answered.
  const RestReply({
    required this.status,
    required this.headers,
    required this.bytes,
  });

  /// The status of the answer.
  final int status;

  /// The headers, each name in lower case, and several values of one header
  /// joined by `, `.
  final Map<String, String> headers;

  /// The body, decoded of its `content-encoding`.
  final List<int> bytes;
}

/// The request did not reach a server, or the connection broke. No other
/// exception of a provider tells that.
final class RestConnectionFailed implements Exception {
  /// Creates the failure with [hint], for the developer of the app.
  const RestConnectionFailed(this.hint);

  /// What went wrong. It has no header, no query and no body.
  final String hint;

  @override
  String toString() => 'RestConnectionFailed: $hint';
}

/// One exchange on the wire, which is all that a provider of the REST role
/// does. `appRest` does the rest with what the provider reports, so every
/// provider keeps the same guarantees.
///
/// Every provider keeps to this. Only a running app shows whether it does:
/// - [send] makes one attempt. A provider repeats nothing.
/// - It follows no redirect: a 3xx is a reply. `appRest` follows them, since
///   it knows which header may go where.
/// - Any status is a reply, and it keeps the headers of the reply, with their
///   names in lower case. [RestReply.bytes] are decoded of `content-encoding`,
///   so a stack that asks for gzip decodes what it gets.
/// - A failure to connect, a TLS handshake or a certificate that fails, and a
///   connection that breaks are [RestConnectionFailed]. On `dart:io` those are
///   `SocketException`, `HttpException` and `TlsException`. Any other error is
///   a bug of the provider, which reaches the caller as the reason `unknown`.
/// - When [RestExchange.stop] completes, it closes the connection at once. It
///   needs no timeout of its own: `appRest` stops every exchange. On `dart:io`,
///   only a TLS handshake that is on its way stays until the server ends it,
///   since `HttpClient` has no way to end one.
/// - On `dart:io`, one that keeps an `HttpClient` makes a new one when
///   `HttpOverrides` change, as they do when a test starts or closes a
///   `RestTestServer`.
/// - It sends [RestExchange.uri] as it is, with those headers and that body.
///   Its stack may add the headers of the transport itself, such as `host`,
///   `user-agent` and `accept-encoding`. It adds no credential or cookie, and
///   keeps none between two calls.
/// - It writes no header, no query and no body to a log or a hint.
/// - Its function takes no arguments, never throws and does not wait, and the
///   implementation works from the first call.
abstract interface class RestTransport {
  /// Sends [exchange] and completes with what the server answered.
  Future<RestReply> send(RestExchange exchange);
}
=== lib/core/rest/session_tokens.dart (role:rest) ===
import '../auth/app_session.dart';
import 'app_rest.dart';

/// The token of the user of [appSession], for the requests of the app.
final class SessionRestTokens implements RestTokens {
  /// Creates the tokens of the session of the app.
  const SessionRestTokens();

  @override
  String? get owner => appSession.value.uid;

  @override
  Future<String?> current() => _read(appSession.userToken);

  @override
  Future<String?> after(String rejected) =>
      _read(() => appSession.userTokenAfter(rejected));

  Future<String?> _read(Future<String?> Function() read) async {
    try {
      return await read();
    } on AuthFailure catch (failure, stackTrace) {
      Error.throwWithStackTrace(
        RestFailure(
          _reasonOf(failure.reason),
          developerHint: failure.developerHint,
        ),
        stackTrace,
      );
    }
  }

  /// A session that ended on the server, and a disabled account, leave nobody
  /// signed in by now, so the request has no user any more.
  static RestFailureReason _reasonOf(AuthFailureReason reason) =>
      switch (reason) {
        AuthFailureReason.network => RestFailureReason.network,
        AuthFailureReason.tooManyAttempts => RestFailureReason.tooManyRequests,
        AuthFailureReason.recentSignInRequired ||
        AuthFailureReason.userDisabled => RestFailureReason.unauthorized,
        _ => RestFailureReason.unknown,
      };
}
=== lib/core/router/app_router.dart (role:router) ===
import 'package:flutter/widgets.dart';

import 'app_router_factory.dart';
import 'navigation.dart';

/// The router of the app, created on first use.
final AppRouter appRouter = createAppRouter();

/// The router of the app: its configuration for `MaterialApp.router` and the
/// navigation that screens use through `context.nav`.
abstract interface class AppRouter {
  /// The configuration that `MaterialApp.router` takes as `routerConfig`.
  RouterConfig<Object> get config;

  /// The navigator that [context] navigates with.
  AppNavigator navigatorOf(BuildContext context);
}

/// Moves between the locations of the app.
abstract interface class AppNavigator {
  /// Shows [location] with the chain of its parents below it as the stack.
  ///
  /// In the main navigation, it selects the branch of [location] and makes
  /// the chain its stack; for a location outside the main navigation, the
  /// chain replaces the whole stack, main navigation included.
  void go(AppLocation location);

  /// Shows [location] on top of the current stack and completes with the
  /// value the page returns when it closes.
  ///
  /// In the main navigation, the current stack is that of the selected
  /// branch, whichever branch [location] belongs to, and the selected
  /// destination stays; a location outside the main navigation is shown
  /// over it. A location in the main navigation goes only on top of the
  /// main navigation itself: from a page shown over it, this throws a
  /// [StateError] and leaves the stack as it is, so [go] to the location
  /// instead. From a page with no main navigation below it, the main
  /// navigation comes on top with the location.
  Future<T?> push<T extends Object?>(AppLocation location);

  /// Replaces the top of the current stack with [location].
  ///
  /// As with [push], a location in the main navigation cannot replace a
  /// page shown over the main navigation: this throws a [StateError] and
  /// leaves the stack as it is, so [go] to the location instead.
  void replace(AppLocation location);
}

import 'package:fixture_app/core/fixture_analytics/fixture_analytics.dart';

import 'package:fixture_app/features/fake_feature/fixture_home_screen.dart'

/// Creates the router of the app: plain navigators whose stacks are lists
/// of locations (fixture).
AppRouter createAppRouter() => _FixtureRouter();

final class _FixtureRouter implements AppRouter {
  final _FixtureDelegate _delegate = _FixtureDelegate();

  /// The configuration of the router, which hears of the back button of
  /// the system too.
  @override
  late final RouterConfig<Object> config = RouterConfig(
    routerDelegate: _delegate,
    backButtonDispatcher: RootBackButtonDispatcher(),
  );

  @override
  AppNavigator navigatorOf(BuildContext context) => _delegate;
}

/// The locations of the destinations of the main navigation, in order, if
/// the app has a layout: each branch of the main navigation starts on one.
const List<AppLocation> _destinations = [];

/// The main navigation, as an entry of the stack of the root navigator.
const _mainNavigation = _MainNavigation();

/// The page of the fallback start screen of the app entry, which the
/// router shows at `/` in an app that no route starts, or none in an app
/// that starts on its start route.
const List<Page<Object?>> _fallbackPages = [];

final class _MainNavigation {
  const _MainNavigation();
}

final class _FixtureDelegate extends RouterDelegate<Object>
    with ChangeNotifier
    implements AppNavigator {
  /// Creates the delegate, which shows the start route of the app, if it
  /// has one, and else the fallback screen.
  _FixtureDelegate() {
    for (final location in <AppLocation>[const FakeFeatureHomeLocation()]) {
      _show(location);
    }
  }

  final GlobalKey<NavigatorState> _navigatorKey = GlobalKey();

  final List<NavigatorObserver> _observers = _newObservers();

  final _screenListeners = <void Function(String? route, String location)>[
    noteFixtureScreen,
    noteFixtureScreenLog,
  ];

  /// The stack of the root navigator: locations, and the main navigation.
  final List<Object> _stack = [];

  /// The stack of each branch of the main navigation.
  final List<List<AppLocation>> _branches = [
    for (final destination in _destinations) [destination],
  ];

  /// The observers of the navigator of each branch that was selected.
  final Map<int, List<NavigatorObserver>> _branchObservers = {};

  /// The key of the navigator of each branch that was selected.
  final Map<int, GlobalKey<NavigatorState>> _branchNavigators = {};

  /// The branch of the main navigation that is selected.
  int _selected = 0;

  final Map<AppLocation, Completer<Object?>> _results = {};

  /// The page on top, as the listeners of the screen last heard of it, or
  /// `null` before the first screen; see [_top].
  (int, AppLocation?)? _shown;

  /// The page on top: the branch it is in, or -1 for the root navigator,
  /// and its location, or `null` for the fallback screen.
  (int, AppLocation?) get _top {
    final top = _stack.lastOrNull;
    if (top == null) return (-1, null);
    if (top case final AppLocation location) return (-1, location);
    return (_selected, _branches[_selected].last);
  }

  /// Whether the main navigation is on top of the root navigator.
  bool get _onMainNavigation =>
      _stack.isNotEmpty && identical(_stack.last, _mainNavigation);

  /// Whether the fallback screen is at the bottom of the root navigator, in
  /// an app that no route starts: when the stack is empty, and below the
  /// pages that pushes showed over the fallback screen.
  bool get _overFallback =>
      _fallbackPages.isNotEmpty && _stack.every(_results.containsKey);

  /// Tells the router of a change of the stacks, as each navigation does.
  ///
  /// When the main navigation has left the stack, the branches get new
  /// navigators, with observers of their own, when they are selected next.
  /// The page of the main navigation that left stays in the tree until the
  /// transition to the page that took its place is over, with the
  /// navigators that it had: a main navigation that comes back sooner
  /// would have their keys in the tree twice.
  @override
  void notifyListeners() {
    if (!_stack.contains(_mainNavigation)) {
      _branchNavigators.clear();
      _branchObservers.clear();
    }
    super.notifyListeners();
  }

  @override
  Widget build(BuildContext context) {
    _showScreen();
    return Navigator(
      key: _navigatorKey,
      observers: _observers,
      pages: [
        if (_overFallback) ..._fallbackPages,
        for (final entry in _stack)
          if (entry case final AppLocation location)
            _page(location)
          else
            MaterialPage<Object?>(
              key: ObjectKey(entry),
              child: _shell(_selected, _select, _branchesWidget()),
            ),
      ],
      onDidRemovePage: (page) => _removed(_stack, page),
    );
  }

  /// The navigators of the branches of the main navigation, of which the
  /// selected one shows: a branch gets its navigator when it is first
  /// selected, and keeps it until the main navigation leaves the stack; see
  /// [notifyListeners].
  Widget _branchesWidget() => IndexedStack(
    index: _selected,
    children: [
      for (final (index, stack) in _branches.indexed)
        if (index == _selected || _branchObservers.containsKey(index))
          Navigator(
            key: _branchNavigators.putIfAbsent(index, GlobalKey.new),
            observers: _branchObservers.putIfAbsent(index, _newObservers),
            pages: [for (final location in stack) _page(location)],
            onDidRemovePage: (page) => _removed(stack, page),
          )
        else
          const SizedBox.shrink(),
    ],
  );

  /// The page of [location], which completes the push of the location with
  /// the value it pops with.
  Page<Object?> _page(AppLocation location) => _LocationPage(
    location,
    (value) => _results.remove(location)?.complete(value),
  );

  /// Takes [page], which its navigator removed, such as when it popped, out
  /// of [stack].
  void _removed(List<Object> stack, Page<Object?> page) {
    if (stack.remove((page.key as ObjectKey?)?.value)) notifyListeners();
  }

  /// Selects the branch at [index], as the layout does when the user
  /// selects its destination.
  void _select(int index) {
    _selected = index;
    notifyListeners();
  }

  /// Tells the listeners of the screen about the page on top when it is
  /// another than they last heard of: a location of the root navigator or
  /// of the selected branch, or the fallback screen at `/`.
  ///
  /// It calls each listener on its own: what one throws keeps no other
  /// from hearing the screen, and reaches neither the router nor the
  /// handlers of the errors of the app. In debug mode it is printed.
  void _showScreen() {
    final top = _top;
    if (top == _shown) return;
    _shown = top;
    final (_, location) = top;
    for (final listener in _screenListeners) {
      try {
        listener(location?.routeName, location?.path ?? '/');
      } on Object catch (error) {
        if (kDebugMode) debugPrint('A listener of the screen failed: $error');
      }
    }
  }

  /// The branch of the main navigation of [location], or `null` if it is
  /// outside the main navigation.
  int? _branchOf(AppLocation location) {
    final name = location.chain.first.routeName;
    final index = _destinations.indexWhere(
      (destination) => destination.routeName == name,
    );
    return index < 0 ? null : index;
  }

  /// Makes the chain of [location] the stack: that of its branch, which
  /// the main navigation shows, if it is in the main navigation.
  void _show(AppLocation location) {
    _stack.clear();
    final branch = _branchOf(location);
    if (branch == null) {
      _stack.addAll(location.chain);
    } else {
      _showMainNavigation(branch, location);
    }
  }

  /// Puts the main navigation on top of the stack with the branch [branch]
  /// selected, whose stack becomes the chain of [location].
  void _showMainNavigation(int branch, AppLocation location) {
    _selected = branch;
    _branches[branch]
      ..clear()
      ..addAll(location.chain);
    _stack.add(_mainNavigation);
  }

  /// Throws a [StateError] instead of showing [location] of the branch
  /// [branch] of the main navigation on top of a page shown over the main
  /// navigation, as [method] would: a location in the main navigation goes
  /// only on top of the main navigation itself.
  void _checkMainNavigation(AppLocation location, int? branch, String method) {
    if (branch == null ||
        _onMainNavigation ||
        !_stack.contains(_mainNavigation)) {
      return;
    }
    throw StateError(
      'Cannot $method ${location.path}: it is in the main navigation, and a '
      'page is shown over the main navigation. Use go() to show it there.',
    );
  }

  /// Closes the route on top when the user presses the back button of the
  /// system, with [NavigatorState.maybePop] of the navigators that the user
  /// sees, the innermost first. A navigator tells [_removed] about a page
  /// that it closes.
  @override
  Future<bool> popRoute() async {
    for (final navigator in _shownNavigators()) {
      if (await navigator.maybePop()) return true;
    }
    return false;
  }

  /// The navigators that the user sees, the innermost first: that of the
  /// selected branch of the main navigation, unless a route of the root
  /// navigator, such as a dialog, covers the main navigation, and the root
  /// navigator.
  List<NavigatorState> _shownNavigators() {
    final branch = _branchNavigators[_selected]?.currentState;
    return [
      if (branch != null && (ModalRoute.of(branch.context)?.isCurrent ?? false))
        branch,
      ?_navigatorKey.currentState,
    ];
  }

  @override
  Future<void> setNewRoutePath(Object configuration) async {}

  @override
  void go(AppLocation location) {
    _show(location);
    notifyListeners();
  }

  @override
  Future<T?> push<T extends Object?>(AppLocation location) {
    final branch = _branchOf(location);
    _checkMainNavigation(location, branch, 'push');
    if (branch == null) {
      _stack.add(location);
    } else if (_onMainNavigation) {
      _branches[_selected].add(location);
    } else {
      _showMainNavigation(branch, location);
    }
    final result = Completer<Object?>();
    _results[location] = result;
    notifyListeners();
    return result.future.then((value) => value as T?);
  }

  @override
  void replace(AppLocation location) {
    final branch = _branchOf(location);
    _checkMainNavigation(location, branch, 'replace');
    // A page that takes the place of one that a push showed closes without
    // the pages below it too, so it is one that a push showed.
    final (_, replaced) = _top;
    if (branch != null && _onMainNavigation) {
      _branches[_selected]
        ..removeLast()
        ..add(location);
    } else {
      if (_stack.isNotEmpty) _stack.removeLast();
      if (branch == null) {
        _stack.add(location);
      } else {
        _showMainNavigation(branch, location);
      }
    }
    if (_results.containsKey(replaced)) {
      _results[location] = Completer<Object?>();
    }
    notifyListeners();
  }
}

/// The page of a location, whose route tells [onPopped] the value it pops
/// with.
final class _LocationPage extends MaterialPage<Object?> {
  _LocationPage(AppLocation location, this.onPopped)
    : super(
        key: ObjectKey(location),
        name: location.routeName,
        child: _screen(location),
      );

  final void Function(Object? value) onPopped;

  @override
  Route<Object?> createRoute(BuildContext context) {
    final route = super.createRoute(context);
    unawaited(route.popped.then(onPopped));
    return route;
  }
}

/// Creates the observers of a navigator. An observer can watch only one
/// navigator, so each navigator gets instances of its own.
List<NavigatorObserver> _newObservers() => [
  for (final create in <NavigatorObserver Function()>[() => FixtureObserver()])
    create(),
];

/// The layout of the main navigation around [body], the navigator of the
/// selected branch, whose index is [index]; [onSelect] selects another.
Widget _shell(int index, ValueChanged<int> onSelect, Widget body) => body;

    screen1.FixtureDetailsScreen(id: id, tab: tab),

  FakeSecondMemberCardLocation() => const screen4.FixtureMemberCardScreen(),
  FakeSecondLoungeLocation() => const screen4.FixtureLoungeScreen(),
  FakeSecondLoungeSeatLocation() => const screen4.FixtureLoungeSeatScreen(),
  FakeOnboardingOnboardingLocation() => const screen5.FixtureOnboardingScreen(),
};

=== lib/core/router/navigation.dart (role:router) ===
import 'package:flutter/widgets.dart';

import 'app_router.dart';

/// A place in the app: a route with the values of its parameters.
///
/// Every route of the app has a location class, such as
/// `HomeDetailsLocation` for the route `home.details`.
sealed class AppLocation {
  /// Allows the location classes to have constant constructors.
  const AppLocation();

  /// The full name of the route, such as `home.details`.
  String get routeName;

  /// The location as a path with its query, such as `/home/details/5`.
  String get path;

  /// The location of the parent route, or `null` for a top-level route.
  AppLocation? get parent => null;

  /// The locations from the top-level route down to this one: the stack
  /// that [NavLink.go] shows.
  List<AppLocation> get chain => [...?parent?.chain, this];
}

/// A location and the context that navigates to it, as the routes of
/// `context.nav` return them.
final class NavLink {
  /// Creates the link to [location] from [context].
  const NavLink(this._context, this.location);

  final BuildContext _context;

  /// The location to navigate to.
  final AppLocation location;

  /// Shows [location] with the chain of its parents below it as the stack.
  void go() => appRouter.navigatorOf(_context).go(location);

  /// Shows [location] on top of the current stack and completes with the
  /// value the page returns when it closes.
  Future<T?> push<T extends Object?>() =>
      appRouter.navigatorOf(_context).push<T>(location);

  /// Replaces the top of the current stack with [location].
  void replace() => appRouter.navigatorOf(_context).replace(location);
}

String _withQuery(String path, Map<String, String?> query) {
  query.removeWhere((key, value) => value == null);
  return query.isEmpty ? path : '$path?${Uri(queryParameters: query).query}';
}
