import 'package:flutter/material.dart';

/// The colour that the themes of the fixture derive their colours from: one
/// of its own at first, so that its themes differ from those that Flutter
/// gives an app without a theme. A test changes it, and the root of the
/// app, which reads it through [FixtureSeedScope], rebuilds in the new
/// colours.
final fixtureSeed = ValueNotifier<Color>(const Color(0xFF00695C));

/// Gives the root of the app the colour of the seed of the fixture, and
/// rebuilds the root when the colour changes. The fixture puts it around
/// the root.
class FixtureSeedScope extends InheritedNotifier<ValueNotifier<Color>> {
  /// Creates the scope of [notifier] around [child].
  const FixtureSeedScope({
    required ValueNotifier<Color> super.notifier,
    required super.child,
    super.key,
  });

  /// The colour of the scope around [context]; the widget of [context]
  /// rebuilds when the colour changes.
  static Color of(BuildContext context) => context
      .dependOnInheritedWidgetOfExactType<FixtureSeedScope>()!
      .notifier!
      .value;
}

/// [context], the context of the root of the app.
ThemeData createLightTheme(BuildContext context) =>
    _themeOf(context, Brightness.light);

/// The dark theme of the fixture, in the colours of the seed around
/// [context], the context of the root of the app.
ThemeData createDarkTheme(BuildContext context) =>
    _themeOf(context, Brightness.dark);

ThemeData _themeOf(BuildContext context, Brightness brightness) => ThemeData(
  colorScheme: ColorScheme.fromSeed(
    seedColor: FixtureSeedScope.of(context),
    brightness: brightness,
  ),
);
=== lib/core/theme/theme_mode.dart (role:theme) ===
import 'package:flutter/material.dart';

import '../preferences/app_preferences.dart';

/// The theme mode that the user selected for the app, which the app
/// remembers between its launches: whether the app is light, dark, or
/// follows the device.
///
/// The root of the app reads it through [AppThemeModeScope], and rebuilds
/// in the new mode when the choice changes.
final appThemeMode = AppThemeModeController._();

/// Keeps the theme mode that the user selected for the app, tells its
/// listeners when the mode changes, and saves each choice in the
/// preferences of the app. The app has one, [appThemeMode].
final class AppThemeModeController extends ChangeNotifier {
  AppThemeModeController._();

  /// The key of the mode in the preferences, which keep it by its name.
  static const _key = 'theme.mode';

  /// The preferences that remember the mode, once the app opened them.
  AppPreferences? _preferences;

  ThemeMode _mode = ThemeMode.system;

  /// The mode of the app: [ThemeMode.system], which follows the device,
  /// unless the user selected another.
  ThemeMode get value => _mode;

  /// Chooses [mode] as the mode of the app, at once, and saves the choice:
  /// the future completes once the mode is saved. Until the app opened its
  /// preferences for the first time, it changes only memory; from then on
  /// it saves through the preferences that the app opened last.
  ///
  /// If the preferences fail to save the mode, the future completes with
  /// their error. The app is in [mode] by then and stays in it while it
  /// runs, but its next launch has the mode that was saved before. The
  /// same choice again saves it.
  Future<void> choose(ThemeMode mode) async {
    if (mode != _mode) {
      _mode = mode;
      notifyListeners();
    }
    await _preferences?.setString(_key, mode.name);
  }

  /// Takes the mode that [preferences] have saved, and keeps the current
  /// one when they have none, or one that the app does not know. It keeps
  /// [preferences] for the choices to come.
  void _restore(AppPreferences preferences) {
    _preferences = preferences;
    final saved = ThemeMode.values.asNameMap()[preferences.getString(_key)];
    if (saved == null || saved == _mode) return;
    _mode = saved;
    notifyListeners();
  }
}

/// Restores the theme mode that the user selected from [preferences], which
/// the app opened, and keeps them for the choices to come: a restorer of
/// the preferences, which the app calls before its first frame.
void restoreAppThemeMode(AppPreferences preferences) =>
    appThemeMode._restore(preferences);

/// Gives the root of the app the theme mode that the user selected, and
/// rebuilds the root, and any other widget below that reads the mode, when
/// the choice changes.
class AppThemeModeScope extends InheritedNotifier<AppThemeModeController> {
  /// Creates the scope of [notifier] around [child].
  const AppThemeModeScope({
    required AppThemeModeController super.notifier,
    required super.child,
    super.key,
  });

  /// The theme mode that the user selected; the widget of [context]
  /// rebuilds when the choice changes.
  static ThemeMode of(BuildContext context) => context
      .dependOnInheritedWidgetOfExactType<AppThemeModeScope>()!
      .notifier!
      .value;
}

import 'package:fixture_app/core/router/fixture_annotations.dart';
import 'package:flutter/material.dart';

import '../../core/onboarding/onboarding_steps.dart';

/// The onboarding of the fixture: a first page of its own, a page for each
/// step of [appOnboardingSteps] that its showIf keeps when the screen
/// opens, in the order of the list, and a last page of its own. The pages
/// turn with a swipe, and the button of the action of a step, or that of
/// its later, shows the next page.
@FixtureScreen('fake_onboarding.onboarding')
class FixtureOnboardingScreen extends StatefulWidget {
  /// Creates the screen.
  const FixtureOnboardingScreen({super.key});

  @override
  State<FixtureOnboardingScreen> createState() =>
      _FixtureOnboardingScreenState();
}

class _FixtureOnboardingScreenState extends State<FixtureOnboardingScreen> {
  final _controller = PageController();

  /// The steps that the screen shows: those of [appOnboardingSteps] that
  /// their showIf keeps when the screen opens. A step stays while the
  /// screen is shown, also once its showIf would leave it out.
  late final List<OnboardingStep> _steps = [
    for (final step in appOnboardingSteps)
      if (step.showIf?.call() ?? true) step,
  ];

  /// Whether the action of a step runs, while the buttons of the steps take
  /// no tap.
  var _running = false;

  @override
  void dispose() {
    _controller.dispose();
    super.dispose();
  }

  /// Shows the page after the one that the user sees.
  void _next() {
    _controller.nextPage(
      duration: const Duration(milliseconds: 300),
      curve: Curves.easeOut,
    );
  }

  /// Calls the action of [step] with the context of the screen and shows
  /// the next page once its future completes, also when it fails: the error
  /// goes on to the handlers of the uncaught errors of the app.
  Future<void> _run(OnboardingStep step) async {
    setState(() => _running = true);
    try {
      await step.run(context);
    } finally {
      if (mounted) {
        setState(() => _running = false);
        _next();
      }
    }
  }

  @override
  Widget build(BuildContext context) => Scaffold(
    body: SafeArea(
      child: PageView(
        controller: _controller,
        children: [
          const _OwnPage('The onboarding of the fixture'),
          for (final step in _steps)
            _StepPage(
              step: step,
              onAction: _running ? null : () => _run(step),
              onLater: _running ? null : _next,
            ),
          const _OwnPage('The onboarding of the fixture is over'),
        ],
      ),
    ),
  );
}

/// A page of the onboarding of the fixture itself, with [title].
class _OwnPage extends StatelessWidget {
  const _OwnPage(this.title);

  final String title;

  @override
  Widget build(BuildContext context) => Center(child: Text(title));
}

/// The page of [step]: its icon, its title as a header for a screen
/// reader, its text, the button of its action, which calls [onAction], and
/// that of its later, if it has one, which calls [onLater]. The labels are
/// read each time the page builds, so they follow the language of the app.
class _StepPage extends StatelessWidget {
  const _StepPage({
    required this.step,
    required this.onAction,
    required this.onLater,
  });

  final OnboardingStep step;
  final VoidCallback? onAction;
  final VoidCallback? onLater;

  @override
  Widget build(BuildContext context) {
    final later = step.later;
    return Padding(
      padding: const EdgeInsets.all(24),
      child: Column(
        mainAxisAlignment: MainAxisAlignment.center,
        children: [
          Icon(step.icon, size: 48),
          const SizedBox(height: 16),
          // A node of its own, which a screen reader announces as a header.
          Semantics(
            container: true,
            header: true,
            child: Text(step.title(context)),
          ),
          const SizedBox(height: 8),
          Text(step.text(context)),
          const SizedBox(height: 24),
          FilledButton(onPressed: onAction, child: Text(step.action(context))),
          if (later != null)
            TextButton(onPressed: onLater, child: Text(later(context))),
        ],
      ),
    );
  }
}

=== test/core/app/status_bar_icons_test.dart (role:app_entry) ===
import 'package:flutter/material.dart';
import 'package:flutter/services.dart';
import 'package:flutter_test/flutter_test.dart';
import 'package:fixture_app/core/app/status_bar_icons.dart';

/// What the widget asks the system for on a screen in [theme].
Future<SystemUiOverlayStyle> _styleIn(
  WidgetTester tester,
  ThemeData theme,
) async {
  await tester.pumpWidget(
    MaterialApp(
      theme: theme,
      home: const StatusBarIcons(child: Text('A screen')),
    ),
  );
  return tester
      .widget<AnnotatedRegion<SystemUiOverlayStyle>>(
        find.descendant(
          of: find.byType(StatusBarIcons),
          matching: find.byType(AnnotatedRegion<SystemUiOverlayStyle>),
        ),
      )
      .value;
}

void main() {
  testWidgets('asks for dark icons on a light theme', (tester) async {
    final style = await _styleIn(tester, ThemeData());

    expect(style.statusBarIconBrightness, Brightness.dark);
    expect(style.statusBarBrightness, Brightness.light);
    expect(find.text('A screen'), findsOneWidget);
  });

  testWidgets('asks for light icons on a dark theme', (tester) async {
    final style = await _styleIn(
      tester,
      ThemeData(brightness: Brightness.dark),
    );

    expect(style.statusBarIconBrightness, Brightness.light);
    expect(style.statusBarBrightness, Brightness.dark);
  });

  testWidgets('leaves the navigation bar of the system as it is', (
    tester,
  ) async {
    final style = await _styleIn(tester, ThemeData());

    expect(style.systemNavigationBarColor, isNull);
    expect(style.systemNavigationBarIconBrightness, isNull);
  });
}
=== test/core/rest/app_rest_test.dart (role:rest) ===
import 'package:flutter_test/flutter_test.dart';
import 'package:fixture_app/core/rest/app_rest.dart';

import 'rest_test_server.dart';

void main() {
  late RestTestServer server;
  late AppRestClient client;

  setUp(() async {
    server = await RestTestServer.start();
    client = AppRestClient(
      transport: createRestTransport(),
      baseAddress: '${server.address}/v1',
    );
  });

  tearDown(() => server.close());

  test(
    'a relative path reaches the address, and the body is decoded',
    () async {
      server.answerJson('GET', '/v1/notes', ['a', 'b']);

      final notes = await client.get('notes', query: {'page': '1'});

      expect(notes.decoded((List<Object?> json) => json.length), 2);
      expect(server.requests.single.uri.toString(), '/v1/notes?page=1');
    },
  );

  test('a 404 fails with notFound', () async {
    await expectLater(
      client.get('notes'),
      throwsA(
        isA<RestFailure>()
            .having(
              (failure) => failure.reason,
              'reason',
              RestFailureReason.notFound,
            )
            .having((failure) => failure.status, 'status', 404),
      ),
    );
  });
}
=== test/core/rest/rest_test_server.dart (role:rest) ===
import 'dart:convert';
import 'dart:io';

import 'package:flutter_test/flutter_test.dart';

/// A request that a [RestTestServer] got.
final class RestTestRequest {
  /// Creates the note of a request.
  const RestTestRequest({
    required this.method,
    required this.uri,
    required this.headers,
    required this.bytes,
  });

  /// The method.
  final String method;

  /// The path and the query.
  final Uri uri;

  /// The headers, each name in lower case.
  final Map<String, String> headers;

  /// The body.
  final List<int> bytes;

  /// The body as UTF-8.
  String get body => utf8.decode(bytes);
}

/// A local server for the tests of the app, for code that makes requests
/// through `appRest`.
///
/// `flutter test` has two traps for a request, and this server is the answer
/// to the first:
/// - The binding of the tests gives every `HttpClient` a stand-in that answers
///   400 to everything, which would reach the code as the reason `rejected`.
///   [start] takes the stand-in away, and the [close] of the last server that
///   is open puts it back.
/// - Inside `testWidgets`, the time is fake, and a real socket never completes
///   there. Make the request in `tester.runAsync`, and let it end before the
///   test does, since the timer of a request that is still running fails the
///   test. A plain `test()` runs in real time and needs nothing.
///
/// Files of tests run at once, so a server takes a free port: give the client
/// [address].
final class RestTestServer {
  RestTestServer._(this._server) {
    _server.listen(_serve, onError: errors.add);
  }

  /// Starts a server on a free port of this machine.
  static Future<RestTestServer> start() async {
    TestWidgetsFlutterBinding.ensureInitialized();
    final server = await HttpServer.bind(InternetAddress.loopbackIPv4, 0);
    if (_open++ == 0) {
      _overrides = HttpOverrides.current;
      HttpOverrides.global = null;
    }
    return RestTestServer._(server);
  }

  /// How many servers are open: the stand-in comes back with the last close.
  static int _open = 0;

  /// What [start] took away.
  static HttpOverrides? _overrides;

  final HttpServer _server;
  bool _closed = false;
  final Map<String, Future<void> Function(HttpRequest request)> _answers = {};

  /// The requests that the server got in full, in order.
  final List<RestTestRequest> requests = [];

  /// What went wrong with a request, such as a client that left during its
  /// upload, which is what a cancel does.
  final List<Object> errors = [];

  /// The address of the server.
  String get address => 'http://${_server.address.address}:${_server.port}';

  /// Answers a request of [method] for [path] with [handler], which closes the
  /// response. Any other request gets a 404.
  void answer(
    String method,
    String path,
    Future<void> Function(HttpRequest request) handler,
  ) => _answers['$method $path'] = handler;

  /// Answers a request of [method] for [path] with [value] as JSON.
  void answerJson(
    String method,
    String path,
    Object? value, {
    int status = 200,
  }) => answer(method, path, (request) async {
    request.response
      ..statusCode = status
      ..headers.contentType = ContentType.json
      ..write(jsonEncode(value));
    await request.response.close();
  });

  /// Stops the server. The last server that is open puts back what [start]
  /// took away.
  Future<void> close() async {
    if (_closed) return;
    _closed = true;
    await _server.close(force: true);
    if (--_open == 0) HttpOverrides.global = _overrides;
  }

  Future<void> _serve(HttpRequest request) async {
    try {
      await _answer(request);
    } on IOException catch (error) {
      errors.add(error);
    }
  }

  Future<void> _answer(HttpRequest request) async {
    final bytes = await request.fold<List<int>>(
      [],
      (all, part) => all..addAll(part),
    );
    final headers = <String, String>{};
    request.headers.forEach(
      (name, values) => headers[name] = values.join(', '),
    );
    requests.add(
      RestTestRequest(
        method: request.method,
        uri: request.uri,
        headers: headers,
        bytes: bytes,
      ),
    );
    final answer = _answers['${request.method} ${request.uri.path}'];
    if (answer != null) return answer(request);
    request.response.statusCode = HttpStatus.notFound;
    await request.response.close();
  }
}
=== tool/write_env.dart (role:ci) ===
// Writes `.env` for a job of CI, from `.env.example` and the variables of the
// process. No value in the file is a secret: every value ends up in the app.
//
// A key whose variable has a value gets it, in one of the two forms of a value
// of `.env`: as it is, or in double quotes when it has white space, `#` or a
// quote. Any other line stays as `.env.example` has it. A value with a double
// quote, a control character or a line separator fits neither form, and could
// add a line of its own, so the script stops and writes nothing. It never prints
// a value.
//
// It never replaces a value. When `.env` has one, the script stops and leaves
// the file as it is, and a developer who wants it written again deletes it. A
// missing `.env`, or one without values such as a copy of `.env.example`, is
// what a job of CI starts from.
import 'dart:convert';
import 'dart:io';

final _key = RegExp(r'^\s*([A-Za-z_][A-Za-z0-9_]*)\s*=');
final _unsafe = RegExp(r'["\x00-\x1F\x7F\u2028\u2029]');
final _quoted = RegExp(r"[\s#'`]");
final _withoutValue = RegExp(
  r'^\s*(?:#.*|[A-Za-z_][A-Za-z0-9_]*\s*=\s*(?:""\s*)?(?:#.*)?)?$',
);

void main() {
  final example = File('.env.example');
  if (!example.existsSync()) {
    stderr.writeln('.env.example is missing.');
    exitCode = 1;
    return;
  }
  final env = File('.env');
  // In latin1, every file reads, so an error shows no part of a value.
  if (env.existsSync() &&
      !env.readAsLinesSync(encoding: latin1).every(_withoutValue.hasMatch)) {
    stderr.writeln(
      '.env has values, which this script would replace. Delete .env to write '
      'it again.',
    );
    exitCode = 1;
    return;
  }
  final lines = <String>[];
  final empty = <String>[];
  final refused = <String>[];
  for (final line in example.readAsLinesSync()) {
    final key = _key.firstMatch(line)?[1];
    if (key == null) {
      lines.add(line);
      continue;
    }
    final value = Platform.environment[key] ?? '';
    if (value.isEmpty) {
      if (_withoutValue.hasMatch(line)) empty.add(key);
      lines.add(line);
    } else if (_unsafe.hasMatch(value)) {
      refused.add(key);
    } else {
      lines.add('$key=${_quoted.hasMatch(value) ? '"$value"' : value}');
    }
  }
  if (refused.isNotEmpty) {
    stderr.writeln(
      'The values of ${refused.join(', ')} have a double quote, a control '
      'character or a line separator, which .env cannot hold.',
    );
    exitCode = 1;
    return;
  }
  env.writeAsStringSync('${lines.join('\n')}\n');
  stdout.writeln('Wrote .env.');
  if (empty.isNotEmpty) {
    stdout.writeln('No value for: ${empty.join(', ')}.');
  }
}
